# Vivekachudamani

### 1

<vt>Devoted Dedication</vt>  
सर्ववेदान्तसिद्धान्तगोचरं तमगोचरम् ।  
गोविन्दं परमानन्दं सद्गुरुं प्रणतोऽस्म्यहम् ॥१॥

*<vt>Devoted Dedication</vt>  
sarvavedāntasiddhāntagocaraṃ tamagocaram |  
govindaṃ paramānandaṃ sadguruṃ praṇato'smyaham || 1 ||*

I bow to Govinda, the Satguru, whose very nature is Supreme Bliss, who is beyond the reach of speech and mind, and who can be known only through the meaning of all Vedanta.

### 2

<vt>Glory of Spiritual Life</vt>  
जन्तूनां नरजन्म दुर्लभमतः पुंस्त्वं ततो विप्रता तस्माद्वैदिकधर्ममार्गपरता विद्वत्त्वमस्मात्परम् ।  
आत्मानात्मविवेचनं स्वनुभवो ब्रह्मात्मना संस्थितिः मुक्तिर्नो शतजन्मकोटिसुकृतैः पुण्यैर्विना लभ्यते ॥२॥

*<vt>Glory of Spiritual Life</vt>  
jantūnāṃ narajanma durlabhamataḥ puṃstvaṃ tato vipratā tasmādvaidikadharmamārgaparatā vidvattvamasmātparam |  
ātmānātmavivecanaṃ svanubhavo brahmātmanā saṃsthitiḥ muktirno śatajanmakoṭisukṛtaiḥ puṇyairvinā labhyate || 2 ||*

For any being, a human birth is hard to gain, a male body harder still. Brahminhood is rarer than that, and rarer again is commitment to the path of Vedic religion. Beyond this comes scholarship in the scriptures. After it, in order, come discrimination between the Self and the not-Self, Realisation, and remaining in identity with Brahman. Mukti (Liberation) of this kind cannot be reached without the hard-earned merit of a hundred crore of births.

### 3

<vt>Unique Graces in Life</vt>  
दुर्लभं त्रयमेवैतद्देवानुग्रहहेतुकम् ।  
मनुष्यत्वं मुमुक्षुत्वं महापुरुषसंश्रयः ॥ ३ ॥

*<vt>Unique Graces in Life</vt>  
durlabhaṃ trayamevaitaddevānugrahahetukam |  
manuṣyatvaṃ mumukṣutvaṃ mahāpuruṣasaṃśrayaḥ || 3 ||*

Three things are truly rare and come only by the grace of God: a human birth, the longing for Liberation, and the protective care of a perfected sage.

### 4

<vt>Miseries of The Unspiritual Man</vt>  
लब्ध्वा कथंचिन्नरजन्म दुर्लभं तत्रापि पुंस्त्वं श्रुतिपारदर्शनम् ।  
यस्त्वात्ममुक्तौ न यतेत मूढधीः स ह्यात्महा स्वं विनिहन्त्यसद्ग्रहात् ॥ ४ ॥

*<vt>Miseries of The Unspiritual Man</vt>  
labdhvā kathaṃcinnarajanma durlabhaṃ tatrāpi puṃstvaṃ śrutipāradarśanam |  
yastvātmamuktau na yateta mūḍhadhīḥ sa hyātmahā svaṃ vinihantyasadgrahāt || 4 ||*

Suppose someone has somehow gained a human birth, a male body, and mastery of the Vedas as well, and is still foolish enough to make no effort toward self-liberation. That person is committing suicide, destroying himself by clinging to what is unreal.

### 5

इतः को न्वस्ति मूढात्मा यस्तु स्वार्थे प्रमाद्यति ।  
दुर्लभं मानुषं देहं प्राप्य तत्रापि पौरुषम् ॥ ५ ॥

*itaḥ ko nvasti mūḍhātmā yastu svārthe pramādyati |  
durlabhaṃ mānuṣaṃ dehaṃ prāpya tatrāpi pauruṣam || 5 ||*

Who could be a greater fool than one who gains a rare human body, a masculine one at that, and then fails to pursue the true purpose of this life?

### 6

वदन्तु शास्त्राणि यजन्तु देवान् कुर्वन्तु कर्माणि भजन्तु देवताः ।  
आत्मैक्यबोधेन विनापि मुक्तिः न सिध्यति ब्रह्मशतान्तरेऽपि ॥ ६ ॥

*vadantu śāstrāṇi yajantu devān kurvantu karmāṇi bhajantu devatāḥ |  
ātmaikyabodhena vināpi muktiḥ na sidhyati brahmaśatāntare'pi || 6 ||*

People may cite the Scriptures and sacrifice to the gods; they may carry out rituals and worship the deities. Yet without realising one's identity with the Ātman there is no Liberation, not even in a span equal to the lifetimes of a hundred Brahmas combined.

### 7

अमृतत्वस्य नाशास्ति वित्तेनेत्येव हि श्रुतिः ।  
ब्रवीति कर्मणो मुक्तेरहेतुत्वं स्फुटं यतः ॥ ७ ॥

*amṛtatvasya nāśāsti vittenetyeva hi śrutiḥ |  
bravīti karmaṇo mukterahetutvaṃ sphuṭaṃ yataḥ || 7 ||*

The Vedas plainly state that wealth offers no hope of immortality. It follows clearly that works cannot bring about Liberation.

### 8

<vt>Means of Wisdom</vt>  
अतो विमुक्त्यै प्रयतेत्विद्वान् संन्यस्तबाह्यार्थसुखस्पृहः सन् ।  
सन्तं महान्तं समुपेत्य देशिकं तेनोपदिष्टार्थसमाहितात्मा ॥ ८ ॥

*<vt>Means of Wisdom</vt>  
ato vimuktyai prayatetvidvān saṃnyastabāhyārthasukhaspṛhaḥ san |  
santaṃ mahāntaṃ samupetya deśikaṃ tenopadiṣṭārthasamāhitātmā || 8 ||*

The learned person should therefore give up the wish for pleasure from outer objects, properly approach a good and generous teacher, fix the mind on the truth that teacher imparts, and strive with all his strength for Liberation.

### 9

उद्धरेदात्मनात्मानं मग्नं संसारवारिधौ ।  
योगारूढत्वमासाद्य सम्यग्दर्शननिष्ठया ॥ ९ ॥

*uddharedātmanātmānaṃ magnaṃ saṃsāravāridhau |  
yogārūḍhatvamāsādya samyagdarśananiṣṭhayā || 9 ||*

Having reached the Yogārūḍha state, one should rescue oneself, sunk as one is in the sea of birth and death, through devotion to right discrimination.

### 10

संन्यस्य सर्वकर्माणि भवबन्धविमुक्तये ।  
यत्यतां पण्डितैर्धीरैरात्माभ्यास उपस्थितैः ॥ १0 ॥

*saṃnyasya sarvakarmāṇi bhavabandhavimuktaye |  
yatyatāṃ paṇḍitairdhīrairātmābhyāsa upasthitaiḥ || 10 ||*

Once the wise and learned person has begun the practice of realising the Ātman, he should give up all works and work to sever the bonds of birth and death.

### 11

चित्तस्य शुद्धये कर्म न तु वस्तूपलब्धये ।  
वस्तुसिद्धिर्विचारेण न किंचित्कर्मकोटिभिः ॥ ११ ॥

*cittasya śuddhaye karma na tu vastūpalabdhaye |  
vastusiddhirvicāreṇa na kiṃcitkarmakoṭibhiḥ || 11 ||*

Work purifies the mind but does not reveal Reality. Truth is realised through discrimination, and ten million acts will not accomplish it in the least.

### 12

सम्यग्विचारतः सिद्धा रज्जुतत्त्वावधारणा ।  
भ्रान्तोदितमहासर्पभयदुःखविनाशिनी ॥ १२ ॥

*samyagvicārataḥ siddhā rajjutattvāvadhāraṇā |  
bhrāntoditamahāsarpabhayaduḥkhavināśinī || 12 ||*

Sound reasoning produces certainty about what the rope really is, and this ends the great fear and misery caused by the snake conjured up in the deluded mind.

### 13

अर्थस्य निश्चयो दृष्टो विचारेण हितोक्तितः ।  
न स्नानेन न दानेन प्राणायमशतेन वा ॥ १३ ॥

*arthasya niścayo dṛṣṭo vicāreṇa hitoktitaḥ |  
na snānena na dānena prāṇāyamaśatena vā || 13 ||*

Certainty about the Truth is seen to arise from reflecting on the beneficial advice of the wise. It does not come from bathing in sacred waters, from giving gifts, or from hundreds of Prāṇāyāmas (exercises controlling the vital force).

### 14

<vt>The Fit Student</vt>  
अधिकारिणमाशास्ते फलसिद्धिर्विशेषतः ।  
उपाया देशकालाद्याः सन्त्यस्मिन्सहकारिणः ॥ १४ ॥

*<vt>The Fit Student</vt>  
adhikāriṇamāśāste phalasiddhirviśeṣataḥ |  
upāyā deśakālādyāḥ santyasminsahakāriṇaḥ || 14 ||*

Success depends above all on the aspirant being qualified. Time, place, and similar means only assist in this.

### 15

अतो विचारः कर्तव्यो जिज्ञासोरात्मवस्तुनः ।  
समासाद्य दयासिन्धुं गुरुं ब्रह्मविदुत्तमम् ॥ १५ ॥

*ato vicāraḥ kartavyo jijñāsorātmavastunaḥ |  
samāsādya dayāsindhuṃ guruṃ brahmaviduttamam || 15 ||*

So the one who seeks the Reality of the Ātman should properly approach a Guru, the finest among the knowers of Brahman and an ocean of mercy, and then take up reasoning.

### 16

मेधावी पुरुषो विद्वानुहापोहविचक्षणः ।  
अधिकार्यात्मविद्यायामुक्तलक्षणलक्षितः ॥ १६ ॥

*medhāvī puruṣo vidvānuhāpohavicakṣaṇaḥ |  
adhikāryātmavidyāyāmuktalakṣaṇalakṣitaḥ || 16 ||*

A person who is intelligent and learned, skilled at arguing for the Scriptures and at refuting arguments against them, and who has the qualities described above, is a fit recipient of the knowledge of the Ātman.

### 17

विवेकिनो विरक्तस्य शमादिगुणशालिनः ।  
मुमुक्षोरेव हि ब्रह्मजिज्ञासायोग्यता मता ॥ १७ ॥

*vivekino viraktasya śamādiguṇaśālinaḥ |  
mumukṣoreva hi brahmajijñāsāyogyatā matā || 17 ||*

Only someone who distinguishes the Real from the unreal, whose mind has turned away from the unreal, who has calmness and the virtues that go with it, and who longs for Liberation, is regarded as qualified to inquire into Brahman.

### 18

<vt>The Four Qualifications</vt>  
साधनान्यत्र चत्वारि कथितानि मनीषिभिः ।  
येषु सत्स्वेव सन्निष्ठा यदभावे न सिध्यति ॥ १८ ॥

*<vt>The Four Qualifications</vt>  
sādhanānyatra catvāri kathitāni manīṣibhiḥ |  
yeṣu satsveva sanniṣṭhā yadabhāve na sidhyati || 18 ||*

On this subject, sages speak of four means of attainment. When they are present, devotion to Brahman succeeds; when they are absent, it fails.

### 19

आदौ नित्यानित्यवस्तुविवेकः परिगम्यते ।  
इहामुत्रफलभोगविरागस्तदनन्तरम् शमादिषट्कसम्पत्तिर्मुमुक्षुत्वमिति स्फुटम् ॥ १९ ॥

*ādau nityānityavastuvivekaḥ parigamyate |  
ihāmutraphalabhogavirāgastadanantaram śamādiṣaṭkasampattirmumukṣutvamiti sphuṭam || 19 ||*

First on the list is discrimination between the Real and the unreal. Next is aversion to enjoying the fruits of one's actions, both in this life and the next. Then comes the group of six attributes, calmness and the rest. Last, plainly, is the yearning for Liberation.

### 20

ब्रह्म सत्यं जगन्मिथ्येत्येवंरूपो विनिश्चयः ।  
सोऽयं नित्यानित्यवस्तुविवेकः समुदाहृतः ॥ २0 ॥

*brahma satyaṃ jaganmithyetyevaṃrūpo viniścayaḥ |  
so'yaṃ nityānityavastuvivekaḥ samudāhṛtaḥ || 20 ||*

The mind's firm conviction that Brahman is real and the universe unreal is what is called discrimination (Viveka) between the Real and the unreal.

### 21

तद्वैराग्यं जिहासा या दर्शनश्रवणादिभिः ।  
देहादिब्रह्मपर्यन्ते ह्यनित्ये भोगवस्तुनि ॥ २१ ॥

*tadvairāgyaṃ jihāsā yā darśanaśravaṇādibhiḥ |  
dehādibrahmaparyante hyanitye bhogavastuni || 21 ||*

Vairāgya, or renunciation, is the wish to give up every passing enjoyment, from those of an animate body up to those of Brahmāhood, their defects having already been learned through observation, instruction, and the like.

### 22

विरज्य विषयव्राताद्दोषदृष्ट्या मुहुर्मुहुः ।  
स्वलक्ष्ये नियतावस्था मनसः शम उच्यते ॥ २२ ॥

*virajya viṣayavrātāddoṣadṛṣṭyā muhurmuhuḥ |  
svalakṣye niyatāvasthā manasaḥ śama ucyate || 22 ||*

Śama, or calmness, is the mind resting firmly on its Goal (Brahman) after it has pulled away from the many sense objects by repeatedly noticing their defects.

### 23

विषयेभ्यः परावर्त्य स्थापनं स्वस्वगोलके ।  
उभयेषामिन्द्रियाणां स दमः परिकीर्तितः बाह्यानालम्बनं वृत्तेरेषोपरतिरुत्तमा ॥ २३ ॥

*viṣayebhyaḥ parāvartya sthāpanaṃ svasvagolake |  
ubhayeṣāmindriyāṇāṃ sa damaḥ parikīrtitaḥ bāhyānālambanaṃ vṛttereṣoparatiruttamā || 23 ||*

Dama, or self-control, is turning both kinds of sense organs away from sense objects and settling them in their own centres. The best Uparati, or self-withdrawal, is when the mind's activity no longer operates through external objects.

### 24

सहनं सर्वदुःखानामप्रतीकारपूर्वकम् ।  
चिन्ताविलापरहितं सा तितिक्षा निगद्यते ॥ २४ ॥

*sahanaṃ sarvaduḥkhānāmapratīkārapūrvakam |  
cintāvilāparahitaṃ sā titikṣā nigadyate || 24 ||*

Titikṣā, or forbearance, is enduring all afflictions without seeking to remedy them, while staying free of anxiety or lament about them.

### 25

शास्त्रस्य गुरुवाक्यस्य सत्यबुद्ध्यवधारणम् ।  
सा श्रद्धा कथिता सद्भिर्यया वस्तूपलभ्यते ॥ २५ ॥

*śāstrasya guruvākyasya satyabuddhyavadhāraṇam |  
sā śraddhā kathitā sadbhiryayā vastūpalabhyate || 25 ||*

Sages use the word Śraddhā, or faith, for firmly judging to be true what the Scriptures and the Guru teach. Through it Reality is perceived.

### 26

सर्वदा स्थापनं बुद्धेः शुद्धे ब्रह्मणि सर्वदा ।  
तत्समाधानमित्युक्तं न तु चित्तस्य लालनम् ॥ २६ ॥

*sarvadā sthāpanaṃ buddheḥ śuddhe brahmaṇi sarvadā |  
tatsamādhānamityuktaṃ na tu cittasya lālanam || 26 ||*

Samādhāna, or self-settledness, is the constant focusing of the intellect (the affirming faculty) on the ever-pure Brahman. It is not merely letting thought indulge its curiosity.

### 27

अहंकारादिदेहान्तान् बन्धानज्ञानकल्पितान् ।  
स्वस्वरूपावबोधेन मोक्तुमिच्छा मुमुक्षुता ॥ २७ ॥

*ahaṃkārādidehāntān bandhānajñānakalpitān |  
svasvarūpāvabodhena moktumicchā mumukṣutā || 27 ||*

Mumukṣutā, or yearning for Freedom, is the wish to be released, by realising one's true nature, from every bondage imposed by Ignorance, from egoism down to the body.

### 28

मन्दमध्यमरूपापि वैराग्येण शमादिना ।  
प्रसादेन गुरोः सेयं प्रवृद्धा सूयते फलम् ॥ २८ ॥

*mandamadhyamarūpāpi vairāgyeṇa śamādinā |  
prasādena guroḥ seyaṃ pravṛddhā sūyate phalam || 28 ||*

Even when dull or only moderate, this yearning for Freedom can bear fruit by the Guru's grace, as it is developed through Vairāgya (renunciation), Śama (calmness), and the rest.

### 29

वैराग्यं च मुमुक्षुत्वं तीव्रं यस्य तु विद्यते ।  
तस्मिन्नेवार्थवन्तः स्युः फलवन्तः शमादयः ॥ २९ ॥

*vairāgyaṃ ca mumukṣutvaṃ tīvraṃ yasya tu vidyate |  
tasminnevārthavantaḥ syuḥ phalavantaḥ śamādayaḥ || 29 ||*

It is only in the person whose renunciation and yearning for Freedom are intense that calmness and the other practices have real meaning and bear fruit.

### 30

एतयोर्मन्दता यत्र विरक्तत्वमुमुक्षयोः ।  
मरौ सलीलवत्तत्र शमादेर्भानमात्रता ॥ ३0 ॥

*etayormandatā yatra viraktatvamumukṣayoḥ |  
marau salīlavattatra śamāderbhānamātratā || 30 ||*

But where renunciation and yearning for Freedom are dull, calmness and the other practices are mere appearances, like water seen in a desert.

### 31

<vt>Bhakti - Firm and Deep</vt>  
मोक्षकारणसामग्र्यां भक्तिरेव गरीयसी ।  
स्वस्वरूपानुसन्धानं भक्तिरित्यभिधीयते ॥ ३१ ॥

*<vt>Bhakti - Firm and Deep</vt>  
mokṣakāraṇasāmagryāṃ bhaktireva garīyasī |  
svasvarūpānusandhānaṃ bhaktirityabhidhīyate || 31 ||*

Of all that helps toward Liberation, devotion (Bhakti) stands highest. Seeking one's real nature is what is called devotion.

### 32

<vt>Courtesy of Approach and Questioning</vt>  
स्वात्मतत्त्वानुसन्धानं भक्तिरित्यपरे जगुः ।  
उक्तसाधनसंपन्नस्तत्त्वजिज्ञासुरात्मनः उपसीदेद्गुरुं प्राज्ञ्यं यस्माद्बन्धविमोक्षणम् ॥ ३२ ॥

*<vt>Courtesy of Approach and Questioning</vt>  
svātmatattvānusandhānaṃ bhaktirityapare jaguḥ |  
uktasādhanasaṃpannastattvajijñāsurātmanaḥ upasīdedguruṃ prājñyaṃ yasmādbandhavimokṣaṇam || 32 ||*

Others hold that inquiry into the truth of one's own self is devotion. One who inquires into the truth of the Ātman and has the means of attainment described above should go to a wise teacher who grants release from bondage.

### 33

श्रोत्रियोऽवृजिनोऽकामहतो यो ब्रह्मवित्तमः ।  
ब्रह्मण्युपरतः शान्तो निरिन्धन इवानलः अहेतुकदयासिन्धुर्बन्धुरानमतां सताम् ॥ ३३ ॥

*śrotriyo'vṛjino'kāmahato yo brahmavittamaḥ |  
brahmaṇyuparataḥ śānto nirindhana ivānalaḥ ahetukadayāsindhurbandhurānamatāṃ satām || 33 ||*

Such a teacher is versed in the Vedas, sinless, untouched by desire, and a supreme knower of Brahman. He has withdrawn into Brahman and is calm, like a fire that has burned up its fuel. He is a limitless reservoir of causeless mercy and a friend to all good people who bow before him.

### 34

तमाराध्य गुरुं भक्त्या प्रह्वप्रश्रयसेवनैः ।  
प्रसन्नं तमनुप्राप्य पृच्छेज्ज्ञातव्यमात्मनः ॥ ३४ ॥

*tamārādhya guruṃ bhaktyā prahvapraśrayasevanaiḥ |  
prasannaṃ tamanuprāpya pṛcchejjñātavyamātmanaḥ || 34 ||*

Worshipping that Guru with devotion and approaching him, once he has been pleased by prostration, humility, and service, the seeker should ask him what he needs to know:

### 35

स्वामिन्नमस्ते नतलोकबन्धो कारुण्यसिन्धो पतितं भवाब्धौ ।  
मामुद्धरात्मीयकटाक्षदृष्ट्या ऋज्व्यातिकारुण्यसुधाभिवृष्ट्या ॥ ३५ ॥

*svāminnamaste natalokabandho kāruṇyasindho patitaṃ bhavābdhau |  
māmuddharātmīyakaṭākṣadṛṣṭyā ṛjvyātikāruṇyasudhābhivṛṣṭyā || 35 ||*

O Master, friend of those who bow to you, ocean of mercy, I bow to you. I have fallen into this sea of birth and death. Rescue me with a direct glance of your eye, which pours out supreme, nectar-like grace.

### 36

दुर्वारसंसारदवाग्नितप्तं दोधूयमानं दुरदृष्टवातैः ।  
भीतं प्रपन्नं परिपाहि मृत्योः शरण्यमन्यद्यदहं न जाने ॥ ३६ ॥

*durvārasaṃsāradavāgnitaptaṃ dodhūyamānaṃ duradṛṣṭavātaiḥ |  
bhītaṃ prapannaṃ paripāhi mṛtyoḥ śaraṇyamanyadyadahaṃ na jāne || 36 ||*

Save me from death. I am scorched by the unquenchable fire of this world-forest and violently shaken by the winds of an adverse fate. Terrified, I seek refuge in you, for I know of no one else who can shelter me.

### 37

शान्ता महान्तो निवसन्ति सन्तो वसन्तवल्लोकहितं चरन्तः ।  
तीर्णाः स्वयं भीमभवार्णवं जनान् अहेतुनान् यानपि तारयन्तः ॥ ३७ ॥

*śāntā mahānto nivasanti santo vasantavallokahitaṃ carantaḥ |  
tīrṇāḥ svayaṃ bhīmabhavārṇavaṃ janān ahetunān yānapi tārayantaḥ || 37 ||*

There are good souls, calm and generous, who benefit others as the spring season does. Having crossed this terrible ocean of birth and death themselves, they help others cross it too, with no motive at all.

### 38

अयं स्वभावः स्वत एव यत्पर श्रमापनोदप्रवणं महात्मनाम् ।  
सुधांशुरेष स्वयमर्ककर्कश प्रभाभितप्तामवति क्षितिं किल ॥ ३८ ॥

*ayaṃ svabhāvaḥ svata eva yatpara śramāpanodapravaṇaṃ mahātmanām |  
sudhāṃśureṣa svayamarkakarkaśa prabhābhitaptāmavati kṣitiṃ kila || 38 ||*

It is the very nature of the generous to set about relieving the troubles of others of their own accord. Take the moon: as everyone knows, it freely rescues the earth when it is parched by the sun's blazing rays.

### 39

ब्रह्मानन्दरसानुभूतिकलितैः पूर्तैः सुशीतैर्युतैः युष्मद्वाक्कलशोज्झितैः श्रुतिसुखैर्वाक्यामृतैः सेचय ।  
संतप्तं भवतापदावदहनज्वालाभिरेनं प्रभो धन्यास्ते भवदीक्षणक्षणगतेः पात्रीकृताः स्वीकृताः ॥ ३९ ॥

*brahmānandarasānubhūtikalitaiḥ pūrtaiḥ suśītairyutaiḥ yuṣmadvākkalaśojjhitaiḥ śrutisukhairvākyāmṛtaiḥ secaya |  
saṃtaptaṃ bhavatāpadāvadahanajvālābhirenaṃ prabho dhanyāste bhavadīkṣaṇakṣaṇagateḥ pātrīkṛtāḥ svīkṛtāḥ || 39 ||*

O Lord, I am tormented by worldly afflictions as if by the tongues of a forest fire. Sprinkle me with your nectar-like speech, sweetened by tasting the elixir of Brahman's bliss, pure, deeply cooling, flowing from your lips as from a pitcher, and delightful to the ear. Blessed are those on whom even a passing glance of yours falls, accepting them as your own.

### 40

कथं तरेयं भवसिन्धुमेतं का वा गतिर्मे कतमोऽस्त्युपायः ।  
जाने न किञ्ज्चित्कृपयाव मां प्रभो संसारदुःखक्षतिमातनुष्व ॥ ४0 ॥

*kathaṃ tareyaṃ bhavasindhumetaṃ kā vā gatirme katamo'styupāyaḥ |  
jāne na kiñjcitkṛpayāva māṃ prabho saṃsāraduḥkhakṣatimātanuṣva || 40 ||*

How to cross this ocean of phenomenal existence, what will become of me, and which means I should take up: of these I know nothing. Please be gracious enough to save me, O Lord, and explain in full how to end the misery of this relative existence.

### 41

<vt>Loving Advice of the Guru</vt>  
तथा वदन्तं शरणागतं स्वं संसारदावानलतापतप्तम् ।  
निरीक्ष्य कारुण्यरसार्द्रदृष्ट्या दद्यादभीतिं सहसा महात्मा ॥ ४१ ॥

*<vt>Loving Advice of the Guru</vt>  
tathā vadantaṃ śaraṇāgataṃ svaṃ saṃsāradāvānalatāpataptam |  
nirīkṣya kāruṇyarasārdradṛṣṭyā dadyādabhītiṃ sahasā mahātmā || 41 ||*

As the seeker says this, tormented by the afflictions of the world, which is like a forest on fire, and asking for protection, the saint looks at him with eyes softened by pity and at once tells him to give up all fear.

### 42

विद्वान् स तस्मा उपसत्तिमीयुषे मुमुक्षवे साधु यथोक्तकारिणे ।  
प्रशान्तचित्ताय शमान्विताय तत्त्वोपदेशं कृपयैव कुर्यात् ॥ ४२ ॥

*vidvān sa tasmā upasattimīyuṣe mumukṣave sādhu yathoktakāriṇe |  
praśāntacittāya śamānvitāya tattvopadeśaṃ kṛpayaiva kuryāt || 42 ||*

To the one who has come for protection, who thirsts for Liberation, who properly follows the injunctions of the Scriptures, and whose mind is serene and calm, the sage begins to teach the truth out of pure grace.

### 43

मा भैष्ट विद्वंस्तव नास्त्यपायः संसारसिन्धोस्तरणेऽस्त्युपायः ।  
येनैव याता यतयोऽस्य पारं तमेव मार्गं तव निर्दिशामि ॥ ४३ ॥

*mā bhaiṣṭa vidvaṃstava nāstyapāyaḥ saṃsārasindhostaraṇe'styupāyaḥ |  
yenaiva yātā yatayo'sya pāraṃ tameva mārgaṃ tava nirdiśāmi || 43 ||*

Have no fear, learned one. There is no death for you. There is a way to cross this sea of relative existence, and I will teach you the very path by which sages have passed beyond it.

### 44

अस्त्युपायो महान् कश्चित्संसारभयनाशनः ।  
तेन तीर्त्वा भवाम्भोधिं परमानन्दमाप्स्यसि ॥ ४४ ॥

*astyupāyo mahān kaścitsaṃsārabhayanāśanaḥ |  
tena tīrtvā bhavāmbhodhiṃ paramānandamāpsyasi || 44 ||*

There is a sovereign means that ends the fear of relative existence. Through it you will cross the sea of Samsāra and reach supreme bliss.

### 45

वेदान्तार्थविचारेण जायते ज्ञानमुत्तमम् ।  
तेनात्यन्तिकसंसारदुःखनाशो भवत्यनु ॥ ४५ ॥

*vedāntārthavicāreṇa jāyate jñānamuttamam |  
tenātyantikasaṃsāraduḥkhanāśo bhavatyanu || 45 ||*

Reasoning on the meaning of Vedanta produces effective knowledge, and this is followed at once by the complete destruction of the misery born of relative existence.

### 46

श्रद्धाभक्तिध्यानयोगाम्मुमुक्षोः मुक्तेर्हेतून्वक्ति साक्षाच्छ्रुतेर्गीः ।  
यो वा एतेष्वेव तिष्ठत्यमुष्य मोक्षोऽविद्याकल्पिताद्देहबन्धात् ॥ ४६ ॥

*śraddhābhaktidhyānayogāmmumukṣoḥ mukterhetūnvakti sākṣācchrutergīḥ |  
yo vā eteṣveva tiṣṭhatyamuṣya mokṣo'vidyākalpitāddehabandhāt || 46 ||*

Faith (Śraddhā), devotion, and the Yoga of meditation are named by the Śruti as the direct means of Liberation for a seeker. Whoever remains in these is released from the bondage of the body, which Ignorance has conjured up.

### 47

अज्ञानयोगात्परमात्मनस्तव ह्यनात्मबन्धस्तत एव संसृतिः ।  
तयोर्विवेकोदितबोधवन्हिः अज्ञानकार्यं प्रदहेत्समूलम् ॥ ४७ ॥

*ajñānayogātparamātmanastava hyanātmabandhastata eva saṃsṛtiḥ |  
tayorvivekoditabodhavanhiḥ ajñānakāryaṃ pradahetsamūlam || 47 ||*

It is indeed by contact with Ignorance that you, the Supreme Self, find yourself bound by the non-Self, and from this alone comes the round of births and deaths. The fire of knowledge, lit by discriminating between the two, burns up the effects of Ignorance along with their root.

### 48

<vt>Questions of the Disciple</vt>  
शिष्य उवाच कृपया श्रूयतां स्वामिन् प्रश्नोऽयं क्रियते मया ।  
यदुत्तरमहं श्रुत्वा कृतार्थः स्यां भवन्मुखात् ॥ ४८ ॥

*<vt>Questions of the Disciple</vt>  
śiṣya uvāca kṛpayā śrūyatāṃ svāmin praśno'yaṃ kriyate mayā |  
yaduttaramahaṃ śrutvā kṛtārthaḥ syāṃ bhavanmukhāt || 48 ||*

The Disciple said: Please be gracious enough to listen, O Master, to the question I am putting to you. I will be glad to hear the answer from your own lips.

### 49

को नाम बन्धः कथमेष आगतः कथं प्रतिष्ठास्य कथं विमोक्षः ।  
कोऽसावनात्मा परमः क आत्मा तयोर्विवेकः कथमेतदुच्यताम् ॥ ४९ ॥

*ko nāma bandhaḥ kathameṣa āgataḥ kathaṃ pratiṣṭhāsya kathaṃ vimokṣaḥ |  
ko'sāvanātmā paramaḥ ka ātmā tayorvivekaḥ kathametaducyatām || 49 ||*

What exactly is bondage? How did it come upon the Self? How does it continue? How does one become free of it? What is this non-Self? Who is the Supreme Self? And how can one tell them apart? Please tell me about all of this.

### 50

<vt>Intelligent Disciple - Appreciated</vt>  
श्रीगुरुवाच धन्योऽसि कृतकृत्योऽसि पावित ते कुलं त्वया ।  
यदविद्याबन्धमुक्त्या ब्रह्मीभवितुमिच्छसि ॥ ५0 ॥

*<vt>Intelligent Disciple - Appreciated</vt>  
śrīguruvāca dhanyo'si kṛtakṛtyo'si pāvita te kulaṃ tvayā |  
yadavidyābandhamuktyā brahmībhavitumicchasi || 50 ||*

The Guru replied: You are blessed! You have achieved the purpose of your life and made your family holy, since you wish to reach Brahmanhood by getting free of the bondage of Ignorance!

### 51

<vt>Glory of Self-Effort</vt>  
ऋणमोचनकर्तारः पितुः सन्ति सुतादयः ।  
बन्धमोचनकर्ता तु स्वस्मादन्यो न कश्चन ॥ ५१ ॥

*<vt>Glory of Self-Effort</vt>  
ṛṇamocanakartāraḥ pituḥ santi sutādayaḥ |  
bandhamocanakartā tu svasmādanyo na kaścana || 51 ||*

A father has sons and others to clear him of his debts, but no one except himself can remove his bondage.

### 52

मस्तकन्यस्तभारादेर्दुःखमन्यैर्निवार्यते ।  
क्षुधादिकृतदुःखं तु विना स्वेन न केनचित् ॥ ५२ ॥

*mastakanyastabhārāderduḥkhamanyairnivāryate |  
kṣudhādikṛtaduḥkhaṃ tu vinā svena na kenacit || 52 ||*

Others can relieve a burden such as a load carried on the head, but only one's own self can stop the pain of hunger and the like.

### 53

पथ्यमौषधसेवा च क्रियते येन रोगिणा ।  
आरोग्यसिद्धिर्दृष्टास्य नान्यानुष्ठितकर्मणा ॥ ५३ ॥

*pathyamauṣadhasevā ca kriyate yena rogiṇā |  
ārogyasiddhirdṛṣṭāsya nānyānuṣṭhitakarmaṇā || 53 ||*

It is only the patient who takes the proper diet and medicine who is seen to recover fully; others doing the work on his behalf will not cure him.

### 54

वस्तुस्वरूपं स्फुटबोधचक्षुषा स्वेनैव वेद्यं न तु पण्डितेन ।  
चन्द्रस्वरूपं निजचक्षुषैव ज्ञातव्यमन्यैरवगम्यते किम् ॥ ५४ ॥

*vastusvarūpaṃ sphuṭabodhacakṣuṣā svenaiva vedyaṃ na tu paṇḍitena |  
candrasvarūpaṃ nijacakṣuṣaiva jñātavyamanyairavagamyate kim || 54 ||*

The true nature of things must be known personally, through the eye of clear illumination, and not through a sage. What the moon is exactly must be seen with one's own eyes. Can others know it for you?

### 55

अविद्याकामकर्मादिपाशबन्धं विमोचितुम् ।  
कः शक्नुयाद्विनात्मानं कल्पकोटिशतैरपि ॥ ५५ ॥

*avidyākāmakarmādipāśabandhaṃ vimocitum |  
kaḥ śaknuyādvinātmānaṃ kalpakoṭiśatairapi || 55 ||*

Who but oneself can be rid of the bondage forged by the fetters of Ignorance, desire, action, and the like, even over a hundred crore of cycles?

### 56

<vt>Knowledge of the Self-Its Beauty</vt>  
न योगेन न सांख्येन कर्मणा नो न विद्यया ।  
ब्रह्मात्मैकत्वबोधेन मोक्षः सिध्यति नान्यथा ॥ ५६ ॥

*<vt>Knowledge of the Self-Its Beauty</vt>  
na yogena na sāṃkhyena karmaṇā no na vidyayā |  
brahmātmaikatvabodhena mokṣaḥ sidhyati nānyathā || 56 ||*

Liberation is not possible through Yoga, through Sānkhya, through work, or through learning. It comes through realising one's identity with Brahman, and by no other means.

### 57

वीणाया रूपसौन्दर्यं तन्त्रीवादनसौष्ठवम् ।  
प्रजारञ्ज्जनमात्रं तन्न साम्राज्याय कल्पते ॥ ५७ ॥

*vīṇāyā rūpasaundaryaṃ tantrīvādanasauṣṭhavam |  
prajārañjjanamātraṃ tanna sāmrājyāya kalpate || 57 ||*

The beauty of a guitar's form and skill in playing its strings serve only to please a few people. They are not enough to confer sovereignty.

### 58

वाग्वैखरी शब्दझरी शास्त्रव्याख्यानकौशलम् ।  
वैदुष्यं विदुषां तद्वद्भुक्तये न तु मुक्तये ॥ ५८ ॥

*vāgvaikharī śabdajharī śāstravyākhyānakauśalam |  
vaiduṣyaṃ viduṣāṃ tadvadbhuktaye na tu muktaye || 58 ||*

Loud speech pouring out in a torrent of words, skill in expounding the Scriptures, and erudition as well bring the scholar a little personal enjoyment, but they do nothing for Liberation.

### 59

अविज्ञाते परे तत्त्वे शास्त्राधीतिस्तु निष्फला ।  
विज्ञातेऽपि परे तत्त्वे शास्त्राधीतिस्तु निष्फला ॥ ५९ ॥

*avijñāte pare tattve śāstrādhītistu niṣphalā |  
vijñāte'pi pare tattve śāstrādhītistu niṣphalā || 59 ||*

Studying the Scriptures is useless as long as the highest Truth is unknown, and it is just as useless once the highest Truth has been known.

### 60

शब्दजालं महारण्यं चित्तभ्रमणकारणम् ।  
अतः प्रयत्नाज्ज्ञातव्यं तत्त्वज्ञैस्तत्त्वमात्मनः ॥ ६0 ॥

*śabdajālaṃ mahāraṇyaṃ cittabhramaṇakāraṇam |  
ataḥ prayatnājjñātavyaṃ tattvajñaistattvamātmanaḥ || 60 ||*

The Scriptures, with their many words, are a dense forest that only sets the mind wandering. Wise people should therefore apply themselves earnestly to knowing the true nature of the Self.

### 61

अज्ञानसर्पदष्टस्य ब्रह्मज्ञानौषधं विना ।  
किमु वेदैश्च शास्त्रैश्च किमु मन्त्रैः किमौषधैः ॥ ६१ ॥

*ajñānasarpadaṣṭasya brahmajñānauṣadhaṃ vinā |  
kimu vedaiśca śāstraiśca kimu mantraiḥ kimauṣadhaiḥ || 61 ||*

Knowledge of Brahman is the one cure for someone bitten by the serpent of Ignorance. What use to that person are the Vedas and Scriptures, Mantras and medicines?

### 62

<vt>Direct Experience - Liberation</vt>  
न गच्छति विना पानं व्याधिरौषधशब्दतः ।  
विनापरोक्षानुभवं ब्रह्मशब्दैर्न मुच्यते ॥ ६२ ॥

*<vt>Direct Experience - Liberation</vt>  
na gacchati vinā pānaṃ vyādhirauṣadhaśabdataḥ |  
vināparokṣānubhavaṃ brahmaśabdairna mucyate || 62 ||*

Saying the name of a medicine without swallowing it will not rid anyone of a disease. In the same way, no one is liberated just by pronouncing the word Brahman, without direct realization.

### 63

अकृत्वा दृश्यविलयमज्ञात्वा तत्त्वमात्मनः ।  
ब्रह्मशब्दैः कुतो मुक्तिरुक्तिमात्रफलैर्नृणाम् ॥ ६३ ॥

*akṛtvā dṛśyavilayamajñātvā tattvamātmanaḥ |  
brahmaśabdaiḥ kuto muktiruktimātraphalairnṛṇām || 63 ||*

If the objective universe has not been made to vanish and the truth of the Self has not been known, how could saying the word Brahman bring Liberation? It would be nothing but an exercise of the voice.

### 64

अकृत्वा शत्रुसंहारमगत्वाखिलभूश्रियम् ।  
राजाहमिति शब्दान्नो राजा भवितुमर्हति ॥ ६४ ॥

*akṛtvā śatrusaṃhāramagatvākhilabhūśriyam |  
rājāhamiti śabdānno rājā bhavitumarhati || 64 ||*

Nobody becomes an emperor by announcing, 'I am an emperor,' without first slaying his enemies and taking possession of the splendor of the whole surrounding region.

### 65

आप्तोक्तिं खननं तथोपरिशिलाद्युत्कर्षणं स्वीकृतिं निक्षेपः समपेक्षते नहि बहिः शब्दैस्तु निर्गच्छति ।  
तद्वद्ब्रह्मविदोपदेशमननध्यानादिभिर्लभ्यते मायाकार्यतिरोहितं स्वममलं तत्त्वं न दुर्युक्तिभिः ॥ ६५ ॥

*āptoktiṃ khananaṃ tathopariśilādyutkarṣaṇaṃ svīkṛtiṃ nikṣepaḥ samapekṣate nahi bahiḥ śabdaistu nirgacchati |  
tadvadbrahmavidopadeśamananadhyānādibhirlabhyate māyākāryatirohitaṃ svamamalaṃ tattvaṃ na duryuktibhiḥ || 65 ||*

A treasure buried underground never comes up when called by name. Getting it requires reliable directions, digging, clearing away the stones and whatever else lies on top, and finally taking hold of it. Likewise, the clear Truth of the Self, hidden by Maya and its effects, is reached through the teaching of a knower of Brahman, followed by reflection, meditation, and the like, and not through twisted arguments.

### 66

तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन भवबन्धविमुक्तये ।  
स्वैरेव यत्नः कर्तव्यो रोगादाविव पण्डितैः ॥ ६६ ॥

*tasmātsarvaprayatnena bhavabandhavimuktaye |  
svaireva yatnaḥ kartavyo rogādāviva paṇḍitaiḥ || 66 ||*

So the wise should work personally, with every means at their disposal, to free themselves from the bondage of repeated birth and death, just as they would with a disease and similar troubles.

### 67

<vt>Discussion on Questions Raised</vt>  
यस्त्वयाद्य कृतः प्रश्नो वरीयाञ्ज्छास्त्रविन्मतः ।  
सूत्रप्रायो निगूढार्थो ज्ञातव्यश्च मुमुक्षुभिः ॥ ६७ ॥

*<vt>Discussion on Questions Raised</vt>  
yastvayādya kṛtaḥ praśno varīyāñjchāstravinmataḥ |  
sūtraprāyo nigūḍhārtho jñātavyaśca mumukṣubhiḥ || 67 ||*

The question you have asked today is excellent. Those who know the Śastras approve of it; it is concise, rich in meaning, and worth knowing for all who seek Liberation.

### 68

शृणुष्वावहितो विद्वन्यन्मया समुदीर्यते ।  
तदेतच्छ्रवणात्सद्यो भवबन्धाद्विमोक्ष्यसे ॥ ६८ ॥

*śṛṇuṣvāvahito vidvanyanmayā samudīryate |  
tadetacchravaṇātsadyo bhavabandhādvimokṣyase || 68 ||*

Learned one, pay close attention to what I am about to say. Hearing it, you will at once be released from the bondage of Samsāra.

### 69

मोक्षस्य हेतुः प्रथमो निगद्यते वैराग्यमत्यन्तमनित्यवस्तुषु ।  
ततः शमश्चापि दमस्तितिक्षा न्यासः प्रसक्ताखिलकर्मणां भृशम् ॥ ६९ ॥

*mokṣasya hetuḥ prathamo nigadyate vairāgyamatyantamanityavastuṣu |  
tataḥ śamaścāpi damastitikṣā nyāsaḥ prasaktākhilakarmaṇāṃ bhṛśam || 69 ||*

The first step toward Liberation is a deep aversion to everything perishable. After it come calmness, self-control, forbearance, and the complete giving up of all work prescribed in the Scriptures.

### 70

ततः श्रुतिस्तन्मननं सतत्त्व ध्यानं चिरं नित्यनिरन्तरं मुनेः ।  
ततोऽविकल्पं परमेत्य विद्वान् इहैव निर्वाणसुखं समृच्छति ॥ ७0 ॥

*tataḥ śrutistanmananaṃ satattva dhyānaṃ ciraṃ nityanirantaraṃ muneḥ |  
tato'vikalpaṃ parametya vidvān ihaiva nirvāṇasukhaṃ samṛcchati || 70 ||*

Next, for the Muni, come hearing, reflection on what was heard, and long, constant, uninterrupted meditation on the Truth. The learned seeker then reaches the supreme Nirvikalpa state and experiences the bliss of Nirvana in this very life.

### 71

यद्बोद्धव्यं तवेदानीमात्मानात्मविवेचनम् ।  
तदुच्यते मया सम्यक्श्रुत्वात्मन्यवधारय ॥ ७१ ॥

*yadboddhavyaṃ tavedānīmātmānātmavivecanam |  
taducyate mayā samyakśrutvātmanyavadhāraya || 71 ||*

I will now explain in full what you need to know: the discrimination between the Self and the non-Self. Listen, and settle it in your mind.

### 72

<vt>Gross Body</vt>  
मज्जास्थिमेदःपलरक्तचर्म त्वगाह्वयैर्धातुभिरेभिरन्वितम् ।  
पादोरुवक्षोभुजपृष्ठमस्तकैः अङ्गैरुपाङ्गैरुपयुक्तमेतत् ॥ ७२ ॥

*<vt>Gross Body</vt>  
majjāsthimedaḥpalaraktacarma tvagāhvayairdhātubhirebhiranvitam |  
pādoruvakṣobhujapṛṣṭhamastakaiḥ aṅgairupāṅgairupayuktametat || 72 ||*

Made of seven components, namely marrow, bones, fat, flesh, blood, skin, and cuticle, and having these limbs and their parts: legs, thighs, chest, arms, back, and head:

### 73

अहंममेतिप्रथितं शरीरं मोहास्पदं स्थूलमितीर्यते बुधैः ।  
नभोनभस्वद्दहनाम्बुभूमयः सूक्ष्माणि भूतानि भवन्ति तानि ॥ ७३ ॥

*ahaṃmametiprathitaṃ śarīraṃ mohāspadaṃ sthūlamitīryate budhaiḥ |  
nabhonabhasvaddahanāmbubhūmayaḥ sūkṣmāṇi bhūtāni bhavanti tāni || 73 ||*

this body, known as the home of the delusion of 'I and mine,' is what the sages call the gross body. Sky, air, fire, water, and earth are the subtle elements. They:

### 74

परस्परांशैर्मिलितानि भूत्वा स्थूलानि च स्थूलशरीरहेतवः ।  
मात्रास्तदीया विषया भवन्ति शब्दादयः पञ्च सुखाय भोक्तुः ॥ ७४ ॥

*parasparāṃśairmilitāni bhūtvā sthūlāni ca sthūlaśarīrahetavaḥ |  
mātrāstadīyā viṣayā bhavanti śabdādayaḥ pañca sukhāya bhoktuḥ || 74 ||*

by combining with portions of one another and turning gross, make up the gross body. Their subtle essences make up the sense objects, the group of five beginning with sound, which serve the happiness of the experiencer, the individual soul.

### 75

य एषु मूढा विषयेषु बद्धा रागोरुपाशेन सुदुर्दमेन ।  
आयान्ति निर्यान्त्यध ऊर्ध्वमुच्चैः स्वकर्मदूतेन जवेन नीताः ॥ ७५ ॥

*ya eṣu mūḍhā viṣayeṣu baddhā rāgorupāśena sudurdamena |  
āyānti niryāntyadha ūrdhvamuccaiḥ svakarmadūtena javena nītāḥ || 75 ||*

Fools bound to these sense objects by the thick rope of attachment, which is so hard to cut, come and go, rising and falling, swept along at full speed by the mighty messenger of their past actions.

### 76

<vt>Sense Objects a Trap - Man Bound</vt>  
शब्दादिभिः पञ्चभिरेव पञ्च पञ्चत्वमापुः स्वगुणेन बद्धाः ।  
कुरङ्गमातङ्गपतङ्गमीन भृङ्गा नरः पञ्चभिरञ्चितः किम् ॥ ७६ ॥

*<vt>Sense Objects a Trap - Man Bound</vt>  
śabdādibhiḥ pañcabhireva pañca pañcatvamāpuḥ svaguṇena baddhāḥ |  
kuraṅgamātaṅgapataṅgamīna bhṛṅgā naraḥ pañcabhirañcitaḥ kim || 76 ||*

The deer, the elephant, the moth, the fish, and the black bee have each perished, bound by their own attachment to just one of the five senses, sound and the rest. What then awaits a human being attached to all five?

### 77

दोषेण तीव्रो विषयः कृष्णसर्पविषादपि ।  
विषं निहन्ति भोक्तारं द्रष्टारं चक्षुषाप्ययम् ॥ ७७ ॥

*doṣeṇa tīvro viṣayaḥ kṛṣṇasarpaviṣādapi |  
viṣaṃ nihanti bhoktāraṃ draṣṭāraṃ cakṣuṣāpyayam || 77 ||*

Sense objects are even deadlier than cobra venom. Poison kills the one who swallows it, but sense objects kill a person who merely looks at them with the eyes.

### 78

विषयाशामहापाशाद्यो विमुक्तः सुदुस्त्यजात् ।  
स एव कल्पते मुक्त्यै नान्यः षट्शास्त्रवेद्यपि ॥ ७८ ॥

*viṣayāśāmahāpāśādyo vimuktaḥ sudustyajāt |  
sa eva kalpate muktyai nānyaḥ ṣaṭśāstravedyapi || 78 ||*

Only the person free of the terrible shackles of craving for sense objects, so very hard to shake off, is fit for Liberation. No one else is, even someone who has mastered all six Śastras.

### 79

आपातवैराग्यवतो मुमुक्षून् भवाब्धिपारं प्रतियातुमुद्यतान् ।  
आशाग्रहो मज्जयतेऽन्तराले निगृह्य कण्ठे विनिवर्त्य वेगात् ॥ ७९ ॥

*āpātavairāgyavato mumukṣūn bhavābdhipāraṃ pratiyātumudyatān |  
āśāgraho majjayate'ntarāle nigṛhya kaṇṭhe vinivartya vegāt || 79 ||*

Some seekers of liberation have only the appearance of dispassion (Vairāgya) as they try to cross the ocean of Samsāra (relative existence). The shark of craving seizes them by the throat, drags them violently away, and drowns them midway.

### 80

विषयाख्यग्रहो येन सुविरक्त्यसिना हतः ।  
स गच्छति भवाम्भोधेः पारं प्रत्यूहवर्जितः ॥ ८0 ॥

*viṣayākhyagraho yena suviraktyasinā hataḥ |  
sa gacchati bhavāmbhodheḥ pāraṃ pratyūhavarjitaḥ || 80 ||*

Whoever has slain the shark called sense object with the sword of mature dispassion crosses the ocean of Samsara with nothing to block the way.

### 81

विषमविषयमार्गैर्गच्छतोऽनच्छबुद्धेः प्रतिपदमभियातो मृत्युरप्येष विद्धि ।  
हितसुजनगुरुक्त्या गच्छतः स्वस्य युक्त्या प्रभवति फलसिद्धिः सत्यमित्येव विद्धि ॥ ८१ ॥

*viṣamaviṣayamārgairgacchato'nacchabuddheḥ pratipadamabhiyāto mṛtyurapyeṣa viddhi |  
hitasujanaguruktyā gacchataḥ svasya yuktyā prabhavati phalasiddhiḥ satyamityeva viddhi || 81 ||*

Understand that death soon catches the foolish person who travels the dreadful roads of sense pleasure. But one who follows the guidance of a benevolent and worthy Guru, along with his own reasoning, reaches his goal. Know this to be true.

### 82

मोक्षस्य कांक्षा यदि वै तवास्ति त्यजातिदूराद्विषयान्विषं यथा ।  
पीयूषवत्तोषदयाक्षमार्जव प्रशान्तिदान्तीर्भज नित्यमादरात् ॥ ८२ ॥

*mokṣasya kāṃkṣā yadi vai tavāsti tyajātidūrādviṣayānviṣaṃ yathā |  
pīyūṣavattoṣadayākṣamārjava praśāntidāntīrbhaja nityamādarāt || 82 ||*

If you truly long for Liberation, keep well away from sense objects as you would from poison, and always carefully cultivate the nectar-like virtues of contentment, compassion, forgiveness, honesty, calmness, and self-control.

### 83

<vt>Fascination for Body Criticised</vt>  
अनुक्षणं यत्परिहृत्य कृत्यं अनाद्यविद्याकृतबन्धमोक्षणम् ।  
देहः परार्थोऽयममुष्य पोषणे यः सज्जते स स्वमनेन हन्ति ॥ ८३ ॥

*<vt>Fascination for Body Criticised</vt>  
anukṣaṇaṃ yatparihṛtya kṛtyaṃ anādyavidyākṛtabandhamokṣaṇam |  
dehaḥ parārtho'yamamuṣya poṣaṇe yaḥ sajjate sa svamanena hanti || 83 ||*

Liberation from the bondage of beginningless Ignorance is what should always be striven for. Whoever sets it aside and eagerly tends this body, which is only an object for others to enjoy, is destroying himself.

### 84

शरीरपोषणार्थी सन् य आत्मानं दिदृक्षति ।  
ग्राहं दारुधिया धृत्वा नदि तर्तुं स गच्छति ॥ ८४ ॥

*śarīrapoṣaṇārthī san ya ātmānaṃ didṛkṣati |  
grāhaṃ dārudhiyā dhṛtvā nadi tartuṃ sa gacchati || 84 ||*

Someone who tries to realize the Self while devoting himself to feeding the body is setting out to cross a river by clinging to a crocodile he has mistaken for a log.

### 85

मोह एव महामृत्युर्मुमुक्षोर्वपुरादिषु ।  
मोहो विनिर्जितो येन स मुक्तिपदमर्हति ॥ ८५ ॥

*moha eva mahāmṛtyurmumukṣorvapurādiṣu |  
moho vinirjito yena sa muktipadamarhati || 85 ||*

For a seeker of Liberation, then, infatuation with things such as the body is a terrible death. Whoever has fully overcome it is worthy of the state of Freedom.

### 86

मोहं जहि महामृत्युं देहदारसुतादिषु ।  
यं जित्वा मुनयो यान्ति तद्विष्णोः परमं पदम् ॥ ८६ ॥

*mohaṃ jahi mahāmṛtyuṃ dehadārasutādiṣu |  
yaṃ jitvā munayo yānti tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padam || 86 ||*

Overcome the terrible death of infatuation with your body, wife, children, and the like. By overcoming it the sages reach that Supreme State of Vishnu.

### 87

<vt>Gross Body Condemned</vt>  
त्वङ्मांसरुधिरस्नायुमेदोमज्जास्थिसंकुलम् ।  
पूर्णं मूत्रपुरीषाभ्यां स्थूलं निन्द्यमिदं वपुः ॥ ८७ ॥

*<vt>Gross Body Condemned</vt>  
tvaṅmāṃsarudhirasnāyumedomajjāsthisaṃkulam |  
pūrṇaṃ mūtrapurīṣābhyāṃ sthūlaṃ nindyamidaṃ vapuḥ || 87 ||*

This gross body deserves contempt, for it is made of skin, flesh, blood, arteries and veins, fat, marrow, and bones, and it is filled with other foul things.

### 88

पञ्चीकृतेभ्यो भूतेभ्यः स्थूलेभ्यः पूर्वकर्मणा ।  
समुत्पन्नमिदं स्थूलं भोगायतनमात्मनः अवस्था जागरस्तस्य स्थूलार्थानुभवो यतः ॥ ८८ ॥

*pañcīkṛtebhyo bhūtebhyaḥ sthūlebhyaḥ pūrvakarmaṇā |  
samutpannamidaṃ sthūlaṃ bhogāyatanamātmanaḥ avasthā jāgarastasya sthūlārthānubhavo yataḥ || 88 ||*

One's past actions produce the gross body from the gross elements, which are formed when the subtle elements unite with one another. It serves as the soul's medium of experience. Its waking state is the one in which it perceives gross objects.

### 89

बाह्येन्द्रियैः स्थूलपदार्थसेवां स्रक्चन्दनस्त्र्यादिविचित्ररूपाम् ।  
करोति जीवः स्वयमेतदात्मना तस्मात्प्रशस्तिर्वपुषोऽस्य जागरे ॥ ८९ ॥

*bāhyendriyaiḥ sthūlapadārthasevāṃ srakcandanastryādivicitrarūpām |  
karoti jīvaḥ svayametadātmanā tasmātpraśastirvapuṣo'sya jāgare || 89 ||*

Identifying with this form, the individual soul, though distinct from it, enjoys gross objects such as garlands and sandal paste through the outer organs. That is why this body is most fully active in the waking state.

### 90

सर्वापि बाह्यसंसारः पुरुषस्य यदाश्रयः ।  
विद्धि देहमिदं स्थूलं गृहवद्गृहमेधिनः ॥ ९0 ॥

*sarvāpi bāhyasaṃsāraḥ puruṣasya yadāśrayaḥ |  
viddhi dehamidaṃ sthūlaṃ gṛhavadgṛhamedhinaḥ || 90 ||*

Understand this gross body as being like a house to its householder. All of a person's dealings with the outer world depend on it.

### 91

स्थूलस्य संभवजरामरणानि धर्माः स्थौल्यादयो बहुविधाः शिशुताद्यवस्थाः ।  
वर्णाश्रमादिनियमा बहुधामयाः स्युः पूजावमानबहुमानमुखा विशेषाः ॥ ९१ ॥

*sthūlasya saṃbhavajarāmaraṇāni dharmāḥ sthaulyādayo bahuvidhāḥ śiśutādyavasthāḥ |  
varṇāśramādiniyamā bahudhāmayāḥ syuḥ pūjāvamānabahumānamukhā viśeṣāḥ || 91 ||*

Birth, decay, and death are the various properties of the gross body, and stoutness and the like, childhood and the like, are its different conditions. It has various rules according to caste and order of life. It is prone to many diseases and receives different kinds of treatment, such as worship, insult, and high honors.

### 92

<vt>Organs of Perception and Action</vt>  
बुद्धीन्द्रियाणि श्रवणं त्वगक्षि घ्राणं च जिव्हा विषयावबोधनात् ।  
वाक्पाणिपादा गुदमप्युपस्थः कर्मेन्द्रियाणि प्रवणेन कर्मसु ॥ ९२ ॥

*<vt>Organs of Perception and Action</vt>  
buddhīndriyāṇi śravaṇaṃ tvagakṣi ghrāṇaṃ ca jivhā viṣayāvabodhanāt |  
vākpāṇipādā gudamapyupasthaḥ karmendriyāṇi pravaṇena karmasu || 92 ||*

The ears, skin, eyes, nose, and tongue are organs of knowledge, since they enable us to perceive objects. The vocal organs, hands, legs, and so on are organs of action, because of their inclination to work.

### 93

<vt>Inner Instruments</vt>  
निगद्यतेऽन्तःकरणं मनोधीः अहंकृतिश्चित्तमिति स्ववृत्तिभिः ।  
मनस्तु संकल्पविकल्पनादिभिः बुद्धिः पदार्थाध्यवसायधर्मतः ॥ ९३ ॥

*<vt>Inner Instruments</vt>  
nigadyate'ntaḥkaraṇaṃ manodhīḥ ahaṃkṛtiścittamiti svavṛttibhiḥ |  
manastu saṃkalpavikalpanādibhiḥ buddhiḥ padārthādhyavasāyadharmataḥ || 93 ||*

The inner organ (Antahkaraṇa) goes by the names Manas, Buddhi, ego, or Chitta, depending on what it is doing. It is Manas when it weighs the pros and cons of something; Buddhi when it determines the truth of objects; ego when it identifies with this body as one's own self; and Chitta when it goes in search of pleasurable objects.

### 94

अत्राभिमानादहमित्यहंकृतिः ।  
स्वार्थानुसन्धानगुणेन चित्तम् ॥ ९४ ॥

*atrābhimānādahamityahaṃkṛtiḥ |  
svārthānusandhānaguṇena cittam || 94 ||*

The inner organ (Antahkaraṇa) goes by the names Manas, Buddhi, ego, or Chitta, depending on what it is doing. It is Manas when it weighs the pros and cons of something; Buddhi when it determines the truth of objects; ego when it identifies with this body as one's own self; and Chitta when it goes in search of pleasurable objects.

### 95

<vt>The Five Pranas</vt>  
प्राणापानव्यानोदानसमाना भवत्यसौ प्राणः ।  
स्वयमेव वृत्तिभेदाद्विकृतिभेदात्सुवर्णसलिलादिवत् ॥ ९५ ॥

*<vt>The Five Pranas</vt>  
prāṇāpānavyānodānasamānā bhavatyasau prāṇaḥ |  
svayameva vṛttibhedādvikṛtibhedātsuvarṇasalilādivat || 95 ||*

One single Prāṇa (vital force) becomes Prāṇa, Apāna, Vyāna, Udāna, and Samāna according to differences of function and modification, as happens with gold, water, and so on.

### 96

<vt>Subtle Body - Effects</vt>  
वागादि पञ्च श्रवणादि पञ्च प्राणादि पञ्चाभ्रमुखानि पञ्च ।  
बुद्ध्याद्यविद्यापि च कामकर्मणी पुर्यष्टकं सूक्ष्मशरीरमाहुः ॥ ९६ ॥

*<vt>Subtle Body - Effects</vt>  
vāgādi pañca śravaṇādi pañca prāṇādi pañcābhramukhāni pañca |  
buddhyādyavidyāpi ca kāmakarmaṇī puryaṣṭakaṃ sūkṣmaśarīramāhuḥ || 96 ||*

The five organs of action beginning with speech, the five organs of knowledge beginning with the ear, the group of five Prāṇas, Buddhi and the rest, together with Nescience, desire, and action: these eight 'cities' make up what is called the subtle body.

### 97

इदं शरीरं शृणु सूक्ष्मसंज्ञितं लिङ्गं त्वपञ्चीकृतसंभवम् ।  
सवासनं कर्मफलानुभावकं स्वाज्ञानतोऽनादिरुपाधिरात्मनः ॥ ९७ ॥

*idaṃ śarīraṃ śṛṇu sūkṣmasaṃjñitaṃ liṅgaṃ tvapañcīkṛtasaṃbhavam |  
savāsanaṃ karmaphalānubhāvakaṃ svājñānato'nādirupādhirātmanaḥ || 97 ||*

Listen. This subtle body, also called the Liṅga body, comes from the elements before they have subdivided and combined with one another. It holds desires and makes the soul experience the results of its actions. It is a beginningless superimposition on the soul, brought about by the soul's own ignorance.

### 98

स्वप्नो भवत्यस्य विभक्त्यवस्था स्वमात्रशेषेण विभाति यत्र ।  
स्वप्ने तु बुद्धिः स्वयमेव जाग्रत् कालीननानाविधवासनाभिः ॥ ९८ ॥

*svapno bhavatyasya vibhaktyavasthā svamātraśeṣeṇa vibhāti yatra |  
svapne tu buddhiḥ svayameva jāgrat kālīnanānāvidhavasanābhiḥ || 98 ||*

Dream is a state of the soul different from waking, in which it shines by itself. In dreams Buddhi on its own takes up the role of agent and the like, because of the many desires of the waking state, while the supreme Ātman shines in Its own glory, with Buddhi as Its only superimposition. It witnesses everything and is untouched by even the smallest work that Buddhi does. Being completely unattached, It is untouched by any work that Its superimpositions may perform.

### 99

कर्त्रादिभावं प्रतिपद्य राजते यत्र स्वयं भाति ह्ययं परात्मा ।  
धीमात्रकोपाधिरशेषसाक्षी न लिप्यते तत्कृतकर्मलेशैः यस्मादसङ्गस्तत एव कर्मभिः न लिप्यते किंचिदुपाधिना कृतैः ॥ ९९ ॥

*kartrādibhāvaṃ pratipadya rājate yatra svayaṃ bhāti hyayaṃ parātmā |  
dhīmātrakopādhiraśeṣasākṣī na lipyate tatkṛtakarmaleśaiḥ yasmādasaṅgastata eva karmabhiḥ na lipyate kiñcidupādhinā kṛtaiḥ || 99 ||*

Dream is a state of the soul different from waking, in which it shines by itself. In dreams Buddhi on its own takes up the role of agent and the like, because of the many desires of the waking state, while the supreme Ātman shines in Its own glory, with Buddhi as Its only superimposition. It witnesses everything and is untouched by even the smallest work that Buddhi does. Being completely unattached, It is untouched by any work that Its superimpositions may perform.

### 100

सर्वव्यापृतिकरणं लिङ्गमिदं स्याच्चिदात्मनः पुंसः ।  
वास्यादिकमिव तक्ष्णस्तेनैवात्मा भवत्यसङ्गोऽयम् ॥ १00 ॥

*sarvavyāpṛtikaraṇaṃ liṅgamidaṃ syāccidātmanaḥ puṃsaḥ |  
vāsyādikamiva takṣṇastenaivātmā bhavatyasaṅgo'yam || 100 ||*

This subtle body is the tool for every activity of the Ātman, who is Knowledge Absolute, much as an adze and other implements serve a carpenter. For this reason the Ātman is perfectly unattached.

### 101

अन्धत्वमन्दत्वपटुत्वधर्माः सौगुण्यवैगुण्यवशाद्धि चक्षुषः ।  
बाधिर्यमूकत्वमुखास्तथैव श्रोत्रादिधर्मा न तु वेत्तुरात्मनः ॥ १0१ ॥

*andhatvamandatvapaṭutvadharmāḥ sauguṇyavaiguṇyavaśāddhi cakṣuṣaḥ |  
bādhiryamūkatvamukhāstathaiva śrotrādidharmā na tu vetturātmanaḥ || 101 ||*

Blindness, weakness, and sharpness are conditions of the eye, resulting simply from its soundness or its defects. Deafness, muteness, and the like likewise belong to the ear and the other organs. They never belong to the Ātman, the Knower.

### 102

<vt>Functions of Prāna</vt>  
उच्छ्वासनिःश्वासविजृम्भणक्षुत् प्रस्यन्दनाद्युत्क्रमणादिकाः क्रियाः ।  
प्राणादिकर्माणि वदन्ति तज्ञाः प्राणस्य धर्मावशनापिपासे ॥ १0२ ॥

*<vt>Functions of Prāna</vt>  
ucchvāsaniḥśvāsavijṛmbhaṇakṣut prasyandanādyutkramaṇādikāḥ kriyāḥ |  
prāṇādikarmāṇi vadanti tajñāḥ prāṇasya dharmāvaśanāpipāse || 102 ||*

Breathing in and out, yawning, sneezing, secretion, leaving this body, and so on are said by experts to be functions of Prāṇa and the others. Hunger and thirst are characteristics of Prāṇa proper.

### 103

<vt>Ego Discussed</vt>  
अन्तःकरणमेतेषु चक्षुरादिषु वर्ष्मणि ।  
अहमित्यभिमानेन तिष्ठत्याभासतेजसा ॥ १0३ ॥

*<vt>Ego Discussed</vt>  
antaḥkaraṇameteṣu cakṣurādiṣu varṣmaṇi |  
ahamityabhimānena tiṣṭhatyābhāsatejasā || 103 ||*

The inner organ (mind) is seated in organs such as the eye and in the body. It identifies with them and is endowed with a reflection of the Ātman.

### 104

अहंकारः स विज्ञेयः कर्ता भोक्ताभिमान्ययम् ।  
सत्त्वादिगुणयोगेन चावस्थात्रयमश्नुते ॥ १0४ ॥

*ahaṃkāraḥ sa vijñeyaḥ kartā bhoktābhimānyayam |  
sattvādiguṇayogena cāvasthātrayamaśnute || 104 ||*

Understand that it is Egoism that identifies with the body and becomes the doer or the experiencer, and that, joined with the Guṇas such as Sattva, takes on the three different states.

### 105

विषयाणामानुकूल्ये सुखी दुःखी विपर्यये ।  
सुखं दुःखं च तद्धर्मः सदानन्दस्य नात्मनः ॥ १0५ ॥

*viṣayāṇāmānukūlye sukhī duḥkhī viparyaye |  
sukhaṃ duḥkhaṃ ca taddharmaḥ sadānandasya nātmanaḥ || 105 ||*

When sense objects are favorable, it is happy; when they are unfavorable, it is miserable. Happiness and misery therefore belong to Egoism and not to the ever-blissful Ātman.

### 106

<vt>Infinite Love - the Self</vt>  
आत्मार्थत्वेन हि प्रेयान्विषयो न स्वतः प्रियः ।  
स्वत एव हि सर्वेषामात्मा प्रियतमो यतः तत आत्मा सदानन्दो नास्य दुःखं कदाचन ॥ १0६ ॥

*<vt>Infinite Love - the Self</vt>  
ātmārthatvena hi preyānviṣayo na svataḥ priyaḥ |  
svata eva hi sarveṣāmātmā priyatamo yataḥ tata ātmā sadānando nāsya duḥkhaṃ kadācana || 106 ||*

Sense objects give pleasure only in dependence on the Ātman manifesting through them, and never on their own, because the Ātman by Its very nature is what is most dear of all. So the Ātman is always blissful and never suffers misery.

### 107

यत्सुषुप्तौ निर्विषय आत्मानन्दोऽनुभूयते ।  
श्रुतिः प्रत्यक्षमैतिह्यमनुमानं च जाग्रति ॥ १0७ ॥

*yatsuṣuptau nirviṣaya ātmānando'nubhūyate |  
śrutiḥ pratyakṣamaitihyamanumānaṃ ca jāgrati || 107 ||*

Śruti, direct perception, tradition, and inference all clearly confirm that in deep sleep we experience the bliss of the Ātman without any sense objects.

### 108

<vt>Māyā - Pointed Out</vt>  
अव्यक्तनाम्नी परमेशशक्तिः अनाद्यविद्या त्रिगुणात्मिका परा ।  
कार्यानुमेया सुधियैव माया यया जगत्सर्वमिदं प्रसूयते ॥ १0८ ॥

*<vt>Māyā - Pointed Out</vt>  
avyaktanāmnī parameśaśaktiḥ anādyavidyā triguṇātmikā parā |  
kāryānumeyā sudhiyaiva māyā yayā jagatsarvamidaṃ prasūyate || 108 ||*

Avidya (Nescience), or Māyā, also called the Undifferentiated, is the power of the Lord. She has no beginning, consists of the three Guṇas, and is superior to the effects as their cause. A person of clear intellect can infer Her only from the effects She produces. It is She who gives birth to this entire universe.

### 109

सन्नाप्यसन्नाप्युभयात्मिका नो भिन्नाप्यभिन्नाप्युभयात्मिका नो ।  
साङ्गाप्यनङ्गा ह्युभयात्मिका नो महाद्भुतानिर्वचनीयरूपा ॥ १0९ ॥

*sannāpyasannāpyubhayātmikā no bhinnāpyabhinnāpyubhayātmikā no |  
sāṅgāpyanaṅgā hyubhayātmikā no mahādbhutānirvacanīyarūpā || 109 ||*

She is not existent, not non-existent, and not a mixture of the two. She is not the same, not different, and not both. She is not made of parts, not an indivisible whole, and not both. She is utterly wondrous and lies beyond description in words.

### 110

शुद्धाद्वयब्रह्मविभोधनाश्या सर्पभ्रमो रज्जुविवेकतो यथा ।  
रजस्तमःसत्त्वमिति प्रसिद्धा गुणास्तदीयाः प्रथितैः स्वकार्यैः ॥ ११0 ॥

*śuddhādvayabrahmavibhodhanāśyā sarpabhramo rajjuvivekato yathā |  
rajastamaḥsattvamiti prasiddhā guṇāstadīyāḥ prathitaiḥ svakāryaiḥ || 110 ||*

Māyā can be destroyed by realizing the pure Brahman, the one without a second, just as the mistaken notion of a snake is dispelled by recognizing the rope. Her Guṇas are Rajas, Tamas, and Sattva, each named for its function.

### 111

<vt>Rajoguņa - Nature and Effects</vt>  
विक्षेपशक्ती रजसः क्रियात्मिका यतः प्रवृत्तिः प्रसृता पुराणी ।  
रागादयोऽस्याः प्रभवन्ति नित्यं दुःखादयो ये मनसो विकाराः ॥ १११ ॥

*<vt>Rajoguņa - Nature and Effects</vt>  
vikṣepaśaktī rajasaḥ kriyātmikā yataḥ pravṛttiḥ prasṛtā purāṇī |  
rāgādayo'syāḥ prabhavanti nityaṃ duḥkhādayo ye manaso vikārāḥ || 111 ||*

Rajas has vikṣepa-Shakti, the projecting power, which is activity in nature. This primeval stream of activity flowed forth from it. From it, too, mental changes such as attachment and grief are constantly generated.

### 112

कामः क्रोधो लोभदम्भाद्यसूया अहंकारेर्ष्यामत्सराद्यास्तु घोराः ।  
धर्मा एते राजसाः पुम्प्रवृत्तिः यस्मादेषा तद्रजो बन्धहेतुः ॥ ११२ ॥

*kāmaḥ krodho lobhadambhādyasūyā ahaṃkārerṣyāmatsarādyāstu ghorāḥ |  
dharmā ete rājasāḥ pumpravṛttiḥ yasmādeṣā tadrajo bandhahetuḥ || 112 ||*

Lust, anger, greed, arrogance, spite, egoism, envy, jealousy, and the like are the terrible attributes of Rajas, and they give rise to a person's worldly tendency. Rajas is therefore a cause of bondage.

### 113

<vt>Tamoguņa - Nature and Effects</vt>  
एषावृतिर्नाम तमोगुणस्य शक्तिर्मया वस्त्ववभासतेऽन्यथा ।  
सैषा निदानं पुरुषस्य संसृतेः विक्षेपशक्तेः प्रवणस्य हेतुः ॥ ११३ ॥

*<vt>Tamoguņa - Nature and Effects</vt>  
eṣāvṛtirnāma tamoguṇasya śaktirmayā vastvavabhāsate'nyathā |  
saiṣā nidānaṃ puruṣasya saṃsṛteḥ vikṣepaśakteḥ pravaṇasya hetuḥ || 113 ||*

Āvṛti, the veiling power, is the power of Tamas that makes things seem other than they are. It is what causes a person's repeated transmigrations, and it sets the projecting power (Vikṣepa) in motion.

### 114

प्रज्ञावानपि पण्डितोऽपि चतुरोऽप्यत्यन्तसूक्ष्मात्मदृग् व्यालीढस्तमसा न वेत्ति बहुधा संबोधितोऽपि स्फुटम् ।  
भ्रान्त्यारोपितमेव साधु कलयत्यालम्बते तद्गुणान् हन्तासौ प्रबला दुरन्ततमसः शक्तिर्महत्यावृतिः ॥ ११४ ॥

*prajñāvānapi paṇḍito'pi caturo'pyatyantasūkṣmātmadṛg vyālīḍhastamasā na vetti bahudhā saṃbodhito'pi sphuṭam |  
bhrāntyāropitameva sādhu kalayatyālambate tadguṇān hantāsau prabalā durantatamasaḥ śaktirmahatyāvṛtiḥ || 114 ||*

Even wise and learned people, clever people skilled in perceiving the extremely subtle Ātman, are overcome by Tamas and fail to understand the Ātman, though it is plainly explained in many ways. They take as true what is only superimposed by delusion, and they cling to its effects. Alas, how mighty is the great Āvṛti Shakti of dreadful Tamas!

### 115

अभावना वा विपरीतभावना असंभावना विप्रतिपत्तिरस्याः ।  
संसर्गयुक्तं न विमुञ्चति ध्रुवं विक्षेपशक्तिः क्षपयत्यजस्रम् ॥ ११५ ॥

*abhāvanā vā viparītabhāvanā asaṃbhāvanā vipratipattirasyāḥ |  
saṃsargayuktaṃ na vimuñcati dhruvaṃ vikṣepaśaktiḥ kṣapayatyajasram || 115 ||*

Lack of right judgment, wrong judgment, absence of firm conviction, and doubt never leave anyone who has any connection with this veiling power. The projecting power then causes endless trouble.

### 116

अज्ञानमालस्यजडत्वनिद्रा प्रमादमूढत्वमुखास्तमोगुणाः ।  
एतैः प्रयुक्तो नहि वेत्ति किंचिन् निद्रालुवत्स्तम्भवदेव तिष्ठति ॥ ११६ ॥

*ajñānamālasyajaḍatvanidrā pramādamūḍhatvamukhāstamoguṇāḥ |  
etaiḥ prayukto nahi vetti kiṃcin nidrāluvatstambhavadeva tiṣṭhati || 116 ||*

Ignorance, lethargy, dullness, sleep, carelessness, stupidity, and the like are attributes of Tamas. Someone bound by them grasps nothing and remains like a sleeper, or like a post or a stone.

### 117

<vt>Sattvaguņa - Nature and Effects</vt>  
सत्त्वं विशुद्धं जलवत्तथापि ताभ्यां मिलित्वा सरणाय कल्पते ।  
यत्रात्मबिम्बः प्रतिबिम्बितः सन् प्रकाशयत्यर्क इवाखिलं जडम् ॥ ११७ ॥

*<vt>Sattvaguņa - Nature and Effects</vt>  
sattvaṃ viśuddhaṃ jalavattathāpi tābhyāṃ militvā saraṇāya kalpate |  
yatrātmabimbaḥ pratibimbitaḥ san prakāśayatyarka ivākhilaṃ jaḍam || 117 ||*

Pure Sattva is clear like water, yet when joined with Rajas and Tamas it leads to transmigration. The reality of the Ātman is reflected in Sattva and, like the sun, illuminates the whole world of matter.

### 118

मिश्रस्य सत्त्वस्य भवन्ति धर्माः त्वमानिताद्या नियमा यमाद्याः ।  
श्रद्धा च भक्तिश्च मुमुक्षता च दैवी च सम्पत्तिरसन्निवृत्तिः ॥ ११८ ॥

*miśrasya sattvasya bhavanti dharmāḥ tvamānitādyā niyamā yamādyāḥ |  
śraddhā ca bhaktiśca mumukṣatā ca daivī ca sampattirasannivṛttiḥ || 118 ||*

Mixed Sattva shows itself in a complete absence of pride and the like, in Niyama, Yama, and so on, and in faith, devotion, longing for Liberation, the divine tendencies, and turning away from the unreal.

### 119

विशुद्धसत्त्वस्य गुणाः प्रसादः स्वात्मानुभूतिः परमा प्रशान्तिः ।  
तृप्तिः प्रहर्षः परमात्मनिष्ठा यया सदानन्दरसं समृच्छति ॥ ११९ ॥

*viśuddhasattvasya guṇāḥ prasādaḥ svātmānubhūtiḥ paramā praśāntiḥ |  
tṛptiḥ praharṣaḥ paramātmaniṣṭhā yayā sadānandarasaṃ samṛcchati || 119 ||*

Pure Sattva shows itself in cheerfulness, realization of one's own Self, supreme peace, contentment, bliss, and steady devotion to the Ātman, through which the aspirant enjoys everlasting bliss.

### 120

<vt>The Causal Body - Its Nature</vt>  
अव्यक्तमेतत्त्रिगुणैर्निरुक्तं तत्कारणं नाम शरीरमात्मनः ।  
सुषुप्तिरेतस्य विभक्त्यवस्था प्रलीनसर्वेन्द्रियबुद्धिवृत्तिः ॥ १२0 ॥

*<vt>The Causal Body - Its Nature</vt>  
avyaktametattriguṇairniruktaṃ tatkāraṇaṃ nāma śarīramātmanaḥ |  
suṣuptiretasya vibhaktyavasthā pralīnasarvendriyabuddhivṛttiḥ || 120 ||*

The causal body of the soul is this Undifferentiated, which is said to be a compound of the three Guṇas. Its particular state is deep sleep, when the mind and all the organs stop functioning.

### 121

सर्वप्रकारप्रमितिप्रशान्तिः बीजात्मनावस्थितिरेव बुद्धेः ।  
सुषुप्तिरेतस्य किल प्रतीतिः किंचिन्न वेद्मीति जगत्प्रसिद्धेः ॥ १२१ ॥

*sarvaprakārapramitipraśāntiḥ bījātmanāvasthitireva buddheḥ |  
suṣuptiretasya kila pratītiḥ kiṃcinna vedmīti jagatprasiddheḥ || 121 ||*

In deep sleep every kind of perception stops, and the mind stays on in a subtle, seed-like form. The proof is what everyone says afterward: "I knew nothing then."

### 122

<vt>Not-Self - Description</vt>  
देहेन्द्रियप्राणमनोऽहमादयः सर्वे विकारा विषयाः सुखादयः ।  
व्योमादिभूतान्यखिलं न विश्वं अव्यक्तपर्यन्तमिदं ह्यनात्मा ॥ १२२ ॥

*<vt>Not-Self - Description</vt>  
dehendriyaprāṇamano'hamādayaḥ sarve vikārā viṣayāḥ sukhādayaḥ |  
vyomādibhūtānyakhilaṃ na viśvaṃ avyaktaparyantamidaṃ hyanātmā || 122 ||*

The body, the organs, the Prāṇas, Manas, egoism and the like, all modifications, the objects of the senses, pleasure and so on, the gross elements such as ether, indeed the entire universe up to the Undifferentiated: all of this is the non-Self.

### 123

माया मायाकार्यं सर्वं महदादिदेहपर्यन्तम् ।  
असदिदमनात्मतत्त्वं विद्धि त्वं मरुमरीचिकाकल्पम् ॥ १२३ ॥

*māyā māyākāryaṃ sarvaṃ mahadādidehaparyantam |  
asadidamanātmatattvaṃ viddhi tvaṃ marumarīcikākalpam || 123 ||*

Everything from Mahat down to the gross body is produced by Māyā. Understand that all these, and Māyā itself, are the non-Self, and so are as unreal as a mirage in the desert.

### 124

<vt>The Self - Its Nature</vt>  
अथ ते संप्रवक्ष्यामि स्वरूपं परमात्मनः ।  
यद्विज्ञाय नरो बन्धान्मुक्तः कैवल्यमश्नुते ॥ १२४ ॥

*<vt>The Self - Its Nature</vt>  
atha te saṃpravakṣyāmi svarūpaṃ paramātmanaḥ |  
yadvijñāya naro bandhānmuktaḥ kaivalyamaśnute || 124 ||*

Now I will explain to you the true nature of the supreme Self. By realizing it, a person is released from bondage and attains Liberation.

### 125

अस्ति कश्चित्स्वयं नित्यमहंप्रत्ययलम्बनः ।  
अवस्थात्रयसाक्षी संपञ्चकोशविलक्षणः ॥ १२५ ॥

*asti kaścitsvayaṃ nityamahaṃpratyayalambanaḥ |  
avasthātrayasākṣī saṃpañcakośavilakṣaṇaḥ || 125 ||*

There is a certain Absolute Entity that is the eternal ground of the sense of ego. It witnesses the three states and is distinct from the five sheaths or coverings.

### 126

यो विजानाति सकलं जाग्रत्स्वप्नसुषुप्तिषु ।  
बुद्धितद्वृत्तिसद्भावमभावमहमित्ययम् ॥ १२६ ॥

*yo vijānāti sakalaṃ jāgratsvapnasuṣuptiṣu |  
buddhitadvṛttisadbhāvamabhāvamahamityayam || 126 ||*

It knows all that occurs in waking, in dream, and in deep sleep. It is aware of whether the mind and its functions are present or absent. It underlies the notion of ego. This is That.

### 127

यः पश्यति स्वयं सर्वं यं न पश्यति कश्चन ।  
यश्चेतयति बुद्ध्यादि न तद्यं चेतयत्ययम् ॥ १२७ ॥

*yaḥ paśyati svayaṃ sarvaṃ yaṃ na paśyati kaścana |  
yaścetayati buddhyādi na tadyaṃ cetayatyayam || 127 ||*

It sees everything, yet no one sees It. It lights up the intellect and the rest, yet they cannot light It up. This is That.

### 128

येन विश्वमिदं व्याप्तं यं न व्याप्नोति किंचन ।  
अभारूपमिदं सर्वं यं भान्त्यमनुभात्ययम् ॥ १२८ ॥

*yena viśvamidaṃ vyāptaṃ yaṃ na vyāpnoti kiṃcana |  
abhārūpamidaṃ sarvaṃ yaṃ bhāntyamanubhātyayam || 128 ||*

It pervades this universe, yet nothing pervades It. When It shines, all this (universe) shines as Its reflection. This is That.

### 129

यस्य सन्निधिमात्रेण देहेन्द्रियमनोधियः ।  
विषयेषु स्वकीयेषु वर्तन्ते प्रेरिता इव ॥ १२९ ॥

*yasya sannidhimātreṇa dehendriyamanodhiyaḥ |  
viṣayeṣu svakīyeṣu vartante preritā iva || 129 ||*

Simply because It is present, the body, the organs, the mind, and the intellect stay within their own fields of action, as servants do!

### 130

अहङ्कारादिदेहान्ता विषयाश्च सुखादयः ।  
वेद्यन्ते घटवद्येन नित्यबोधस्वरूपिणा ॥ १३0 ॥

*ahaṅkārādidehāntā viṣayāśca sukhādayaḥ |  
vedyante ghaṭavadyena nityabodhasvarūpiṇā || 130 ||*

Through It, everything from the ego down to the body, the objects of the senses, pleasure and so on is known as plainly as a jar, for It is the very essence of Eternal Knowledge!

### 131

एषोऽन्तरात्मा पुरुषः पुराणो निरन्तराखण्डसुखानुभूतिः ।  
सदैकरूपः प्रतिबोधमात्रो येनेषिता वागसवश्चरन्ति ॥ १३१ ॥

*eṣo'ntarātmā puruṣaḥ purāṇo nirantarākhaṇḍasukhānubhūtiḥ |  
sadaikarūpaḥ pratibodhamātro yeneṣitā vāgasavaścaranti || 131 ||*

This is the innermost Self, the primeval Purusha (Being). Its essence is the unbroken experience of infinite Bliss. It stays always the same, yet is reflected through the various modifications of the mind, and at Its command the organs and Prāṇas carry out their work.

### 132

अत्रैव सत्त्वात्मनि धीगुहायां अव्याकृताकाश उशत्प्रकाशः ।  
आकाश उच्चै रविवत्प्रकाशते स्वतेजसा विश्वमिदं प्रकाशयन् ॥ १३२ ॥

*atraiva sattvātmani dhīguhāyāṃ avyākṛtākāśa uśatprakāśaḥ |  
ākāśa uccai ravivatprakāśate svatejasā viśvamidaṃ prakāśayan || 132 ||*

Right here in this body, in the mind full of Sattva, in the hidden chamber of the intellect, in the Akasha called the Unmanifested, the Ātman of enchanting splendor shines like the sun on high, revealing this universe by Its own radiance.

### 133

ज्ञाता मनोऽहंकृतिविक्रियाणां देहेन्द्रियप्राणकृतक्रियाणाम् ।  
अयोऽग्निवत्ताननुवर्तमानो न चेष्टते नो विकरोति किंचन ॥ १३३ ॥

*jñātā mano'haṃkṛtivikriyāṇāṃ dehendriyaprāṇakṛtakriyāṇām |  
ayo'gnivattānanuvartamāno na ceṣṭate no vikaroti kiṃcana || 133 ||*

It knows the movements of the mind and ego, and the activities of the body, organs, and Prāṇas. It seems to take on their forms, as fire takes the form of a ball of iron. Yet It does not act and does not change in the slightest.

### 134

न जायते नो म्रियते न वर्धते न क्षीयते नो विकरोति नित्यः ।  
विलीयमानेऽपि वपुष्यमुष्मिन् न लीयते कुम्भ इवाम्बरं स्वयम् ॥ १३४ ॥

*na jāyate no mriyate na vardhate na kṣīyate no vikaroti nityaḥ |  
vilīyamāne'pi vapuṣyamuṣmin na līyate kumbha ivāmbaraṃ svayam || 134 ||*

It is not born and does not die. It neither grows nor declines, and being eternal It undergoes no change. It does not stop existing even when this body is destroyed, just as the sky in a jar remains (after the jar is broken), because It depends on nothing.

### 135

प्रकृतिविकृतिभिन्नः शुद्धबोधस्वभावः सदसदिदमशेषं भासयन्निर्विशेषः ।  
विलसति परमात्मा जाग्रदादिष्ववस्था स्वहमहमिति साक्षात्साक्षिरूपेण बुद्धेः ॥ १३५ ॥

*prakṛtivikṛtibhinnaḥ śuddhabodhasvabhāvaḥ sadasadidamaśeṣaṃ bhāsayannirviśeṣaḥ |  
vilasati paramātmā jāgradādiṣvavasthā svahamahamiti sākṣātsākṣirūpeṇa buddheḥ || 135 ||*

The Supreme Self is different from Prakriti and its modifications; Its essence is Pure Knowledge, and It is Absolute. In waking and the other states It directly reveals this whole gross and subtle universe as the ground of the persisting sense of ego, and It reveals Itself as the Witness of the Buddhi, the faculty that determines.

### 136

<vt>Advice for Self-control</vt>  
नियमितमनसामुं त्वं स्वमात्मानमात्मन्य् अयमहमिति साक्षाद्विद्धि बुद्धिप्रसादात् ।  
जनिमरणतरङ्गापारसंसारसिन्धुं प्रतर भव कृतार्थो ब्रह्मरूपेण संस्थः ॥ १३६ ॥

*<vt>Advice for Self-control</vt>  
niyamitamanasāmuṃ tvaṃ svamātmānamātmany ayamahamiti sākṣādviddhi buddhiprasādāt |  
janimaraṇataraṅgāpārasaṃsārasindhuṃ pratara bhava kṛtārtho brahmarūpeṇa saṃsthaḥ || 136 ||*

With a disciplined mind and a purified intellect (Buddhi), directly realize your own Self within the body and become one with It. Cross the endless ocean of Samsara, whose waves are birth and death, and be blessed, firmly rooted in Brahman as your own essence.

### 137

<vt>What is Bondage - The Reply</vt>  
अत्रानात्मन्यहमिति मतिर्बन्ध एषोऽस्य पुंसः प्राप्तोऽज्ञानाज्जननमरणक्लेशसंपातहेतुः ।  
येनैवायं वपुरिदमसत्सत्यमित्यात्मबुद्ध्या पुष्यत्युक्षत्यवति विषयैस्तन्तुभिः कोशकृद्वत् ॥ १३७ ॥

*<vt>What is Bondage - The Reply</vt>  
atrānātmanyahamiti matirbandha eṣo'sya puṃsaḥ prāpto'jñānājjananamaraṇakleśasaṃpātahetuḥ |  
yenaivāyaṃ vapuridamasatsatyamityātmabuddhyā puṣyatyukṣatyavati viṣayaistantubhiḥ kośakṛdvat || 137 ||*

To identify the Self with what is not the Self: this is human bondage. It comes from ignorance and brings the sufferings of birth and death in its wake. Because of it, a person takes this fleeting body to be real, identifies with it, and feeds, bathes, and protects it with (pleasant) objects of the senses. In this way he becomes bound, as a caterpillar is bound by the threads of its cocoon.

### 138

अतस्मिंस्तद्बुद्धिः प्रभवति विमूढस्य तमसा विवेकाभावाद्वै स्फुरति भुजगे रज्जुधिषणा ।  
ततोऽनर्थव्रातो निपतति समादातुरधिकः ततो योऽसद्ग्राहः स हि भवति बन्धः शृणु सखे ॥ १३८ ॥

*atasmiṃstadbuddhiḥ prabhavati vimūḍhasya tamasā vivekābhāvādvai sphurati bhujage rajjudhiṣaṇā |  
tato'narthavrāto nipatati samādāturadhikaḥ tato yo'sadgrāhaḥ sa hi bhavati bandhaḥ śṛṇu sakhe || 138 ||*

Someone overcome by ignorance takes a thing to be what it is not. Lack of discrimination makes a person mistake a snake for a rope, and when he grabs it under that false idea, great danger comes upon him. So listen, my friend: taking passing things to be real is what constitutes bondage.

### 139

अखण्डनित्याद्वयबोधशक्त्या स्फुरन्तमात्मानमनन्तवैभवम् ।  
समावृणोत्यावृतिशक्तिरेषा तमोमयी राहुरिवार्कबिम्बम् ॥ १३९ ॥

*akhaṇḍanityādvayabodhaśaktyā sphurantamātmānamanantavaibhavam |  
samāvṛṇotyāvṛtiśaktireṣā tamomayī rāhurivārkabimbam || 139 ||*

This veiling power (Avriti), which dominates in ignorance, covers the Self, just as Rāhu covers the disc of the sun. The Self has infinite glories, reveals Itself through the power of knowledge, and is indivisible, eternal, and one without a second.

### 140

तिरोभूते स्वात्मन्यमलतरतेजोवति पुमान् अनात्मानं मोहादहमिति शरीरं कलयति ।  
ततः कामक्रोधप्रभृतिभिरमुं बन्धनगुणैः परं विक्षेपाख्या रजस उरुशक्तिर्व्यथयति ॥ १४0 ॥

*tirobhūte svātmanyamalataratejovati pumān anātmānaṃ mohādahamiti śarīraṃ kalayati |  
tataḥ kāmakrodhaprabhṛtibhiramuṃ bandhanaguṇaiḥ paraṃ vikṣepākhyā rajasa uruśaktirvyathayati || 140 ||*

When a person's own Self, shining with the purest splendor, is hidden from sight, ignorance leads him to identify falsely with this body, which is the non-Self. Then the great power of rajas known as the projecting power torments him severely with the binding chains of lust, anger, and the like.

### 141

महामोहग्राहग्रसनगलितात्मावगमनो धियो नानावस्थां स्वयमभिनयंस्तद्गुणतया ।  
अपारे संसरे विषयविषपूरे जलनिधौ निमज्योन्मज्यायं भ्रमति कुमतिः कुत्सितगतिः ॥ १४१ ॥

*mahāmohagrāhagrasanagalitātmāvagamano dhiyo nānāvasthāṃ svayamabhinayaṃstadguṇatayā |  
apāre saṃsare viṣayaviṣapūre jalanidhau nimajyonmajyāyaṃ bhramati kumatiḥ kutsitagatiḥ || 141 ||*

A person of distorted intellect, whose Self-knowledge has been swallowed by the shark of total ignorance, imitates the various states of the intellect (Buddhi), since that is Its superimposed attribute. He drifts up and down in this limitless ocean of Samsara, full of the poison of sense-enjoyment, now sinking, now rising. What a wretched fate!

### 142

भानुप्रभासंजनिताभ्रपङ्क्तिः भानुं तिरोधाय विजृम्भते यथा ।  
आत्मोदिताहंकृतिरात्मतत्त्वं तथा तिरोधाय विजृम्भते स्वयम् ॥ १४२ ॥

*bhānuprabhāsaṃjanitābhrapaṅktiḥ bhānuṃ tirodhāya vijṛmbhate yathā |  
ātmoditāhaṃkṛtirātmatattvaṃ tathā tirodhāya vijṛmbhate svayam || 142 ||*

Banks of cloud produced by the sun's rays cover the sun, and they alone are then visible (in the sky). In the same way ego, produced by the Self, covers the reality of the Self and appears on its own.

### 143

<vt>The Powers - Agitation and Veiling</vt>  
कवलितदिननार्थे दुर्दिने सान्द्रमेघैः व्यथयति हिमझंझावायुरुग्रो यथैतान् ।  
अविरततमसात्मन्यावृते मूढबुद्धिं क्षपयति बहुदुःखैस्तीव्रविक्षेपशक्तिः ॥ १४३ ॥

*<vt>The Powers - Agitation and Veiling</vt>  
kavalitadinanārthe durdine sāndrameghaiḥ vyathayati himajhaṃjhāvāyurugro yathaitān |  
aviratatamasātmanyāvṛte mūḍhabuddhiṃ kṣapayati bahuduḥkhaistīvravikṣepaśaktiḥ || 143 ||*

On an overcast day, when thick clouds have swallowed the sun, fierce cold winds harass them. In the same way, when intense ignorance has hidden the Ātman, the terrible Vikṣepa Shakti (projecting power) afflicts the foolish person with many sorrows.

### 144

एताभ्यामेव शक्तिभ्यां बन्धः पुंसः समागतः ।  
याभ्यां विमोहितो देहं मत्वात्मानं भ्रमत्ययम् ॥ १४४ ॥

*etābhyāmeva śaktibhyāṃ bandhaḥ puṃsaḥ samāgataḥ |  
yābhyāṃ vimohito dehaṃ matvātmānaṃ bhramatyayam || 144 ||*

Human bondage has arisen from these two powers. Deceived by them, a person mistakes the body for the Self and wanders (from body to body).

### 145

<vt>Bondage in Action</vt>  
बीजं संसृतिभूमिजस्य तु तमो देहात्मधीरङ्कुरो रागः पल्लवमम्बु कर्म तु वपुः स्कन्धोओऽसवः शाखिकाः ।  
अग्राणीन्द्रियसंहतिश्च विषयाः पुष्पाणि दुःखं फलं नानाकर्मसमुद्भवं बहुविधं भोक्तात्र जीवः खगः ॥ १४५ ॥

*<vt>Bondage in Action</vt>  
bījaṃ saṃsṛtibhūmijasya tu tamo dehātmadhīraṅkuro rāgaḥ pallavamambu karma tu vapuḥ skandhoo'savaḥ śākhikāḥ |  
agrāṇīndriyasaṃhatiśca viṣayāḥ puṣpāṇi duḥkhaṃ phalaṃ nānākarmasamudbhavaṃ bahuvidhaṃ bhoktātra jīvaḥ khagaḥ || 145 ||*

Ignorance is the seed of the tree of Samsara. Identification with the body is its sprout, attachment its tender leaves, work its water, the body its trunk, the vital forces its branches, the organs its twigs, the objects of the senses its flowers, the many sufferings from various works its fruits, and the individual soul is the bird sitting on it.

### 146

अज्ञानमूलोऽयमनात्मबन्धो नैसर्गिकोऽनादिरनन्त ईरितः ।  
जन्माप्ययव्याधिजरादिदुःख प्रवाहपातं जनयत्यमुष्य ॥ १४६ ॥

*ajñānamūlo'yamanātmabandho naisargiko'nādirananta īritaḥ |  
janmāpyayavyādhijarādiduḥkha pravāhapātaṃ janayatyamuṣya || 146 ||*

This bondage of the non-Self arises from ignorance, is self-caused, and is said to be without beginning or end. It exposes a person to the long chain of sufferings such as birth, death, disease, and old age.

### 147

<vt>Ātman and Anātman - Discrimination</vt>  
नास्त्रैर्न शस्त्रैरनिलेन वन्हिना छेत्तुं न शक्यो न च कर्मकोटिभिः ।  
विवेकविज्ञानमहासिना विना धातुः प्रसादेन शितेन मञ्जुना ॥ १४७ ॥

*<vt>Ātman and Anātman - Discrimination</vt>  
nāstrairna śastrairanilena vanhinā chettuṃ na śakyo na ca karmakoṭibhiḥ |  
vivekavijñānamahāsinā vinā dhātuḥ prasādena śitena mañjunā || 147 ||*

Weapons cannot destroy this bondage, nor wind, nor fire, nor millions of acts. Nothing can, except the marvelous sword of knowledge born of discrimination and sharpened by the grace of the Lord.

### 148

श्रुतिप्रमाणैकमतेः स्वधर्म निष्ठा तयैवात्मविशुद्धिरस्य ।  
विशुद्धबुद्धेः परमात्मवेदनं तेनैव संसारसमूलनाशः ॥ १४८ ॥

*śrutipramāṇaikamateḥ svadharma niṣṭhā tayaivātmaviśuddhirasya |  
viśuddhabuddheḥ paramātmavedanaṃ tenaiva saṃsārasamūlanāśaḥ || 148 ||*

Whoever is ardently devoted to the authority of the Śrutis becomes steady in his Svadharma, and that alone leads to purity of mind. A person of pure mind realizes the Supreme Self, and by this alone Samsara is destroyed, root and all.

### 149

कोशैरन्नमयाद्यैः पञ्चभिरात्मा न संवृतो भाति ।  
निजशक्तिसमुत्पन्नैः शैवालपटलैरिवाम्बु वापीस्थम् ॥ १४९ ॥

*kośairannamayādyaiḥ pañcabhirātmā na saṃvṛto bhāti |  
nijaśaktisamutpannaiḥ śaivālapaṭalairivāmbu vāpīstham || 149 ||*

Covered by the five sheaths, starting with the material one, which are products of Its own power, the Self no longer appears, just as the water of a tank is hidden when moss builds up on it.

### 150

तच्छैवालापनये सम्यक्सलिलं प्रतीयते शुद्धम् ।  
तृष्णासन्तापहरं सद्यः सौख्यप्रदं परं पुंसः ॥ १५0 ॥

*tacchaivālāpanaye samyaksalilaṃ pratīyate śuddham |  
tṛṣṇāsantāpaharaṃ sadyaḥ saukhyapradaṃ paraṃ puṃsaḥ || 150 ||*

When that moss is cleared away, perfectly pure water, which relieves the pain of thirst and brings instant joy, lies open before the person.

### 151

पञ्चानामपि कोशानामपवादे विभात्ययं शुद्धः ।  
नित्यानन्दैकरसः प्रत्यग्रूपः परः स्वयंज्योतिः ॥ १५१ ॥

*pañcānāmapi kośānāmapavāde vibhātyayaṃ śuddhaḥ |  
nityānandaikarasaḥ pratyagrūpaḥ paraḥ svayaṃjyotiḥ || 151 ||*

When all five sheaths have been set aside, a person's Self appears: pure, made of lasting and unmixed bliss, indwelling, supreme, and self-radiant.

### 152

आत्मानात्मविवेकः कर्तव्यो बन्धमुक्तये विदुषा ।  
तेनैवानन्दी भवति स्वं विज्ञाय सच्चिदानन्दम् ॥ १५२ ॥

*ātmānātmavivekaḥ kartavyo bandhamuktaye viduṣā |  
tenaivānandī bhavati svaṃ vijñāya saccidānandam || 152 ||*

A wise person who wants to end his bondage should discriminate between the Self and the non-Self. That alone lets him know his own Self as Existence-Knowledge-Bliss Absolute, and he becomes happy.

### 153

मुञ्जादिषीकामिव दृश्यवर्गात् प्रत्यञ्चमात्मानमसङ्गमक्रियम् ।  
विविच्य तत्र प्रविलाप्य सर्वं तदात्मना तिष्ठति यः स मुक्तः ॥ १५३ ॥

*muñjādiṣīkāmiva dṛśyavargāt pratyañcamātmānamasaṅgamakriyam |  
vivicya tatra pravilāpya sarvaṃ tadātmanā tiṣṭhati yaḥ sa muktaḥ || 153 ||*

That person is truly free who separates all objects of the senses from the indwelling, unattached, and inactive Self, the way one pulls a stalk of grass from its enclosing sheath, and who, merging everything in It, stays in identity with That.

### 154

<vt>a) Annamaya kośa (Food sheath)</vt>  
देहोऽयमन्नभवनोऽन्नमायास्तु कोशः चान्नेन जीवति विनश्यति तद्विहीनः ।  
त्वक्चर्ममांसरुधिरास्थिपुरीषराशिः नायं स्वयं भवितुमर्हति नित्यशुद्धः ॥ १५४ ॥

*<vt>a) Annamaya kośa (Food sheath)</vt>  
deho'yamannabhavano'nnaMāyāstu kośaḥ cānnena jīvati vinaśyati tadvihīnaḥ |  
tvakcarmamāṃsarudhirāsthipurīṣarāśiḥ nāyaṃ svayaṃ bhavitumarhati nityaśuddhaḥ || 154 ||*

This body of ours is made from food and constitutes the material sheath. It lives on food and dies without it. It is a heap of skin, flesh, blood, bones, and filth, and it can never be the eternally pure, self-existent Ātman.

### 155

पूर्वं जनेरधिमृतेरपि नायमस्ति जातक्षणः क्षणगुणोऽनियतस्वभावः ।  
नैको जडश्च घटवत्परिदृश्यमानः स्वात्मा कथं भवति भावविकारवेत्ता ॥ १५५ ॥

*pūrvaṃ janeradhimṛterapi nāyamasti jātakṣaṇaḥ kṣaṇaguṇo'niyatasvabhāvaḥ |  
naiko jaḍaśca ghaṭavatparidṛśyamānaḥ svātmā kathaṃ bhavati bhāvavikāravettā || 155 ||*

It does not exist before its origin or after its dissolution; it lasts only for a short (intervening) period. Its good qualities are fleeting, and by nature it keeps changing. It is manifold and inert, and is an object of the senses, like a jar. How could it be one's own Self, the Witness of the changes in all things?

### 156

पाणिपादादिमान्देहो नात्मा व्यङ्गेऽपि जीवनात् ।  
तत्तच्छक्तेरनाशाच्च न नियम्यो नियामकः ॥ १५६ ॥

*pāṇipādādimāndeho nātmā vyaṅge'pi jīvanāt |  
tattacchakteranāśācca na niyamyo niyāmakaḥ || 156 ||*

The body, made up of arms, legs, and so on, cannot be the Ātman, since a person goes on living when particular limbs are lost, and the organism's various functions also remain intact. A body subject to another's rule cannot be the Self, which is the Ruler of all.

### 157

देहतद्धर्मतत्कर्मतदवस्थादिसाक्षिणः ।  
सत एव स्वतःसिद्धं तद्वैलक्षण्यमात्मनः ॥ १५७ ॥

*dehataddharmatatkarmatadavasthādisākṣiṇaḥ |  
sata eva svataḥsiddhaṃ tadvailakṣaṇyamātmanaḥ || 157 ||*

It is self-evident that the Ātman, the abiding Reality, is different from the body and from its characteristics, activities, states, and so on, all of which It witnesses.

### 158

शल्यराशिर्मांसलिप्तो मलपूर्णोऽतिकश्मलः ।  
कथं भवेदयं वेत्ता स्वयमेतद्विलक्षणः ॥ १५८ ॥

*śalyarāśirmāṃsalipto malapūrṇo'tikaśmalaḥ |  
kathaṃ bhavedayaṃ vettā svayametadvilakṣaṇaḥ || 158 ||*

How can the body, a bundle of bones wrapped in flesh, full of filth and extremely impure, be the self-existent Ātman, the Knower, which is always distinct from it?

### 159

त्वङ्मांसमेदोऽस्थिपुरीषराशा वहंमतिं मूढजनः करोति ।  
विलक्षणं वेत्ति विचारशीलो निजस्वरूपं परमार्थ भूतम् ॥ १५९ ॥

*tvaṅmāṃsamedo'sthipurīṣarāśā vahaṃmatiṃ mūḍhajanaḥ karoti |  
vilakṣaṇaṃ vetti vicāraśīlo nijasvarūpaṃ paramārtha bhūtam || 159 ||*

The foolish person identifies with a mass of skin, flesh, fat, bones, and filth. The person of discrimination knows his own Self, the only Reality there is, as distinct from the body.

### 160

देहोऽहमित्येव जडस्य बुद्धिः देहे च जीवे विदुषस्त्वहंधीः ।  
विवेकविज्ञानवतो महात्मनो ब्रह्माहमित्येव मतिः सदात्मनि ॥ १६0 ॥

*deho'hamityeva jaḍasya buddhiḥ dehe ca jīve viduṣastvahaṃdhīḥ |  
vivekavijñānavato mahātmano brahmāhamityeva matiḥ sadātmani || 160 ||*

The dull person believes he is the body. The book-learned person identifies with the mixture of body and soul. The sage, who has realization born of discrimination, regards the eternal Ātman as his Self and thinks, "I am Brahman."

### 161

अत्रात्मबुद्धिं त्यज मूढबुद्धे त्वङ्मांसमेदोऽस्थिपुरीषराशौ ।  
सर्वात्मनि ब्रह्मणि निर्विकल्पे कुरुष्व शान्ति परमां भजस्व ॥ १६१ ॥

*atrātmabuddhiṃ tyaja mūḍhabuddhe tvaṅmāṃsamedo'sthipurīṣarāśau |  
sarvātmani brahmaṇi nirvikalpe kuruṣva śānti paramāṃ bhajasva || 161 ||*

Foolish one, stop identifying with this bundle of skin, flesh, fat, bones, and filth. Identify instead with the Absolute Brahman, the Self of all, and so reach supreme Peace.

### 162

देहेन्द्रियादावसति भ्रमोदितां विद्वानहं तां न जहाति यावत् ।  
तावन्न तस्यास्ति विमुक्तिवार्ताप्य् अस्त्वेष वेदान्तनयान्तदर्शी ॥ १६२ ॥

*dehendriyādāvasati bhramoditāṃ vidvānahaṃ tāṃ na jahāti yāvat |  
tāvanna tasyāsti vimuktivārtāpy astveṣa vedāntanayāntadarśī || 162 ||*

So long as the book-learned person does not give up his mistaken identification with the body, the organs, and so on, which are unreal, there can be no talk of emancipation for him, however learned he may be in Vedanta philosophy.

### 163

छायाशरीरे प्रतिबिम्बगात्रे यत्स्वप्नदेहे हृदि कल्पिताङ्गे ।  
यथात्मबुद्धिस्तव नास्ति काचिज् जीवच्छरीरे च तथैव मास्तु ॥ १६३ ॥

*chāyāśarīre pratibimbagātre yatsvapnadehe hṛdi kalpitāṅge |  
yathātmabuddhistava nāsti kācij jīvaccharīre ca tathaiva māstu || 163 ||*

You do not identify with your shadow-body, your image-body, your dream-body, or the body you have in your heart's imaginings. Stop doing so with the living body as well.

### 164

देहात्मधीरेव नृणामसद्धियां जन्मादिदुःखप्रभवस्य बीजम् ।  
यतस्ततस्त्वं जहि तां प्रयत्नात् त्यक्ते तु चित्ते न पुनर्भवाशा ॥ १६४ ॥

*dehātmadhīreva nṛṇāmasaddhiyāṃ janmādiduḥkhaprabhavasya bījam |  
yatastatastvaṃ jahi tāṃ prayatnāt tyakte tu citte na punarbhavāśā || 164 ||*

Identification with the body is the sole root that gives rise to the misery of birth and so on for those attached to the unreal. So destroy it with the greatest care. Once this mind-made identification is given up, there is no further possibility of rebirth.

### 165

<vt>b) Prņamaya kośa (Vital air sheath)</vt>  
कर्मेन्द्रियैः पञ्चभिरञ्चितोऽयं प्राणो भवेत्प्राणमायास्तु कोशः ।  
येनात्मवानन्नमयोऽनुपूर्णः प्रवर्ततेऽसौ सकलक्रियासु ॥ १६५ ॥

*<vt>b) Prņamaya kośa (Vital air sheath)</vt>  
karmendriyaiḥ pañcabhirañcito'yaṃ prāṇo bhavetprāṇaMāyāstu kośaḥ |  
yenātmavānannamayo'nupūrṇaḥ pravartate'sau sakalakriyāsu || 165 ||*

The familiar Prāṇa, joined with the five organs of action, forms the vital sheath. Permeated by it, the material sheath takes part in all activities as if it were alive.

### 166

नैवात्मापि प्राणमयो वायुविकारो गन्तागन्ता वायुवदन्तर्बहिरेषः ।  
यस्मात्किंचित्क्वापि न वेत्तीष्टमनिष्टं स्वं वान्यं वा किंचन नित्यं परतन्त्रः ॥ १६६ ॥

*naivātmāpi prāṇamayo vāyuvikāro gantāgantā vāyuvadantarbahireṣaḥ |  
yasmātkiṃcitkvāpi na vettīṣṭamaniṣṭaṃ svaṃ vānyaṃ vā kiṃcana nityaṃ paratantraḥ || 166 ||*
