The vital sheath is not the Self either. It is a modification of Vayu, and like air it moves into and out of the body. It never has the least knowledge of its own good and ill or of anyone else's, being forever dependent on the Self.

### 167

<vt>c) Manomaya kosa (Mental sheath)</vt>  
ज्ञानेन्द्रियाणि च मनश्च मनोमायाः स्यात् कोशो ममाहमिति वस्तुविकल्पहेतुः ।  
संज्ञादिभेदकलनाकलितो बलीयांस् तत्पूर्वकोशमभिपूर्य विजृम्भते यः ॥ १६७ ॥

*<vt>c) Manomaya kosa (Mental sheath)</vt>  
jñānendriyāṇi ca manaśca manoMāyāḥ syāt kośo mamāhamiti vastuvikalpahetuḥ |  
saṃjñādibhedakalanākalito balīyāṃs tatpūrvakośamabhipūrya vijṛmbhate yaḥ || 167 ||*

The organs of knowledge together with the mind make up the mental sheath, which is the cause of the diversity of things such as "I" and "mine." It is powerful and has the ability to create differences of name and so on. It shows itself as permeating the preceding sheath, the vital sheath.

### 168

पञ्चेन्द्रियैः पञ्चभिरेव होतृभिः प्रचीयमानो विषयाज्यधारया ।  
जाज्वल्यमानो बहुवासनेन्धनैः मनोमयाग्निर्दहति प्रपञ्चम् ॥ १६८ ॥

*pañcendriyaiḥ pañcabhireva hotṛbhiḥ pracīyamāno viṣayājyadhārayā |  
jājvalyamāno bahuvāsanendhanaiḥ manomayāgnirdahati prapañcam || 168 ||*

The mental sheath is the (sacrificial) fire that produces this phenomenal universe. The five sense-organs, acting as priests, feed it with the fuel of many desires, and the objects of the senses, acting as the stream of oblations, set it ablaze.

### 169

न ह्यस्त्यविद्या मनसोऽतिरिक्ता मनो ह्यविद्या भवबन्धहेतुः ।  
तस्मिन्विनष्टे सकलं विनष्टं विजृम्भितेऽस्मिन्सकलं विजृम्भते ॥ १६९ ॥

*na hyastyavidyā manaso'tiriktā mano hyavidyā bhavabandhahetuḥ |  
tasminvinaṣṭe sakalaṃ vinaṣṭaṃ vijṛmbhite'sminsakalaṃ vijṛmbhate || 169 ||*

Ignorance (Avidya) does not exist outside the mind. The mind alone is Avidya, the cause of the bondage of transmigration. When the mind is destroyed, all else is destroyed; when it is manifest, everything else is manifest.

### 170

स्वप्नेऽर्थशून्ये सृजति स्वशक्त्या भोक्त्रादिविश्वं मन एव सर्वम् ।  
तथैव जाग्रत्यपि नो विशेषः तत्सर्वमेतन्मनसो विजृम्भणम् ॥ १७0 ॥

*svapne'rthaśūnye sṛjati svaśaktyā bhoktrādiviśvaṃ mana eva sarvam |  
tathaiva jāgratyapi no viśeṣaḥ tatsarvametanmanaso vijṛmbhaṇam || 170 ||*

In dreams, with no real contact with the outer world, the mind by itself creates a whole universe, including the experiencer and the rest. The waking state is no different. So all of this (phenomenal universe) is the mind's projection.

### 171

सुषुप्तिकाले मनसि प्रलीने नैवास्ति किंचित्सकलप्रसिद्धेः ।  
अतो मनःकल्पितेव पुंसः संसार एतस्य न वस्तुतोऽस्ति ॥ १७१ ॥

*suṣuptikāle manasi pralīne naivāsti kiṃcitsakalaprasiddheḥ |  
ato manaḥkalpiteva puṃsaḥ saṃsāra etasya na vastuto'sti || 171 ||*

In dreamless sleep, when the mind has been reduced to its causal state, nothing exists (for the sleeper), as everyone's experience shows. So a person's relative existence is merely the creation of his mind and has no objective reality.

### 172

वायुनानीयते मेधः पुनस्तेनैव नीयते ।  
मनसा कल्प्यते बन्धो मोक्षस्तेनैव कल्प्यते ॥ १७२ ॥

*vāyunānīyate medhaḥ punastenaiva nīyate |  
manasā kalpyate bandho mokṣastenaiva kalpyate || 172 ||*

The wind brings the clouds in, and the same wind drives them away. In the same way the mind causes a person's bondage, and the mind alone causes Liberation too.

### 173

देहादिसर्वविषये परिकल्प्य रागं बध्नाति तेन पुरुषं पशुवद्गुणेन ।  
वैरस्यमत्र विषवत्सुवुधाय पश्चाद् एनं विमोचयति तन्मन एव बन्धात् ॥ १७३ ॥

*dehādisarvaviṣaye parikalpya rāgaṃ badhnāti tena puruṣaṃ paśuvadguṇena |  
vairasyamatra viṣavatsuvudhāya paścād enaṃ vimocayati tanmana eva bandhāt || 173 ||*

(First) it creates in a person an attachment to the body and to all other objects of the senses, and through that attachment it ties him up, like an animal with ropes. Later, that very mind creates in him a complete distaste for these objects, as if they were poison, and frees him from bondage.

### 174

तस्मान्मनः कारणमस्य जन्तोः बन्धस्य मोक्षस्य च वा विधाने ।  
बन्धस्य हेतुर्मलिनं रजोगुणैः मोक्षस्य शुद्धं विरजस्तमस्कम् ॥ १७४ ॥

*tasmānmanaḥ kāraṇamasya jantoḥ bandhasya mokṣasya ca vā vidhāne |  
bandhasya heturmalinaṃ rajoguṇaiḥ mokṣasya śuddhaṃ virajastamaskam || 174 ||*

So the mind is the sole cause of a person's bondage or Liberation. When stained by the effects of Rajas it leads to bondage; when pure and stripped of Rajas and Tamas it leads to Liberation.

### 175

विवेकवैराग्यगुणातिरेकाच् छुद्धत्वमासाद्य मनो विमुक्त्यै ।  
भवत्यतो बुद्धिमतो मुमुक्षोस् ताभ्यां दृढाभ्यां भवितव्यमग्रे ॥ १७५ ॥

*vivekavairāgyaguṇātirekāc chuddhatvamāsādya mano vimuktyai |  
bhavatyato buddhimato mumukṣos tābhyāṃ dṛḍhābhyāṃ bhavitavyamagre || 175 ||*

Made pure by a strong measure of discrimination and renunciation, the mind leads to Liberation. So the wise seeker of Liberation must first strengthen these two.

### 176

मनो नाम महाव्याघ्रो विषयारण्यभूमिषु ।  
चरत्यत्र न गच्छन्तु साधवो ये मुमुक्षवः ॥ १७६ ॥

*mano nāma mahāvyāghro viṣayāraṇyabhūmiṣu |  
caratyatra na gacchantu sādhavo ye mumukṣavaḥ || 176 ||*

A huge tiger called the mind prowls the forest of sense-pleasures. Let good people who long for Liberation never go there.

### 177

मनः प्रसूते विषयानशेषान् स्थूलात्मना सूक्ष्मतया च भोक्तुः ।  
शरीरवर्णाश्रमजातिभेदान् गुणक्रियाहेतुफलानि नित्यम् ॥ १७७ ॥

*manaḥ prasūte viṣayānaśeṣān sthūlātmanā sūkṣmatayā ca bhoktuḥ |  
śarīravarṇāśramajātibhedān guṇakriyāhetuphalāni nityam || 177 ||*

The mind continually produces for the experiencer every object of the senses without exception, whether perceived as gross or subtle, along with the differences of body, caste, order of life, and tribe, and the varieties of qualification, action, means, and results.

### 178

असङ्गचिद्रूपममुं विमोह्य देहेन्द्रियप्राणगुणैर्निबद्ध्य ।  
अहंममेति भ्रमायात्यजस्रं मनः स्वकृत्येषु फलोपभुक्तिषु ॥ १७८ ॥

*asaṅgacidrūpamamuṃ vimohya dehendriyaprāṇaguṇairnibaddhya |  
ahaṃmameti bhraMāyātyajasraṃ manaḥ svakṛtyeṣu phalopabhuktiṣu || 178 ||*

The mind deludes the Jīva, which is unattached Pure Intelligence, and binds it with the ties of body, organs, and Prāṇas. With ideas of "I" and "mine," it makes the Jīva wander among the many enjoyments of results it has brought about itself.

### 179

अध्यासदोषात्पुरुषस्य संसृतिः अध्यासबन्धस्त्वमुनैव कल्पितः ।  
रजस्तमोदोषवतोऽविवेकिनो जन्मादिदुःखस्य निदानमेतत् ॥ १७९ ॥

*adhyāsadoṣātpuruṣasya saṃsṛtiḥ adhyāsabandhastvamunaiva kalpitaḥ |  
rajastamodoṣavato'vivekino janmādiduḥkhasya nidānametat || 179 ||*

A person's transmigration comes from the evil of superimposition, and the bondage of superimposition is created by the mind alone. This is what brings the misery of birth and so on to the person without discrimination, stained by Rajas and Tamas.

### 180

अतः प्राहुर्मनोऽविद्यां पण्डितास्तत्त्वदर्शिनः ।  
येनैव भ्राम्यते विश्वं वायुनेवाभ्रमण्डलम् ॥ १८0 ॥

*ataḥ prāhurmano'vidyāṃ paṇḍitāstattvadarśinaḥ |  
yenaiva bhrāmyate viśvaṃ vāyunevābhramaṇḍalam || 180 ||*

That is why sages who have grasped its secret have called the mind Avidya, or ignorance. By it alone the universe is pushed back and forth, like masses of cloud by the wind.

### 181

तन्मनःशोधनं कार्यं प्रयत्नेन मुमुक्षुणा ।  
विशुद्धे सति चैतस्मिन्मुक्तिः करफलायते ॥ १८१ ॥

*tanmanaḥśodhanaṃ kāryaṃ prayatnena mumukṣuṇā |  
viśuddhe sati caitasminmuktiḥ karaphalāyate || 181 ||*

So anyone seeking Liberation must take care to purify the mind. Once it is pure, Liberation is as easy to reach as a fruit lying in one's palm.

### 182

मोक्षैकसक्त्या विषयेषु रागं निर्मूल्य संन्यस्य च सर्वकर्म ।  
सच्छ्रद्धया यः श्रवणादिनिष्ठो रजःस्वभावं स धुनोति बुद्धेः ॥ १८२ ॥

*mokṣaikasaktyā viṣayeṣu rāgaṃ nirmūlya saṃnyasya ca sarvakarma |  
sacchraddhayā yaḥ śravaṇādiniṣṭho rajaḥsvabhāvaṃ sa dhunoti buddheḥ || 182 ||*

A person who, through one-pointed devotion to Liberation, tears out attachment to sense-objects, gives up all actions, and with faith in the Real Brahman regularly practises hearing and the rest, succeeds in cleansing the intellect of its Rājasika nature.

### 183

मनोमयो नापि भवेत्परात्मा ह्याद्यन्तवत्त्वात्परिणामिभावात् ।  
दुःखात्मकत्वाद्विषयत्वहेतोः द्रष्टा हि दृश्यात्मतया न दृष्टः ॥ १८३ ॥

*manomayo nāpi bhavetparātmā hyādyantavattvātpariṇāmibhāvāt |  
duḥkhātmakatvādviṣayatvahetoḥ draṣṭā hi dṛśyātmatayā na dṛṣṭaḥ || 183 ||*

The mental sheath cannot be the Supreme Self either. It has a beginning and an end, it undergoes change, it is marked by pain and suffering, and it is an object. The subject can never be identified with the things it knows.

### 184

<vt>d) Vijnanamaya kośa (Intellectual sheath)</vt>  
बुद्धिर्बुद्धीन्द्रियैः सार्धं सवृत्तिः कर्तृलक्षणः ।  
विज्ञानमायाकोशः स्यात्पुंसः संसारकारणम् ॥ १८४ ॥

*<vt>d) Vijnanamaya kośa (Intellectual sheath)</vt>  
buddhirbuddhīndriyaiḥ sārdhaṃ savṛttiḥ kartṛlakṣaṇaḥ |  
vijñānaMāyākośaḥ syātpuṃsaḥ saṃsārakāraṇam || 184 ||*

The Buddhi, together with its modifications and the organs of knowledge, makes up the Vijñānamāyā Kośa, the knowledge sheath. It has the character of an agent, and it is what causes a person's transmigration.

### 185

अनुव्रजच्चित्प्रतिबिम्बशक्तिः विज्ञानसंज्ञः प्रकृतेर्विकारः ।  
ज्ञानक्रियावानहमित्यजस्रं देहेन्द्रियादिष्वभिमन्यते भृशम् ॥ १८५ ॥

*anuvrajaccitpratibimbaśaktiḥ vijñānasaṃjñaḥ prakṛtervikāraḥ |  
jñānakriyāvānahamityajasraṃ dehendriyādiṣvabhimanyate bhṛśam || 185 ||*

This knowledge sheath, which a reflection of the power of the Chit seems to accompany, is a modification of Prakriti. It has the function of knowing, and it always identifies itself completely with the body, the organs, and so on.

### 186

अनादिकालोऽयमहंस्वभावो जीवः समस्तव्यवहारवोढा ।  
करोति कर्माण्यपि पूर्ववासनः पुण्यान्यपुण्यानि च तत्फलानि ॥ १८६ ॥

*anādikālo'yamahaṃsvabhāvo jīvaḥ samastavyavahāravoḍhā |  
karoti karmāṇyapi pūrvavāsanaḥ puṇyānyapuṇyāni ca tatphalāni || 186 ||*

It has no beginning, it is marked by egoism, it is called the Jiva, and it carries out all activity on the relative plane. Driven by past desires, it does good and evil deeds and tastes their results. Born into one body after another, it comes and goes, rising and falling. It is this knowledge sheath that passes through waking, dream, and the other states, and that feels joy and grief.

### 187

भुङ्क्ते विचित्रास्वपि योनिषु व्रजन् नायाति निर्यात्यध ऊर्ध्वमेषः ।  
अस्यैव विज्ञानमायास्य जाग्रत् स्वप्नाद्यवस्थाः सुखदुःखभोगः ॥ १८७ ॥

*bhuṅkte vicitrāsvapi yoniṣu vrajan nāyāti niryātyatha ūrdhvameṣaḥ |  
asyaiva vijñānamāyāsya jāgrat svapnādyavasthāḥ sukhaduḥkhabhogaḥ || 187 ||*

It has no beginning, it is marked by egoism, it is called the Jiva, and it carries out all activity on the relative plane. Driven by past desires, it does good and evil deeds and tastes their results. Born into one body after another, it comes and goes, rising and falling. It is this knowledge sheath that passes through waking, dream, and the other states, and that feels joy and grief.

### 188

देहादिनिष्ठाश्रमधर्मकर्म गुणाभिमानः सततं ममेति ।  
विज्ञानकोशोऽयमतिप्रकाशः प्रकृष्टसान्निध्यवशात्परात्मनः ।  
अतो भवत्येष उपाधिरस्य यदात्मधीः संसरति भ्रमेण ॥ १८८ ॥

*dehādiniṣṭhāśramadharmakarma guṇābhimānaḥ satataṃ mameti |  
vijñānakośo'yamatiprakāśaḥ prakṛṣṭasānnidhyavaśātparātmanaḥ |  
ato bhavatyeṣa upādhirasya yadātmadhīḥ saṃsarati bhrameṇa || 188 ||*

It constantly takes the duties, functions, and attributes of the orders of life, which belong to the body, to be its own. Because it lies so close to the Supreme Self, the knowledge sheath shines with great brilliance; and the Self, identifying with it through delusion, undergoes transmigration. The sheath is therefore a superimposition on the Self.

### 189

<vt>Ātman - Unattached</vt>  
योऽयं विज्ञानमायाः प्राणेषु हृदि स्फुरत्ययं ज्योतिः ।  
कूटस्थः सन्नात्मा कर्ता भोक्ता भवत्युपाधिस्थः ॥ १८९ ॥

*<vt>Ātman - Unattached</vt>  
yo'yaṃ vijñānaMāyāḥ prāṇeṣu hṛdi sphuratyayaṃ jyotiḥ |  
kūṭasthaḥ sannātmā kartā bhoktā bhavatyupādhisthaḥ || 189 ||*

The self-luminous Ātman, which is Pure Knowledge, shines among the Prāṇas, within the heart. It never changes, yet because of the knowledge sheath superimposed on It, It becomes the doer and the experiencer.

### 190

स्वयं परिच्छेदमुपेत्य बुद्धेः तादात्म्यदोषेण परं मृषात्मनः ।  
सर्वात्मकः सन्नपि वीक्षते स्वयं स्वतः पृथक्त्वेन मृदो घटानिव ॥ १९0 ॥

*svayaṃ paricchedamupetya buddheḥ tādātmyadoṣeṇa paraṃ mṛṣātmanaḥ |  
sarvātmakaḥ sannapi vīkṣate svayaṃ svataḥ pṛthaktvena mṛdo ghaṭāniva || 190 ||*

This Ātman is the Self of all that exists. Yet by taking on the limits of the Buddhi and wrongly identifying with that wholly unreal thing, It regards Itself as something different, just as earthen jars might be thought different from the clay that forms them.

### 191

उपाधिसंबन्धवशात्परात्मा ह्युपाधिधर्माननुभाति तद्गुणः ।  
अयोविकारानविकारिवन्हिवत् सदैकरूपोऽपि परः स्वभावात् ॥ १९१ ॥

*upādhisaṃbandhavaśātparātmā hyupādhidharmānanubhāti tadguṇaḥ |  
ayovikārānavikārivanhivat sadaikarūpo'pi paraḥ svabhāvāt || 191 ||*

Through Its connection with the superimpositions, the Supreme Self, though perfect by nature (transcending Nature) and forever unchanging, takes on their qualities and appears to act just as they do. In the same way, changeless fire takes on the shapes of the iron it makes red-hot.

### 192

<vt>What is Liberation? - Disciple</vt>  
शिष्य उवाच भ्रमेणाप्यन्यथा वास्तु जीवभावः परात्मनः ।  
तदुपाधेरनादित्वान्नानादेर्नाश इष्यते ॥ १९२ ॥

*<vt>What is Liberation? - Disciple</vt>  
śiṣya uvāca bhrameṇāpyanyathā vāstu jīvabhāvaḥ parātmanaḥ |  
tadupādheranāditvānnānādernāśa iṣyate || 192 ||*

The disciple asked: Whether by delusion or by some other cause, the Supreme Self has come to regard Itself as the Jīva. This superimposition has no beginning, and what has no beginning cannot be supposed to have an end either.

### 193

अतोऽस्य जीवभावोऽपि नित्या भवति संसृतिः ।  
न निवर्तेत तन्मोक्षः कथं मे श्रीगुरो वद ॥ १९३ ॥

*ato'sya jīvabhāvo'pi nityā bhavati saṃsṛtiḥ |  
na nivarteta tanmokṣaḥ kathaṃ me śrīguro vada || 193 ||*

So the soul's state as a Jīva must also be endless, and its transmigration must go on forever. How then can the soul ever be liberated? Please make this clear to me, revered Master.

### 194

<vt>Self-Knowledge gives Liberation</vt>  
श्रीगुरुरुवाच सम्यक्पृष्टं त्वया विद्वन्सावधानेन तच्छृणु ।  
प्रामाणिकी न भवति भ्रान्त्या मोहितकल्पना ॥ १९४ ॥

*<vt>Self-Knowledge gives Liberation</vt>  
śrīgururuvāca samyakpṛṣṭaṃ tvayā vidvansāvadhānena tacchṛṇu |  
prāmāṇikī na bhavati bhrāntyā mohitakalpanā || 194 ||*

The Teacher said: Your question is a good one, learned man. So listen closely. What has been conjured up by delusion in the imagination can never be accepted as fact.

### 195

भ्रान्तिं विना त्वसङ्गस्य निष्क्रियस्य निराकृतेः ।  
न घटेतार्थसंबन्धो नभसो नीलतादिवत् ॥ १९५ ॥

*bhrāntiṃ vinā tvasaṅgasya niṣkriyasya nirākṛteḥ |  
na ghaṭetārthasaṃbandho nabhaso nīlatādivat || 195 ||*

Apart from delusion, the Self, which is unattached, free of activity, and formless, can have no connection with the objective world, any more than the sky has any real connection with blueness and the like.

### 196

स्वस्य द्रष्टुर्निर्गुणस्याक्रियस्य प्रत्यग्बोधानन्दरूपस्य बुद्धेः ।  
भ्रान्त्या प्राप्तो जीवभावो न सत्यो मोहापाये नास्त्यवस्तुस्वभावात् ॥ १९६ ॥

*svasya draṣṭurnirguṇasyākriyasya pratyagbodhānandarūpasya buddheḥ |  
bhrāntyā prāpto jīvabhāvo na satyo mohāpāye nāstyavastusvabhāvāt || 196 ||*

The Ātman is the Witness, beyond qualities and beyond activity, and It is realized within as Knowledge and Bliss Absolute. Its state as a Jīva has been superimposed by the delusion of the Buddhi and is not real. Being unreal by nature, that state ceases to exist once the delusion is gone.

### 197

यावद्भ्रान्तिस्तावदेवास्य सत्ता मिथ्याज्ञानोज्जृम्भितस्य प्रमादात् ।  
रज्ज्वां सर्पो भ्रान्तिकालीन एव भ्रान्तेर्नाशे नैव सर्पोऽपि तद्वत् ॥ १९७ ॥

*yāvadbhrāntistāvadevāsya sattā mithyājñānojjṛmbhitasya pramādāt |  
rajjvāṃ sarpo bhrāntikālīna eva bhrānternāśe naiva sarpo'pi tadvat || 197 ||*

It lasts only as long as the delusion lasts, since it arises from a lack of discrimination caused by illusion. A rope is taken for a snake only while the mistake continues; when the illusion vanishes, there is no snake any more. So it is here.

### 198

अनादित्वमविद्यायाः कार्यस्यापि तथेष्यते ।  
उत्पन्नायां तु विद्यायामाविद्यकमनाद्यपि ॥ १९८ ॥

*anāditvamavidy āyāḥ kāryasyāpi tatheṣyate |  
utpannāyāṃ tu vidyāyāmāvidyakamanādyapi || 198 ||*

Avidya, or Nescience, and its effects are also regarded as beginningless. But when Vidyā, or realization, dawns, all the effects of Avidya, beginningless though they are, are destroyed along with their root, as dreams are on waking from sleep. Clearly the phenomenal universe, even though it has no beginning, is not eternal; it is like previous non-existence.

### 199

प्रबोधे स्वप्नवत्सर्वं सहमूलं विनश्यति ।  
अनाद्यपीदं नो नित्यं प्रागभाव इव स्फुटम् ॥ १९९ ॥

*prabodhe svapnavatsarvaṃ sahamūlaṃ vinaśyati |  
anādyapīdaṃ no nityaṃ prāgabhāva iva sphuṭam || 199 ||*

Avidya, or Nescience, and its effects are also regarded as beginningless. But when Vidyā, or realization, dawns, all the effects of Avidya, beginningless though they are, are destroyed along with their root, as dreams are on waking from sleep. Clearly the phenomenal universe, even though it has no beginning, is not eternal; it is like previous non-existence.

### 200

अनादेरपि विध्वंसः प्रागभावस्य वीक्षितः ।  
यद्बुद्ध्युपाधिसंबन्धात्परिकल्पितमात्मनि ॥ २00 ॥

*anāderapi vidhvaṃsaḥ prāgabhāvasya vīkṣitaḥ |  
yadbuddhyupādhisaṃbandhātparikalpitamātmani || 200 ||*

Previous non-existence has no beginning, yet it is seen to come to an end. In the same way, the Jīvahood imagined in the Ātman through its relation to superimposed attributes such as the Buddhi is not real, while the Ātman is essentially different from it. The relation between the Ātman and the Buddhi comes from false knowledge.

### 201

जीवत्वं न ततोऽन्यस्तु स्वरूपेण विलक्षणः ।  
संबन्धस्त्वात्मनो बुद्ध्या मिथ्याज्ञानपुरःसरः ॥ २0१ ॥

*jīvatvaṃ na tato'nyastu svarūpeṇa vilakṣaṇaḥ |  
saṃbandhastvaatmano buddhyā mithyājñānapuraḥsaraḥ || 201 ||*

Previous non-existence has no beginning, yet it is seen to come to an end. In the same way, the Jīvahood imagined in the Ātman through its relation to superimposed attributes such as the Buddhi is not real, while the Ātman is essentially different from it. The relation between the Ātman and the Buddhi comes from false knowledge.

### 202

विनिवृत्तिर्भवेत्तस्य सम्यग्ज्ञानेन नान्यथा ।  
ब्रह्मात्मैकत्वविज्ञानं सम्यग्ज्ञानं श्रुतेर्मतम् ॥ २0२ ॥

*vinivṛttirbhavettasya samyagjñānena nānyathā |  
brahmātmaikatvavijñānaṃ samyagjñānaṃ śrutermatam || 202 ||*

That superimposition ends through perfect knowledge and in no other way. According to the Śrutis, perfect knowledge is the realization that the individual soul and Brahman are one.

### 203

तदात्मानात्मनोः सम्यग्विवेकेनैव सिध्यति ।  
ततो विवेकः कर्तव्यः प्रत्यगात्मसदात्मनोः ॥ २0३ ॥

*tadātmānātmanoḥ samyagvivekenaiva sidhyati |  
tato vivekaḥ kartavyaḥ pratyagātmasadātmanoḥ || 203 ||*

This realization comes through perfect discrimination between the Self and the non-Self. One must therefore strive to discriminate between the individual soul and the eternal Self.

### 204

जलं पङ्कवदत्यन्तं पङ्कापाये जलं स्फुटम् ।  
यथा भाति तथात्मापि दोषाभावे स्फुटप्रभः ॥ २0४ ॥

*jalaṃ paṅkavadatyantaṃ paṅkāpāye jalaṃ sphuṭam |  
yathā bhāti tathātmāpi doṣābhāve sphuṭaprabhaḥ || 204 ||*

Very muddy water becomes clear again once the mud is taken away. In the same way, the Ātman shows Its undimmed radiance once the taint has been removed.

### 205

असन्निवृत्तौ तु सदात्मना स्फुटं प्रतीतिरेतस्य भवेत्प्रतीचः ।  
ततो निरासः करणीय एव सदात्मनः साध्वहमादिवस्तुनः ॥ २0५ ॥

*asannivṛttau tu sadātmanā sphuṭaṃ pratītiretasya bhavetpratīcaḥ |  
tato nirāsaḥ karaṇīya eva sadātmanaḥ sādhvahamādivastunaḥ || 205 ||*

When the unreal ceases to exist, this very individual soul is known with certainty to be the eternal Self. One must therefore make a point of removing egoism and all such things entirely from the eternal Self.

### 206

अतो नायं परात्मा स्याद्विज्ञानमायाशब्दभाक् ।  
विकारित्वाज्जडत्वाच्च परिच्छिन्नत्वहेतुतः ।  
दृश्यत्वाद्व्यभिचारित्वान्नानित्यो नित्य इष्यते ॥ २0६ ॥

*ato nāyaṃ parātmā syādvijñānaMāyāśabdabhāk |  
vikāritvājjaḍatvācca paricchinnatvahetutaḥ |  
dṛśyatvādvyabhicāritvānnānityo nitya iṣyate || 206 ||*

The knowledge sheath (Vijñānamāyā Kośa) we have been discussing cannot be the Supreme Self, for these reasons: it changes, it is insentient, it is limited, it is an object of the senses, and it is not always present. Something unreal cannot be taken for the real Ātman.

### 207

<vt>Anandamaya kośa (Bliss sheath)</vt>  
आनन्दप्रतिबिम्बचुम्बिततनुर्वृत्तिस्तमोजृम्भिता स्यादानन्दमायाः प्रियादिगुणकः स्वेष्टार्थलाभोदयः ।  
पुण्यस्यानुभवे विभाति कृतिनामानन्दरूपः स्वयं सर्वो नन्दति यत्र साधु तनुभृन्मात्रः प्रयत्नं विना ॥ २0७ ॥

*<vt>Anandamaya kośa (Bliss sheath)</vt>  
ānandapratibimbacumbitatanurvṛttistamojṛmbhitā syādānandaMāyāḥ priyādiguṇakaḥ sveṣṭārthalābhodayaḥ |  
puṇyasyānubhave vibhāti kṛtināmānandarūpaḥ svayaṃ sarvo nandati yatra sādhu tanubhṛnmātraḥ prayatnaṃ vinā || 207 ||*

The blissful sheath (Ānandamāyā Kośa) is the modification of Nescience that appears by catching a reflection of the Ātman, which is Bliss Absolute. Its attributes are pleasure and the like, and it comes into view when something agreeable presents itself. The fortunate feel it arise on its own when their virtuous deeds bear fruit, and from it every embodied being draws great joy without the least effort.

### 208

आनन्दमायाकोशस्य सुषुप्तौ स्फूर्तिरुत्कटा ।  
स्वप्नजागरयोरीषदिष्टसंदर्शनाविना ॥ २0८ ॥

*ānandaMāyākośasya suṣuptau sphūrtirutkaṭā |  
svapnajāgarayorīṣadiṣṭasaṃdarśanāvinā || 208 ||*

The blissful sheath comes into its fullest play in deep sleep. In the dream and waking states it shows itself only in part, prompted by the sight of pleasant objects and the like.

### 209

नैवायमानन्दमायाः परात्मा सोपाधिकत्वात्प्रकृतेर्विकारात् ।  
कार्यत्वहेतोः सुकृतक्रियाया विकारसङ्घातसमाहितत्वात् ॥ २0९ ॥

*naivāyamānandaMāyāḥ parātmā sopādhikatvātprakṛtervikārāt |  
kāryatvahetoḥ sukṛtakriyāyā vikārasaṅghātasamāhitatvāt || 209 ||*

The blissful sheath is not the Supreme Self either, since it has changing attributes, is a modification of Prakriti, results from past good deeds, and lies embedded in the other sheaths, which are themselves modifications.

### 210

पञ्चानामपि कोशानां निषेधे युक्तितः श्रुतेः ।  
तन्निषेधावधि साक्षी बोधरूपोऽवशिष्यते ॥ २१0 ॥

*pañcānāmapi kośānāṃ niṣedhe yuktitaḥ śruteḥ |  
tanniṣedhāvadhi sākṣī bodharūpo'vaśiṣyate || 210 ||*

When all five sheaths have been set aside through reasoning on the Shruti passages, what remains at the culmination of the process is the Witness, Knowledge Absolute: the Ātman.

### 211

<vt>Ātman - Other than the Five Kośas</vt>  
योऽयमात्मा स्वयंज्योतिः पञ्चकोशविलक्षणः ।  
अवस्थात्रयसाक्षी सन्निर्विकारो निरञ्जनः सदानन्दः स विज्ञेयः स्वात्मत्वेन विपश्चिता ॥ २११ ॥

*<vt>Ātman - Other than the Five Kośas</vt>  
yo'yamātmā svayaṃjyotiḥ pañcakośavilakṣaṇaḥ |  
avasthātrayasākṣī sannirvikāro nirañjanaḥ sadānandaḥ sa vijñeyaḥ svātmatvena vipaścitā || 211 ||*

This self-luminous Ātman stands apart from the five sheaths. It is the Witness of the three states, the Real, the Changeless, the Untainted, everlasting Bliss. The wise person should realize It as his own Self.

### 212

<vt>What is Ātman? - Disciple</vt>  
शिष्य उवाच मिथ्यात्वेन निषिद्धेषु कोशेष्वेतेषु पञ्चसु ।  
सर्वाभावं विना किंचिन्न पश्याम्यत्र हे गुरो विज्ञेयं किमु वस्त्वस्ति स्वात्मनात्मविपश्चिता ॥ २१२ ॥

*<vt>What is Ātman? - Disciple</vt>  
śiṣya uvāca mithyātvena niṣiddheṣu kośeṣveteṣu pañcasu |  
sarvābhāvaṃ vinā kiṃcinna paśyāmyatra he guro vijñeyaṃ kimu vastvasti svātmanātmavipaścitā || 212 ||*

The disciple asked: Once these five sheaths have been set aside as unreal, Master, I find nothing in this universe but a Void, the absence of everything. What entity, then, is left for the wise knower of the Self to realize his identity with?

### 213

<vt>Nature of the Self - Discussion</vt>  
श्रीगुरुरुवाच सत्यमुक्तं त्वया विदन्निपुणोऽसि विचारणे ।  
अहमादिविकारास्ते तदभावोऽयमप्यनु ॥ २१३ ॥

*<vt>Nature of the Self - Discussion</vt>  
śrīgururuvāca satyamuktaṃ tvayā vidannipuṇo'si vicāraṇe |  
ahamādivikārāste tadabhāvo'yamapyanu || 213 ||*

The Guru answered: You have spoken rightly, learned man, and you are skilled indeed in discrimination. That by which all these modifications such as egoism, and also their later absence (in deep sleep), are perceived, but which is not Itself perceived: know that to be the Ātman, the Knower, by means of the sharpest intellect.

### 214

सर्वे येनानुभूयन्ते यः स्वयं नानुभूयते ।  
तमात्मानं वेदितारं विद्दि बुद्ध्या सुसूक्ष्मया ॥ २१४ ॥

*sarve yenānubhūyante yaḥ svayaṃ nānubhūyate |  
tamātmānaṃ veditāraṃ viddhi buddhyā susūkṣmayā || 214 ||*

The Guru answered: You have spoken rightly, learned man, and you are skilled indeed in discrimination. That by which all these modifications such as egoism, and also their later absence (in deep sleep), are perceived, but which is not Itself perceived: know that to be the Ātman, the Knower, by means of the sharpest intellect.

### 215

तत्साक्षिकं भवेत्तत्तद्यद्यद्येनानुभूयते ।  
कस्याप्यननुभूतार्थे साक्षित्वं नोपयुज्यते ॥ २१५ ॥

*tatsākṣikaṃ bhavettattadyadyadyenānubhūyate |  
kasyāpyananubhūtārthe sākṣitvaṃ nopayujyate || 215 ||*

Whatever is perceived by something else has that other thing as its witness. Where no agent exists to perceive a thing, we cannot say it has been perceived at all.

### 216

असौ स्वसाक्षिको भावो यतः स्वेनानुभूयते ।  
अतः परं स्वयं साक्षात्प्रत्यगात्मा न चेतरः ॥ २१६ ॥

*asau svasākṣiko bhāvo yataḥ svenānubhūyate |  
ataḥ paraṃ svayaṃ sākṣātpratyagātmā na cetaraḥ || 216 ||*

This Ātman is known by Itself, and so It is self-cognized. The individual soul is therefore itself, directly, the Supreme Brahman and nothing else.

### 217

जाग्रत्स्वप्नसुषुप्तिषु स्फुटतरं योऽसौ समुज्जृम्भते प्रत्यग्रूपतया सदाहमहमित्यन्तः स्फुरन्नैकधा ।  
नानाकारविकारभागिन इमान् पश्यन्नहंधीमुखान् नित्यानन्दचिदात्मना स्फुरति तं विद्धि स्वमेतं हृदि ॥ २१७ ॥

*jāgratsvapnasuṣuptiṣu sphuṭataraṃ yo'sau samujjṛmbhate pratyagrūpatayā sadāhamahamityantaḥ sphurannaikadhā |  
nānākāravikārabhāgina imān paśyannahaṃdhīmukhān nityānandacidātmanā sphurati taṃ viddhi svametaṃ hṛdi || 217 ||*

It shows Itself clearly in waking, dream, and deep sleep. It is felt inwardly in the mind, in many forms, as an unbroken series of egoistic impressions. It witnesses the ego, the Buddhi, and the rest, with their varied forms and modifications. It makes Itself known as Existence-Knowledge-Bliss Absolute. Know this Ātman, your own Self, within your heart.

### 218

घटोदके बिम्बितमर्कबिम्बम् आलोक्य मूढो रविमेव मन्यते ।  
तथा चिदाभासमुपाधिसंस्थं भ्रान्त्याहमित्येव जडोऽभिमन्यते ॥ २१८ ॥

*ghaṭodake bimbitamarkabimbam ālokya mūḍho ravimeva manyate |  
tathā cidābhāsamupādhisaṃsthaṃ bhrāntyāhamityeva jaḍo'bhimanyate || 218 ||*

On seeing the sun's reflection in the water of a jar, a fool takes it for the sun itself. In the same way the dull person, through delusion, identifies himself with the reflection of the Chit caught in the Buddhi, which is Its superimposition.

### 219

घटं जलं तद्गतमर्कबिम्बं विहाय सर्वं विनिरीक्ष्यतेऽर्कः ।  
तटस्थ एतत्त्रितयावभासकः स्वयंप्रकाशो विदुषा यथा तथा ॥ २१९ ॥

*ghaṭaṃ jalaṃ tadgatamarkabimbaṃ vihāya sarvaṃ vinirīkṣyate'rkaḥ |  
taṭastha etattritayāvabhāsakaḥ svayaṃprakāśo viduṣā yathā tathā || 219 ||*

The wise person sets aside the jar, the water, and the reflection of the sun in it, and sees the self-luminous sun that lights all three and does not depend on them.

### 220

देहं धियं चित्प्रतिबिम्बमेवं विसृज्य बुद्धौ निहितं गुहायाम् ।  
द्रष्टारमात्मानमखण्डबोधं सर्वप्रकाशं सदसद्विलक्षणम् ॥ २२0 ॥

*dehaṃ dhiyaṃ citpratibimbamevaṃ visṛjya buddhau nihitaṃ guhāyām |  
draṣṭāramātmānamakhaṇḍabodhaṃ sarvaprakāśaṃ sadasadvilakṣaṇam || 220 ||*

In the same way, one sets aside the body, the Buddhi, and the reflection of the Chit in it, and realizes the Witness, the Self, Knowledge Absolute, the cause by which everything is manifested. It is hidden in the depths of the Buddhi, distinct from gross and subtle, eternal, omnipresent, all-pervading, and supremely subtle. It has no inside or outside and is one with oneself. Fully realizing this true nature of one's own, a person becomes free of sin, taint, death, and grief, and becomes the very embodiment of Bliss. Illumined, he fears no one. A seeker of Liberation has no other way to break the bonds of transmigration than realizing the truth of his own Self.

### 221

नित्यं विभुं सर्वगतं सुसूक्ष्मं अन्तर्बहिःशून्यमनन्यमात्मनः ।  
विज्ञाय सम्यङ्निजरूपमेतत् पुमान् विपाप्मा विरजो विमृत्युः ॥ २२१ ॥

*nityaṃ vibhuṃ sarvagataṃ susūkṣmaṃ antarbahiḥśūnyamananayamātmanaḥ |  
vijñāya samyaṅnijarūpametat pumān vipāpmā virajo vimṛtyuḥ || 221 ||*

In the same way, one sets aside the body, the Buddhi, and the reflection of the Chit in it, and realizes the Witness, the Self, Knowledge Absolute, the cause by which everything is manifested. It is hidden in the depths of the Buddhi, distinct from gross and subtle, eternal, omnipresent, all-pervading, and supremely subtle. It has no inside or outside and is one with oneself. Fully realizing this true nature of one's own, a person becomes free of sin, taint, death, and grief, and becomes the very embodiment of Bliss. Illumined, he fears no one. A seeker of Liberation has no other way to break the bonds of transmigration than realizing the truth of his own Self.

### 222

विशोक आनन्दघनो विपश्चित्  
स्वयं कुतश्चिन्न बिभेति कश्चित् ।  
नान्योऽस्ति पन्था भवबन्धमुक्तेः  
विना स्वतत्त्वावगमं मुमुक्षोः ॥ २२२ ॥

*viśoka ānandaghano vipaścit  
svayaṃ kutaścinna bibheti kaścit |  
nānyo'sti panthā bhavabandhamukteḥ  
vinā svatattvāvagamaṃ mumukṣoḥ || 222 ||*

In the same way, one sets aside the body, the Buddhi, and the reflection of the Chit in it, and realizes the Witness, the Self, Knowledge Absolute, the cause by which everything is manifested. It is hidden in the depths of the Buddhi, distinct from gross and subtle, eternal, omnipresent, all-pervading, and supremely subtle. It has no inside or outside and is one with oneself. Fully realizing this true nature of one's own, a person becomes free of sin, taint, death, and grief, and becomes the very embodiment of Bliss. Illumined, he fears no one. A seeker of Liberation has no other way to break the bonds of transmigration than realizing the truth of his own Self.

### 223

ब्रह्माभिन्नत्वविज्ञानं भवमोक्षस्य कारणम् ।  
येनाद्वितीयमानन्दं ब्रह्म सम्पद्यते बुधैः ॥ २२३ ॥

*brahmābhinnatvavijñānaṃ bhavamokṣasya kāraṇam |  
yenādvitīyamānandaṃ brahma sampadyate budhaiḥ || 223 ||*

Realizing one's identity with Brahman is what brings Liberation from the bonds of Samsara. Through it the wise person reaches Brahman, the One without a second, Bliss Absolute.

### 224

ब्रह्मभूतस्तु संसृत्यै विद्वान्नावर्तते पुनः ।  
विज्ञातव्यमतः सम्यग्ब्रह्माभिन्नत्वमात्मनः ॥ २२४ ॥

*brahmabhūtastu saṃsṛtyai vidvānnāvartate punaḥ |  
vijñātavyamataḥ samyagbrahmābhinnatvamātmanaḥ || 224 ||*

Once Brahman is realized, one does not come back to the realm of transmigration. One must therefore fully realize one's identity with Brahman.

### 225

सत्यं ज्ञानमनन्तं ब्रह्म विशुद्धं परं स्वतःसिद्धम् ।  
नित्यानन्दैकरसं प्रत्यगभिन्नं निरन्तरं जयति ॥ २२५ ॥

*satyaṃ jñānamanantaṃ brahma viśuddhaṃ paraṃ svataḥsiddham |  
nityānandaikarasaṃ pratyagabhinnaṃ nirantaraṃ jayati || 225 ||*

Brahman is Existence, Knowledge, and Infinity. It is pure, supreme, self-existent, eternal, undivided Bliss. It is not, in reality, different from the individual soul, and It has no inside or outside. It is ever triumphant.

### 226

<vt>All Manifestation Absolute</vt>  
सदिदं परमाद्वैतं स्वस्मादन्यस्य वस्तुनोऽभावात् ।  
न ह्यन्यदस्ति किंचित्सम्यक्परमार्थतत्त्वबोधदशायाम् ॥ २२६ ॥

*<vt>All Manifestation Absolute</vt>  
sadidaṃ paramādvaitaṃ svasmādanyasya vastuno'bhāvāt |  
na hyanyadasti kiṃcitsamyakparamārthatattvabodhadaśāyām || 226 ||*

This Supreme Oneness alone is real, since nothing exists but the Self. Truly, when the highest Truth is realized, no other independent entity remains.

### 227

यदिदं सकलं विश्वं नानारूपं प्रतीतमज्ञानात् ।  
तत्सर्वं ब्रह्मैव प्रत्यस्ताशेषभावनादोषम् ॥ २२७ ॥

*yadidaṃ sakalaṃ viśvaṃ nānārūpaṃ pratītamajñānāt |  
tatsarvaṃ brahmaiva pratyastāśeṣabhāvanādoṣam || 227 ||*

This whole universe, which ignorance makes appear in many forms, is nothing other than Brahman, which is completely free of all the limits of human thought.

### 228

मृत्कार्यभूतोऽपि मृदो न भिन्नः कुम्भोऽस्ति सर्वत्र तु मृत्स्वरूपात् ।  
न कुम्भरूपं पृथगस्ति कुम्भः कुतो मृषा कल्पितनाममात्रः ॥ २२८ ॥

*mṛtkāryabhūto'pi mṛdo na bhinnaḥ kumbho'sti sarvatra tu mṛtsvarūpāt |  
na kumbharūpaṃ pṛthagasti kumbhaḥ kuto mṛṣā kalpitanāmamātraḥ || 228 ||*

A jar is a modification of clay, yet it is not different from clay; throughout, the jar is essentially the same as the clay. Why then call it a jar? It is a fiction, merely a name someone has imagined.

### 229

केनापि मृद्भिन्नतया स्वरूपं घटस्य संदर्शयितुं न शक्यते ।  
अतो घटः कल्पित एव मोहान् मृदेव सत्यं परमार्थभूतम् ॥ २२९ ॥

*kenāpi mṛdbhinnatayā svarūpaṃ ghaṭasya saṃdarśayituṃ na śakyate |  
ato ghaṭaḥ kalpita eva mohān mṛdeva satyaṃ paramārthabhūtam || 229 ||*

No one can show that the essence of a jar is anything other than the clay that makes it up. The jar is only imagined as separate through delusion; the clay that composes it is the one lasting reality in the matter.

### 230

सद्ब्रह्मकार्यं सकलं सदेवं तन्मात्रमेतन्न ततोऽन्यदस्ति ।  
अस्तीति यो वक्ति न तस्य मोहो विनिर्गतो निद्रितवत्प्रजल्पः ॥ २३0 ॥

*sadbrahmakāryaṃ sakalaṃ sadevaṃ tanmātrametanna tato'nyadasti |  
astīti yo vakti na tasya moho vinirgato nidritavatprajalpaḥ || 230 ||*

In the same way, the whole universe, as the effect of the real Brahman, is in truth nothing but Brahman. That is its essence, and it has no existence apart from That. Whoever claims otherwise is still deluded and is babbling like someone asleep.

### 231

ब्रह्मैवेदं विश्वमित्येव वाणी श्रौती ब्रूतेऽथर्वनिष्ठा वरिष्ठा ।  
तस्मादेतद्ब्रह्ममात्रं हि विश्वं नाधिष्ठानाद्भिन्नतारोपितस्य ॥ २३१ ॥

*brahmaivedaṃ viśvamityeva vāṇī śrautī brūte'tharvaniṣṭhā variṣṭhā |  
tasmādetadbrahmamātraṃ hi viśvaṃ nādhiṣṭhānādbhinnatāropitasya || 231 ||*

"This universe is truly Brahman": so runs the solemn declaration of the Atharva Veda. The universe, then, is nothing but Brahman, for what is superimposed on something has no existence separate from its substratum.

### 232

सत्यं यदि स्याज्जगदेतदात्मनो ऽनन्तत्त्वहानिर्निगमाप्रमाणता ।  
असत्यवादित्वमपीशितुः स्यान् नैतत्त्रयं साधु हितं महात्मनाम् ॥ २३२ ॥

*satyaṃ yadi syājjagadetadātmano 'nantattvahānirnigamāpramāṇatā |  
asatyavāditvamapīśituḥ syān naitattrayaṃ sādhu hitaṃ mahātmanām || 232 ||*

If the universe, just as it appears, were real, duality would never end, the scriptures would be proved false, and the Lord Himself would be guilty of untruth. The noble-minded regard none of these three as desirable or wholesome.

### 233

ईश्वरो वस्तुतत्त्वज्ञो न चाहं तेष्ववस्थितः ।  
न च मत्स्थानि भूतानीत्येवमेव व्यचीकॢपत् ॥ २३३ ॥

*īśvaro vastutattvajño na cāhaṃ teṣvavasthitaḥ |  
na ca matsthāni bhūtānītyevameva vyacīkḷpat || 233 ||*

The Lord, who knows the secret of all things, has upheld this view in the words: "But I am not in them" … "nor are the beings in Me."

### 234

यदि सत्यं भवेद्विश्वं सुषुप्तामुपलभ्यताम् ।  
यन्नोपलभ्यते किंचिदतोऽसत्स्वप्नवन्मृषा ॥ २३४ ॥

*yadi satyaṃ bhavedviśvaṃ suṣuptāmupalabhyatām |  
yannopalabhyate kiṃcidato'satsvapnavanmṛṣā || 234 ||*

If the universe is true, let it be perceived in deep sleep as well. Since it is not perceived there at all, it must be unreal and false, like a dream.

### 235

अतः पृथङ्नास्ति जगत्परात्मनः पृथक्प्रतीतिस्तु मृषा गुणादिवत् ।  
आरोपितस्यास्ति किमर्थवत्ताद् धिष्ठानमाभाति तथा भ्रमेण ॥ २३५ ॥

*ataḥ pṛthaṅnāsti jagatparātmanaḥ pṛthakpratītistu mṛṣā guṇādivat |  
āropitasyāsti kimarthavattād dhiṣṭhānamābhāti tathā bhrameṇa || 235 ||*

So the universe does not exist apart from the Supreme Self, and to perceive it as separate is as false as the qualities (blueness and so on) seen in the sky. Does a superimposed attribute have any meaning apart from its substratum? It is the substratum that appears in that way through delusion.

### 236

भ्रान्तस्य यद्यद्भ्रमतः प्रतीतं भ्रामैव तत्तद्रजतं हि शुक्तिः ।  
इदंतया ब्रह्म सदैव रूप्यते त्वारोपितं ब्रह्मणि नाममात्रम् ॥ २३६ ॥

*bhrāntasya yadyadbhramataḥ pratītaṃ bhrāmaiva tattadrajataṃ hi śuktiḥ |  
idaṃtayā brahma sadaiva rūpyate tvāropitaṃ brahmaṇi nāmamātram || 236 ||*

Whatever a deluded person mistakenly perceives is Brahman and Brahman alone. The silver is nothing but mother-of-pearl. It is Brahman that is constantly taken to be this universe, while the universe superimposed on Brahman is merely a name.

### 237

<vt>Brahman - Its Nature</vt>  
अतः परं ब्रह्म सदद्वितीयं विशुद्धविज्ञानघनं निरञ्जनम् ।  
प्राशान्तमाद्यन्तविहीनमक्रियं निरन्तरानन्दरसस्वरूपम् ॥ २३७ ॥

*<vt>Brahman - Its Nature</vt>  
ataḥ paraṃ brahma sadadvatīyaṃ viśuddhavijñānaghanaṃ nirañjanam |  
prāśāntamādyantavihīnamakrīyaṃ nirantarānandarasasvarūpam || 237 ||*

So whatever is manifested, this universe, is the Supreme Brahman Itself: the Real, the One without a second, pure, the Essence of Knowledge, stainless, serene, without beginning or end, beyond activity, the Essence of Bliss Absolute. It transcends all the diversity created by Māyā or Nescience. It is eternal, forever out of reach of pain, indivisible, immeasurable, formless, undifferentiated, nameless, immutable, and self-luminous.

### 238

निरस्तमायाकृतसर्वभेदं नित्यं सुखं निष्कलमप्रमेयम् ।  
अरूपमव्यक्तमनाख्यमव्ययं ज्योतिः स्वयं किंचिदिदं चकास्ति ॥ २३८ ॥

*nirastamāyākṛtasarvabhedaṃ nityaṃ sukhaṃ niṣkalamaprameyam |  
arūpamavyaktamanākhyamavyayaṃ jyotiḥ svayaṃ kiṃcididaṃ cakāsti || 238 ||*

So whatever is manifested, this universe, is the Supreme Brahman Itself: the Real, the One without a second, pure, the Essence of Knowledge, stainless, serene, without beginning or end, beyond activity, the Essence of Bliss Absolute. It transcends all the diversity created by Māyā or Nescience. It is eternal, forever out of reach of pain, indivisible, immeasurable, formless, undifferentiated, nameless, immutable, and self-luminous.

### 239

ज्ञातृज्ञेयज्ञानशून्यमनन्तं निर्विकल्पकम् ।  
केवलाखण्डचिन्मात्रं परं तत्त्वं विदुर्बुधाः ॥ २३९ ॥

*jñātṛjñeyajñānaśūnyamanantaṃ nirvikalpakam |  
kevalākhaṇḍacinmātraṃ paraṃ tattvaṃ vidurbudhāḥ || 239 ||*

Sages realize the Supreme Truth, Brahman, in which knower, knowledge, and known are not differentiated, and which is infinite, transcendent, and the Essence of Knowledge Absolute.

### 240

अहेयमनुपादेयं मनोवाचामगोचरम् ।  
अप्रमेयमनाद्यन्तं ब्रह्म पूर्णमहं महः ॥ २४0 ॥

*aheyamanupādeyaṃ manovācāmagocaram |  
aprameyamanādyantaṃ brahma pūrṇamahaṃ mahaḥ || 240 ||*

It can be neither cast off nor taken up. It lies beyond the reach of mind and speech. It is immeasurable, without beginning or end, the Whole, one's very Self, and of surpassing glory.

### 241

<vt>That Thou Art - Explanation</vt>  
तत्त्वंपदाभ्यामभिधीयमानयोः ब्रह्मात्मनोः शोधितयोर्यदीत्थम् ।  
श्रुत्या तयोस्तत्त्वमसीति सम्यग् एकत्वमेव प्रतिपाद्यते मुहुः ॥ २४१ ॥

*<vt>That Thou Art - Explanation</vt>  
tattvaṃpadābhyāmabhidhīyamānayoḥ brahmātmanoḥ śodhitayoryadīttham |  
śrutyā tayostattvamasīti samyag ekatvameva pratipādyate muhuḥ || 241 ||*

When the Shruti, in the saying "Thou art That" (Tat-Tvam-Asi), repeatedly affirms the complete identity of Brahman (or Ishwara) and the Jiva, indicated by the words "That" (Tat) and "thou" (Tvam), while stripping those words of their relative associations, what it means to teach is the identity of their implied meanings, not their literal ones. Taken literally, the two have opposite attributes, like the sun and a glow-worm, a king and a servant, the ocean and a well, or Mount Meru and an atom.

### 242

अइक्यं तयोर्लक्षितयोर्न वाच्ययोः निगद्यतेऽन्योन्यविरुद्धधर्मिणोः ।  
खद्योतभान्वोरिव राजभृत्ययोः कूपाम्बुराश्योः परमाणुमेर्वोः ॥ २४२ ॥

*aikyaṃ tayorlakṣitayorna vācyayoḥ nigadyate'nyonyaviruddhadharmiṇoḥ |  
khadyotabhānvoriva rājabhṛtyayoḥ kūpāmburāśyoḥ paramāṇumervoḥ || 242 ||*

When the Shruti, in the saying "Thou art That" (Tat-Tvam-Asi), repeatedly affirms the complete identity of Brahman (or Ishwara) and the Jiva, indicated by the words "That" (Tat) and "thou" (Tvam), while stripping those words of their relative associations, what it means to teach is the identity of their implied meanings, not their literal ones. Taken literally, the two have opposite attributes, like the sun and a glow-worm, a king and a servant, the ocean and a well, or Mount Meru and an atom.

### 243

तयोर्विरोधोऽयमुपाधिकल्पितो न वास्तवः कश्चिदुपाधिरेषः ।  
ईशस्य माया महदादिकारणं जीवस्य कार्यं शृणु पञ्चकोशम् ॥ २४३ ॥

*tayorvirodho'yamupādhikalpito na vāstavaḥ kaścidupādhireṣaḥ |  
īśasya māyā mahadādikāraṇaṃ jīvasya kāryaṃ śṛṇu pañcakośam || 243 ||*

This opposition between them is produced by superimposition; it is not real. In the case of Ishwara (the Lord), the superimposition is Maya, or Nescience, the cause of Mahat and everything that follows. In the case of the Jiva (the individual soul), listen: the five sheaths, which are the products of Maya, play that role.

### 244

एतावुपाधी परजीवयोस्तयोः सम्यङ्निरासे न परो न जीवः ।  
राज्यं नरेन्द्रस्य भटस्य खेटक् तयोरपोहे न भटो न राजा ॥ २४४ ॥

*etāvupādhī parajīvayostayoḥ samyaṅnirāse na paro na jīvaḥ |  
rājyaṃ narendrasya bhaṭasya kheṭak tayorapohe na bhaṭo na rājā || 244 ||*

These two are the superimpositions belonging to Ishwara and to the Jiva. When they are completely removed, there is neither Ishwara nor Jiva. A kingdom marks a king, and a shield marks a soldier; take these away, and there is no king and no soldier.

### 245

अथात आदेश इति श्रुतिः स्वयं निषेधति ब्रह्मणि कल्पितं द्वयम् ।  
श्रुतिप्रमाणानुगृहीतबोधात् तयोर्निरासः करणीय एव ॥ २४५ ॥

*athāta ādeśa iti śrutiḥ svayaṃ niṣedhati brahmaṇi kalpitaṃ dvayam |  
śrutipramāṇānugṛhītabodhāt tayornirāsaḥ karaṇīya eva || 245 ||*

The Vedas themselves, in passages such as "now then is the injunction," reject the duality imagined in Brahman. One must remove those two superimpositions through realization backed by the authority of the Vedas.

### 246

नेदं नेदं कल्पितत्वान्न सत्यं रज्जुदृष्टव्यालवत्स्वप्नवच्च ।  
इत्थं दृश्यं साधुयुक्त्या व्यपोह्य ज्ञेयः पश्चादेकभावस्तयोर्यः ॥ २४६ ॥

*nedaṃ nedaṃ kalpitatvānna satyaṃ rajjudṛṣṭavyālavatsvapnavacca |  
itthaṃ dṛśyaṃ sādhuyuktyā vyapohya jñeyaḥ paścādekabhāvastayoryaḥ || 246 ||*

Neither this gross universe nor this subtle universe is the Atman. Being imagined, they are unreal, like the snake seen in a rope or the things seen in dreams. Having thoroughly eliminated the objective world in this way through reasoning, one should go on to realize the oneness that underlies Ishwara and the Jiva.

### 247

ततस्तु तौ लक्षणया सुलक्ष्यौ तयोरखण्डैकरसत्वसिद्धये ।  
नालं जहत्या न तथाजहत्या किन्तूभयार्थात्मिकयैव भाव्यम् ॥ २४७ ॥

*tatastu tau lakṣaṇayā sulakṣyau tayorakhaṇḍaikarasatvasiddhaye |  
nālaṃ jahatyā na tathājahatyā kintūbhayārthātmikayaiva bhāvyam || 247 ||*

Therefore those two terms, Ishwara and Jiva, must be weighed carefully by their implied meanings, so that their complete identity can be established. Neither rejecting their meaning entirely nor keeping it entirely will work. One must reason it out by the method that combines both.

### 248

स देवदत्तोऽयमितीह चैकता विरुद्धधर्मांशमपास्य कथ्यते ।  
यथा तथा तत्त्वमसीतिवाक्ये विरुद्धधर्मानुभयत्र हित्वा ॥ २४८ ॥

*sa devadatto'yamitīha caikatā viruddhadharmāṃśamapāsya kathyate |  
yathā tathā tattvamasītivākye viruddhadharmānubhayatra hitvā || 248 ||*

Just as the sentence "This is that Devadatta" expresses identity by setting aside the parts that conflict, so in the sentence "Thou art That" the wise person must drop the conflicting elements on both sides and recognize the identity of Ishwara and Jiva, attending closely to the essence shared by both, which is Chit, Knowledge Absolute. In this way hundreds of scriptural texts teach the oneness and identity of Brahman and the Jiva.

### 249

संलक्ष्य चिन्मात्रतया सदात्मनोः अखण्डभावः परिचीयते बुधैः ।  
एवं महावाक्यशतेन कथ्यते ब्रह्मात्मनोरैक्यमखण्डभावः ॥ २४९ ॥

*saṃlakṣya cinmātratayā sadātmanoḥ akhaṇḍabhāvaḥ paricīyate budhaiḥ |  
evaṃ mahāvākyaśatena kathyate brahmātmanoraikyamakhaṇḍabhāvaḥ || 249 ||*

Just as the sentence "This is that Devadatta" expresses identity by setting aside the parts that conflict, so in the sentence "Thou art That" the wise person must drop the conflicting elements on both sides and recognize the identity of Ishwara and Jiva, attending closely to the essence shared by both, which is Chit, Knowledge Absolute. In this way hundreds of scriptural texts teach the oneness and identity of Brahman and the Jiva.

### 250

<vt>Attitude in Meditation</vt>  
अस्थूलमित्येतदसन्निरस्य सिद्धं स्वतो व्योमवदप्रतर्क्यम् ।  
अतो मृषामात्रमिदं प्रतीतं जहीहि यत्स्वात्मतया गृहीतम् ब्रह्माहमित्येव विशुद्धबुद्ध्या विद्धि स्वमात्मानमखण्डबोधम् ॥ २५0 ॥

*<vt>Attitude in Meditation</vt>  
asthūlamityetadasannirasya siddhaṃ svato vyomavadapratarkyam |  
ato mṛṣāmātramidaṃ pratītaṃ jahīhi yatsvātmatayā gṛhītam brahmāhamityeva viśuddhabuddhyā viddhi svamātmānamakhaṇḍabodham || 250 ||*

By eliminating the not-Self in the light of passages such as "It is not gross," one realizes the Atman, which stands on its own, is unattached like the sky, and lies beyond the reach of thought. So dismiss this mere phantom of a body that you perceive and have taken to be your own self. With the purified understanding that you are Brahman, realize your own Self, the Knowledge Absolute.

### 251

मृत्कार्यं सकलं घटादि सततं मृन्मात्रमेवाहितं तद्वत्सज्जनितं सदात्मकमिदं सन्मात्रमेवाखिलम् ।  
यस्मान्नास्ति सतः परं किमपि तत्सत्यं स आत्मा स्वयं तस्मात्तत्त्वमसि प्रशान्तममलं ब्रह्माद्वयं यत्परम् ॥ २५१ ॥

*mṛtkāryaṃ sakalaṃ ghaṭādi satataṃ mṛnmātramevāhitaṃ tadvatsajjanitaṃ sadātmakamidaṃ sanmātramevākhilam |  
yasmānnāsti sataḥ paraṃ kimapi tatsatyaṃ sa ātmā svayaṃ tasmāttattvamasi praśāntamamalaṃ brahmādvayaṃ yatparam || 251 ||*

All the forms clay takes, such as a jar, which the mind always accepts as real, are in truth nothing but clay. In the same way, this whole universe, which comes forth from the real Brahman, is Brahman Itself and nothing else. Since there is nothing whatsoever apart from Brahman, and That is the sole self-existent Reality, our very Self, you are that serene, pure, Supreme Brahman, the One without a second.

### 252

निद्राकल्पितदेशकालविषयज्ञात्रादि सर्वं यथा मिथ्या तद्वदिहापि जाग्रति जगत्स्वाज्ञानकार्यत्वतः ।  
यस्मादेवमिदं शरीरकरणप्राणाहमाद्यप्यसत् तस्मात्तत्त्वमसि प्रशान्तममलं ब्रह्माद्वयं यत्परम् ॥ २५२ ॥

*nidrākalpitadeśakālaviṣayajñātrādi sarvaṃ yathā mithyā tadvadihāpi jāgrati jagatsvājñānakāryatvataḥ |  
yasmādevamidaṃ śarīrakaraṇaprāṇāhamādyapyasat tasmāttattvamasi praśāntamamalaṃ brahmādvayaṃ yatparam || 252 ||*

Just as the place, time, objects, knower, and so on that appear in a dream are all unreal, so too is the world experienced here in the waking state, for it is entirely the product of one's own ignorance. Since this body, the organs, the Pranas, egoism, and the rest are likewise unreal, you are that serene, pure, Supreme Brahman, the One without a second.

### 253

यत्र भ्रान्त्या कल्पित तद्विवेके तत्तन्मात्रं नैव तस्माद्विभिन्नम् ।  
स्वप्ने नष्टं स्वप्नविश्वं विचित्रं स्वस्माद्भिन्नं किन्नु दृष्टं प्रबोधे ॥ २५३ ॥

*yatra bhrāntyā kalpita tadviveke tattanmātraṃ naiva tasmādvibhinnam |  
svapne naṣṭaṃ svapnaviśvaṃ vicitraṃ svasmādbhinnaṃ kinnu dṛṣṭaṃ prabodhe || 253 ||*

Whatever is mistakenly thought to exist in something is, once the truth about it is known, nothing but that underlying substratum and in no way different from it. The varied universe of a dream appears and vanishes within the dream itself. On waking, does it appear as anything separate from one's own Self?

### 254

<vt>Aids to Meditation</vt>  
जातिनीतिकुलगोत्रदूरगं नामरूपगुणदोषवर्जितम् ।  
देशकालविषयातिवर्ति यद् ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २५४ ॥

*<vt>Aids to Meditation</vt>  
jātinītikulagotradūragaṃ nāmarūpaguṇadoṣavarjitam |  
deśakālaviṣayātivarti yad brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 254 ||*

That which is beyond caste and creed, family and lineage; which has no name or form, no merit or demerit; which transcends space, time, and sense-objects: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 255

यत्परं सकलवागगोचरं गोचरं विमलबोधचक्षुषः ।  
शुद्धचिद्घनमनादि वस्तु यद् ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २५५ ॥

*yatparaṃ sakalavāgagocaraṃ gocaraṃ vimalabodhacakṣuṣaḥ |  
śuddhacidghanamanādi vastu yad brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 255 ||*

That Supreme Brahman which lies beyond the reach of all speech yet is open to the eye of pure illumination; which is pure, the very Embodiment of Knowledge, the reality without beginning: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 256

षड्भिरूर्मिभिरयोगि योगिहृद् भावितं न करणैर्विभावितम् ।  
बुद्ध्यवेद्यमनवद्यमस्ति यद् ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २५६ ॥

*ṣaḍbhirūrmibhirayogi yogihṛd bhāvitaṃ na karaṇairvibhāvitam |  
buddhyavedyamanavadyamasti yad brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 256 ||*

That which the sixfold wave does not touch; which the Yogi's heart meditates upon but the sense-organs cannot grasp; which the Buddhi cannot know; and which cannot be faulted: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 257

भ्रान्तिकल्पितजगत्कलाश्रयं स्वाश्रयं च सदसद्विलक्षणम् ।  
निष्कलं निरुपमानवद्धि यद् ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २५७ ॥

*bhrāntikalpitajagatkalāśrayaṃ svāśrayaṃ ca sadasadvilakṣaṇam |  
niṣkalaṃ nirupamānavaddhi yad brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 257 ||*

That which underlies the universe with all its divisions, every one of them a creation of delusion; which Itself rests on nothing else; which is distinct from the gross and the subtle; which has no parts and truly no model: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 258

जन्मवृद्धिपरिणत्यपक्षय व्याधिनाशनविहीनमव्ययम् ।  
विश्वसृष्ट्यवविघातकारणं ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २५८ ॥

*janmavṛddhipariṇatyapakṣaya vyādhināśanavihīnamavyayam |  
viśvasṛṣṭyavavighātakāraṇaṃ brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 258 ||*

That which is free from birth, growth, development, decay, disease, and death; which cannot be destroyed; which is the cause of the projection, maintenance, and dissolution of the universe: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 259

अस्तभेदमनपास्तलक्षणं निस्तरङ्गजलराशिनिश्चलम् ।  
नित्यमुक्तमविभक्तमूर्ति यद् ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २५९ ॥

*astabhedamanapāstalakṣaṇaṃ nistaraṅgajalarāśiniścalam |  
nityamuktamavibhaktamūrti yad brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 259 ||*

That which is free of all differentiation; whose essence never ceases to exist; which is still like an ocean without waves; ever free; indivisible in Form: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 260

एकमेव सदनेककारणं कारणान्तरनिरास्यकारणम् ।  
कार्यकारणविलक्षणं स्वयं ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २६0 ॥

*ekameva sadanekakāraṇaṃ kāraṇāntaranirāsyakāraṇam |  
kāryakāraṇavilakṣaṇaṃ svayaṃ brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 260 ||*

That which, though One alone, is the cause of the many; which rules out every other cause yet has no cause Itself; which is distinct from Maya and from its effect, the universe; and which is independent: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 261

निर्विकल्पकमनल्पमक्षरं यत्क्षराक्षरविलक्षणं परम् ।  
नित्यमव्ययसुखं निरञ्जनं ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २६१ ॥

*nirvikalpakamanalpamakṣaraṃ yatkṣarākṣaravilakṣaṇaṃ param |  
nityamavyayasukhaṃ nirañjanaṃ brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 261 ||*

That which is free from duality; infinite and indestructible; distinct from the universe and from Maya; supreme and eternal; undying Bliss; without stain: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 262

यद्विभाति सदनेकधा भ्रमान् नामरूपगुणविक्रियात्मना ।  
हेमवत्स्वयमविक्रियं सदा ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २६२ ॥

*yadvibhāti sadanekadhā bhramān nāmarūpaguṇavikriyātmanā |  
hemavatsvayamavikriyaṃ sadā brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 262 ||*

That Reality which, though One, appears in many ways through delusion, taking on names and forms, qualities and changes, while Itself remaining ever unchanged, like gold in its various shapes: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 263

यच्चकास्त्यनपरं परात्परं प्रत्यगेकरसमात्मलक्षणम् ।  
सत्यचित्सुखमनन्तमव्ययं ब्रह्म तत्त्वमसि भावयात्मनि ॥ २६३ ॥

*yaccakāstyanaparaṃ parātparaṃ pratyagekarasamātmalakṣaṇam |  
satyacitsukhamanantamavyayaṃ brahma tattvamasi bhāvayātmani || 263 ||*

That beyond which nothing exists; which shines even above Maya, which in turn stands above its effect, the universe; the innermost Self of all, free from differentiation; the Real Self, Existence-Knowledge-Bliss Absolute; infinite and unchanging: that Brahman you are. Meditate on this in your mind.

### 264

उक्तमर्थमिममात्मनि स्वयं भावयेत्प्रथितयुक्तिभिर्धिया ।  
संशयादिरहितं कराम्बुवत् तेन तत्त्वनिगमो भविष्यति ॥ २६४ ॥

*uktamarthamimamātmani svayaṃ bhāvayetprathitayuktibhirdhiyā |  
saṃśayādirahitaṃ karāmbuvat tena tattvanigamo bhaviṣyati || 264 ||*

One must meditate in one's own mind on the Truth taught above, using the intellect and the accepted lines of reasoning. By doing so one will realize the truth free of doubt and the like, as plainly as water in the palm of one's hand.

### 265

संबोधमात्रं परिशुद्धतत्त्वं विज्ञाय सङ्घे नृपवच्च सैन्ये ।  
तदाश्रयः स्वात्मनि सर्वदा स्थितो विलापय ब्रह्मणि विश्वजातम् ॥ २६५ ॥

*saṃbodhamātraṃ pariśuddhatattvaṃ vijñāya saṅghe nṛpavacca sainye |  
tadāśrayaḥ svātmani sarvadā sthito vilāpaya brahmaṇi viśvajātam || 265 ||*

Realizing in this body the Knowledge Absolute, free of Nescience and its effects, as one picks out the king in an army, and remaining always established in your own Self by resting on that Knowledge, dissolve the universe in Brahman.

### 266

बुद्धौ गुहायां सदसद्विलक्षणं ब्रह्मास्ति सत्यं परमद्वितीयम् ।  
तदात्मना योऽत्र वसेद्गुहायां पुनर्न तस्याङ्गगुहाप्रवेशः ॥ २६६ ॥

*buddhau guhāyāṃ sadasadvilakṣaṇaṃ brahmāsti satyaṃ paramadvitīyam |  
tadātmanā yo'tra vasedguhāyāṃ punarna tasyāṅgaguhāpraveśaḥ || 266 ||*

In the cave of the Buddhi dwells Brahman, distinct from the gross and the subtle, Existence Absolute, Supreme, the One without a second. For the one who lives in this cave as Brahman, O beloved, there is no further entry into a mother's womb.

### 267

<vt>Give up Vāsanās - the Method</vt>  
ज्ञाते वस्तुन्यपि बलवती वासनानादिरेषा कर्ता भोक्ताप्यहमिति दृढा यास्य संसारहेतुः ।  
प्रत्यग्दृष्ट्यात्मनि निवसता सापनेया प्रयत्नान् मुक्तिं प्राहुस्तदिह मुनयो वासनातानवं यत् ॥ २६७ ॥

*<vt>Give up Vāsanās - the Method</vt>  
jñāte vastunyapi balavatī vāsanānādireṣā kartā bhoktāpyahamiti dṛḍhā yāsya saṃsārahetuḥ |  
pratyagdṛṣṭyātmani nivasatā sāpaneyā prayatnān muktiṃ prāhustadiha munayo vāsanātānavaṃ yat || 267 ||*

Even after the Truth has been realized, there persists the strong, beginningless, stubborn impression that one is the doer and the experiencer, and this is the cause of one's transmigration. It must be carefully removed by living in constant identification with the Supreme Self. The sages call Liberation the wearing away of Vasanas (impressions) here and now.

### 268

अहं ममेति यो भावो देहाक्षादावनात्मनि ।  
अध्यासोऽयं निरस्तव्यो विदुषा स्वात्मनिष्ठया ॥ २६८ ॥

*ahaṃ mameti yo bhāvo dehākṣādāvanātmani |  
adhyāso'yaṃ nirastavyo viduṣā svātmaniṣṭhayā || 268 ||*

The notion of "me" and "mine" in the body, the organs, and the rest, which are the non-Self: this superimposition the wise person must bring to an end by identifying himself with the Atman.

### 269

ज्ञात्वा स्वं प्रत्यगात्मानं बुद्धितद्वृत्तिसाक्षिणम् ।  
सोऽहमित्येव सद्वृत्त्यानात्मन्यात्ममतिं जहि ॥ २६९ ॥

*jñātvā svaṃ pratyagātmānaṃ buddhitadvṛttisākṣiṇam |  
so'hamityeva sadvṛttyānātmanyātmamatiṃ jahi || 269 ||*

Realizing your own innermost Self, the Witness of the Buddhi and its changes, and continually turning over the positive thought "I am That," overcome this identification with the non-Self.

### 270

लोकानुवर्तनं त्यक्त्वा त्यक्त्वा देहानुवर्तनम् ।  
शास्त्रानुवर्तनं त्यक्त्वा स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २७0 ॥

*lokānuvartanaṃ tyaktvā tyaktvā dehānuvartanam |  
śāstrānuvartanaṃ tyaktvā svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 270 ||*

Giving up the observance of social conventions, abandoning all concern with adorning the body, and avoiding excessive absorption in the Scriptures, remove the superimposition that has settled on you.

### 271

लोकवासनया जन्तोः शास्त्रवासनयापि च ।  
देहवासनया ज्ञानं यथावन्नैव जायते ॥ २७१ ॥

*lokavāsanayā jantoḥ śāstravāsanayāpi ca |  
dehavāsanayā jñānaṃ yathāvannaiva jāyate || 271 ||*

Because of the urge to chase after society, the passion for excessive study of the Scriptures, and the wish to keep the body in good condition, people fail to reach true Realization.

### 272

संसारकारागृहमोक्षमिच्छोर् अयोमायां पादनिबन्धशृङ्खलम् ।  
वदन्ति तज्ज्ञाः पटु वासनात्रयं योऽस्माद्विमुक्तः समुपैति मुक्तिम् ॥ २७२ ॥

*saṃsārakārāgṛhamokṣamicchor ayoMāyāṃ pādanibandhaśṛṅkhalam |  
vadanti tajjñāḥ paṭu vāsanātrayaṃ yo'smādvimuktaḥ samupaiti muktim || 272 ||*

For one who seeks release from the prison of this world (Samsara), the wise have named those three desires heavy iron chains that bind the feet. The one who is free of them truly reaches Liberation.

### 273

जलादिसंसर्गवशात्प्रभूत दुर्गन्धधूतागरुदिव्यवासना ।  
संघर्षणेनैव विभाति सम्यग् विधूयमाने सति बाह्यगन्धे ॥ २७३ ॥

*jalādisaṃsargavaśātprabhūta durgandhadhūtāgarudivyavāsanā |  
saṃgharṣaṇenaiva vibhāti samyag vidhūyamāne sati bāhyagandhe || 273 ||*

The pleasant fragrance of Agaru (agalochum), hidden by a strong stench from contact with water and the like, comes through as soon as the foreign smell has been fully rubbed away.

### 274

अन्तःश्रितानन्तदूरन्तवासना धूलीविलिप्ता परमात्मवासना ।  
प्रज्ञातिसंघर्षणतो विशुद्धा प्रतीयते चन्दनगन्धवत्स्फुटम् ॥ २७४ ॥

*antaḥśritānantadūrantavāsanā dhūlīviliptā paramātmavāsanā |  
prajñātisaṃgharṣaṇato viśuddhā pratīyate candanagandhavatsphuṭam || 274 ||*

Like the fragrance of sandalwood, the perfume of the Supreme Self, buried under the dust of countless violent impressions lodged in the mind, is clearly perceived again once it is purified by the constant friction of Knowledge.

### 275

अनात्मवासनाजालैस्तिरोभूतात्मवासना ।  
नित्यात्मनिष्ठया तेषां नाशे भाति स्वयं स्फुटम् ॥ २७५ ॥

*anātmavāsanājālaistirobhūtātmavāsanā |  
nityātmaniṣṭhayā teṣāṃ nāśe bhāti svayaṃ sphuṭam || 275 ||*

The desire for Self-realization is hidden by countless desires for things other than the Self. When those have been destroyed through constant devotion to the Self, the Atman shows Itself clearly of Its own accord.

### 276

यथा यथा प्रत्यगवस्थितं मनः तथा तथा मुञ्चति बाह्यवासनाम् ।  
निःशेषमोक्षे सति वासनानां आत्मानुभूतिः प्रतिबन्धशून्या ॥ २७६ ॥

*yathā yathā pratyagavasthitaṃ manaḥ tathā tathā muñcati bāhyavāsanām |  
niḥśeṣamokṣe sati vāsanānāṃ ātmānubhūtiḥ pratibandhaśūnyā || 276 ||*

As the mind becomes step by step established in the innermost Self, it gives up, in the same measure, its desires for external objects. And when all such desires are gone, the Atman is realized without obstruction.

### 277

<vt>End Superimposition - The Means</vt>  
स्वात्मन्येव सदा स्थित्वा मनो नश्यति योगिनः ।  
वासनानां क्षयश्चातः स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २७७ ॥

*<vt>End Superimposition - The Means</vt>  
svātmanyeva sadā sthitvā mano naśyati yoginaḥ |  
vāsanānāṃ kṣayaścātaḥ svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 277 ||*

The Yogi's mind dies by being fixed constantly on his own Self. From this follows the ending of desires. Therefore remove your superimposition.

### 278

तमो द्वाभ्यां रजः सत्त्वात्सत्त्वं शुद्धेन नश्यति ।  
तस्मात्सत्त्वमवष्टभ्य स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २७८ ॥

*tamo dvābhyāṃ rajaḥ sattvātsattvaṃ śuddhena naśyati |  
tasmātsattvamavaṣṭabhya svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 278 ||*

Tamas is destroyed by both Sattva and Rajas; Rajas by Sattva; and Sattva dies when it is purified. Therefore remove your superimposition with the help of Sattva.

### 279

प्रारब्धं पुष्यति वपुरिति निश्चित्य निश्चलः ।  
धैर्यमालम्ब्य यत्नेन स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २७९ ॥

*prārabdhaṃ puṣyati vapuriti niścitya niścalaḥ |  
dhairyamālambya yatnena svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 279 ||*

Knowing for certain that Prarabdha work will sustain this body, stay calm and remove your superimposition carefully and patiently.

### 280

नाहं जीवः परं ब्रह्मेत्यतद्व्यावृत्तिपूर्वकम् ।  
वासनावेगतः प्राप्तस्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २८0 ॥

*nāhaṃ jīvaḥ paraṃ brahmetyatadvyāvṛttipūrvakam |  
vāsanāvegataḥ prāptasvādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 280 ||*

"I am not the individual soul but the Supreme Brahman." Eliminating in this way everything that is not the Self, remove your superimposition, which has arisen from the momentum of past impressions.

### 281

श्रुत्या युक्त्या स्वानुभूत्या ज्ञात्वा सार्वात्म्यमात्मनः ।  
क्वचिदाभासतः प्राप्तस्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २८१ ॥

*śrutyā yuktyā svānubhūtyā jñātvā sārvātmyamātmanaḥ |  
kvacidābhāsataḥ prāptasvādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 281 ||*

Realizing yourself as the Self of all through Scripture, reasoning, and your own experience, remove your superimposition, even when only a trace of it seems to show.

### 282

अनादानविसर्गाभ्यामीषन्नास्ति क्रिया मुनेः ।  
तदेकनिष्ठया नित्यं स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २८२ ॥

*anādānavisargābhyāmīṣannāsti kriyā muneḥ |  
tadekaniṣṭhayā nityaṃ svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 282 ||*

The sage has no connection with action, since he has no notion of taking up or giving up. Therefore, through constant absorption in Brahman, remove your superimposition.

### 283

तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्योत्थब्रह्मात्मैकत्वबोधतः ।  
ब्रह्मण्यात्मत्वदार्ढ्याय स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २८३ ॥

*tattvamasyādivākyotthabrahmātmaikatvabodhataḥ |  
brahmaṇyātmatvadārḍhyāya svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 283 ||*

Through realizing the identity of Brahman and the soul, which follows from great sayings such as "Thou art That," remove your superimposition, so as to strengthen your identification with Brahman.

### 284

अहंभावस्य देहेऽस्मिन्निःशेषविलयावधि ।  
सावधानेन युक्तात्मा स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २८४ ॥

*ahaṃbhāvasya dehe'sminniḥśeṣavilayāvadhi |  
sāvadhānena yuktātmā svādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 284 ||*

Until identification with this body is completely uprooted, remove your superimposition with vigilance and a concentrated mind.

### 285

प्रतीतिर्जीवजगतोः स्वप्नवद्भाति यावता ।  
तावन्निरन्तरं विद्वन्स्वाध्यासापनयं कुरु ॥ २८५ ॥

*pratītirjīvajagatoḥ svapnavadbhāti yāvatā |  
tāvannirantaraṃ vidvansvādhyāsāpanayaṃ kuru || 285 ||*

As long as even a dream-like perception of the universe and of souls remains, remove your superimposition, O learned one, without the slightest pause.

### 286

निद्राया लोकवार्तायाः शब्दादेरपि विस्मृतेः ।  
क्वचिन्नावसरं दत्त्वा चिन्तयात्मानमात्मनि ॥ २८६ ॥

*nidrāyā lokavārtāyāḥ śabdāderapi vismṛteḥ |  
kvacinnāvasaraṃ dattvā cintayātmānamātmani || 286 ||*

Giving not the least opening to forgetfulness, whether from sleep, worldly concerns, or the sense-objects, reflect on the Self in your mind.

### 287

मातापित्रोर्मलोद्भूतं मलमांसमायां वपुः ।  
त्यक्त्वा चाण्डालवद्दूरं ब्रह्मीभूय कृती भव ॥ २८७ ॥

*mātāpitrormalodbhūtaṃ malamāṃsaMāyāṃ vapuḥ |  
tyaktvā cāṇḍālavaddūraṃ brahmībhūya kṛtī bhava || 287 ||*

Keeping a safe distance from the body, which has come from the impurities of the parents and is itself made of flesh and impurities, as one keeps away from an outcast, be Brahman and reach the fulfillment of your life.

### 288

घटाकाशं महाकाश इवात्मानं परात्मनि ।  
विलाप्याखण्डभावेन तूष्णी भव सदा मुने ॥ २८८ ॥

*ghaṭākāśaṃ mahākāśa ivātmānaṃ parātmani |  
vilāpyākhaṇḍabhāvena tūṣṇī bhava sadā mune || 288 ||*

Merging the finite soul in the Supreme Self, as the space enclosed in a jar merges into infinite space, through meditation on their identity, remain always at peace, O sage.

### 289

स्वप्रकाशमधिष्ठानं स्वयंभूय सदात्मना ।  
ब्रह्माण्डमपि पिण्डाण्डं त्यज्यतां मलभाण्डवत् ॥ २८९ ॥

*svaprakāśamadhiṣṭhānaṃ svayaṃbhūya sadātmanā |  
brahmāṇḍamapi piṇḍāṇḍaṃ tyajyatāṃ malabhāṇḍavat || 289 ||*

Becoming yourself the self-luminous Brahman, the substratum of all phenomena, and as that Reality, discard both the macrocosm and the microcosm, like two filthy containers.

### 290

चिदात्मनि सदानन्दे देहारूढामहंधियम् ।  
निवेश्य लिङ्गमुत्सृज्य केवलो भव सर्वदा ॥ २९0 ॥

*cidātmani sadānande dehārūḍhāmahaṃdhiyam |  
niveśya liṅgamutsṛjya kevalo bhava sarvadā || 290 ||*

Shifting the identification now rooted in the body over to the Atman, Existence-Knowledge-Bliss Absolute, and casting off the subtle body, be forever alone and independent.

### 291

यत्रैष जगदाभासो दर्पणान्तः पुरं यथा ।  
तद्ब्रह्माहमिति ज्ञात्वा कृतकृत्यो भविष्यसि ॥ २९१ ॥

*yatraiṣa jagadābhāso darpaṇāntaḥ puraṃ yathā |  
tadbrahmāhamiti jñātvā kṛtakṛtyo bhaviṣyasi || 291 ||*

That in which this universe is reflected, like a city in a mirror: that Brahman you are. Knowing this, you will reach the fulfillment of your life.

### 292

यत्सत्यभूतं निजरूपमाद्यं चिदद्वयानन्दमरूपमक्रियम् ।  
तदेत्य मिथ्यावपुरुत्सृजेत शैलूषवद्वेषमुपात्तमात्मनः ॥ २९२ ॥

*yatsatyabhūtaṃ nijarūpamādyaṃ cidadvayānandamarūpamakriyam |  
tadetya mithyāvapurutsṛjeta śailūṣavadveṣamupāttamātmanaḥ || 292 ||*

That which is real and is one's own original Essence, that Knowledge and Bliss Absolute, the One without a second, beyond form and activity: on attaining That, one should stop identifying with one's false bodies, like an actor setting aside the mask he has put on.

### 293

<vt>The Perceived I' Factor - False</vt>  
सर्वात्मना दृश्यमिदं मृषैव नैवाहमर्थः क्षणिकत्वदर्शनात् ।  
जानाम्यहं सर्वमिति प्रतीतिः कुतोऽहमादेः क्षणिकस्य सिध्येत् ॥ २९३ ॥

*<vt>The Perceived I' Factor - False</vt>  
sarvātmanā dṛśyamidaṃ mṛṣaiva naivāhamarthaḥ kṣaṇikatvadarśanāt |  
jānāmyahaṃ sarvamiti pratītiḥ kuto'hamādeḥ kṣaṇikasya sidhyet || 293 ||*

This objective universe is wholly unreal. Nor is egoism real, for it is seen to be momentary. How can the perception "I know all" hold true of egoism and the rest, which are momentary?

### 294

अहंपदार्थस्त्वहमादिसाक्षी नित्यं सुषुप्तावपि भावदर्शनात् ।  
ब्रूते ह्यजो नित्य इति श्रुतिः स्वयं तत्प्रत्यगात्मा सदसद्विलक्षणः ॥ २९४ ॥

*ahaṃpadārthastvahamādisākṣī nityaṃ suṣuptāvapi bhāvadarśanāt |  
brūte hyajo nitya iti śrutiḥ svayaṃ tatpratyagātmā sadasadvilakṣaṇaḥ || 294 ||*

The real "I," however, is that which witnesses the ego and everything else. It exists always, even in deep sleep. The Shruti itself says, "It is birthless, eternal," and so on. Therefore the Paramatman is different from the gross and subtle bodies.

### 295

विकारिणां सर्वविकारवेत्ता नित्याविकारो भवितुं समर्हति ।  
मनोरथस्वप्नसुषुप्तिषु स्फुटं पुनः पुनर्दृष्टमसत्त्वमेतयोः ॥ २९५ ॥

*vikāriṇāṃ sarvavikāravettā nityāvikāro bhavituṃ samarhati |  
manorathasvapnasuṣuptiṣu sphuṭaṃ punaḥ punardṛṣṭamasattvametayoḥ || 295 ||*

The knower of all changes in changing things must itself be eternal and changeless. The unreality of the gross and subtle bodies is observed clearly, again and again, in imagination, dream, and deep sleep.

### 296

अतोऽभिमानं त्यज मांसपिण्डे पिण्डाभिमानिन्यपि बुद्धिकल्पिते ।  
कालत्रयाबाध्यमखण्डबोधं ज्ञात्वा स्वमात्मानमुपैहि शान्तिम् ॥ २९६ ॥

*ato'bhimānaṃ tyaja māṃsapiṇḍe piṇḍābhimāninyapi buddhikalpite |  
kālatrayābādhyamakhaṇḍabodhaṃ jñātvā svamātmānamupaihi śāntim || 296 ||*

Therefore give up identification with this lump of flesh, the gross body, and with the ego, the subtle body, both of which are imagined by the Buddhi. Realizing your own Self, which is Knowledge Absolute and cannot be denied in past, present, or future, attain Peace.

### 297

त्यजाभिमानं कुलगोत्रनाम रूपाश्रमेष्वार्द्रशवाश्रितेषु ।  
लिङ्गस्य धर्मानपि कर्तृतादिंस् त्यक्ता भवाखण्डसुखस्वरूपः ॥ २९७ ॥

*tyajābhimānaṃ kulagotranāma rūpāśrameṣvārdraśavāśriteṣu |  
liṅgasya dharmānapi kartṛtādiṃs tyaktā bhavākhaṇḍasukhasvarūpaḥ || 297 ||*

Stop identifying yourself with family, lineage, name, form, and stage of life, which belong to the body, a body that is like a rotting corpse to a person of realization. Likewise, giving up notions of agency and the like, which are attributes of the subtle body, be the Essence of Bliss Absolute.

### 298

<vt>Condemnation of the Ego</vt>  
सन्त्यन्ये प्रतिबन्धाः पुंसः संसारहेतवो दृष्टाः ।  
तेषामेवं मूलं प्रथमविकारो भवत्यहंकारः ॥ २९८ ॥

*<vt>Condemnation of the Ego</vt>  
santyanye pratibandhāḥ puṃsaḥ saṃsārahetavo dṛṣṭāḥ |  
teṣāmevaṃ mūlaṃ prathamavikāro bhavatyahaṃkāraḥ || 298 ||*

Other obstacles too are seen to exist for people, and they lead to transmigration. For the reasons given above, their root is the first modification of Nescience, called egoism.

### 299

यावत्स्यात्स्वस्य संबन्धोऽहंकारेण दुरात्मना ।  
तावन्न लेशमात्रापि मुक्तिवार्ता विलक्षणा ॥ २९९ ॥

*yāvatsyātsvasya saṃbandho'haṃkāreṇa durātmanā |  
tāvanna leśamātrāpi muktivārtā vilakṣaṇā || 299 ||*

As long as one keeps any connection with this wicked ego, there can be no talk whatsoever of Liberation, which is unlike anything else.

### 300

अहंकारग्रहान्मुक्तः स्वरूपमुपपद्यते ।  
चन्द्रवद्विमलः पूर्णः सदानन्दः स्वयंप्रभः ॥ ३00 ॥

*ahaṃkāragrahānmuktaḥ svarūpamupapadyate |  
candravadvimalaḥ pūrṇaḥ sadānandaḥ svayaṃprabhaḥ || 300 ||*

Released from the grip of egoism, as the moon is released from Rahu, a person attains his true nature and becomes pure, infinite, ever blissful, and self-luminous.

### 301

यो वा पुरे सोऽहमिति प्रतीतो बुद्ध्या प्रकॢप्तस्तमसातिमूढया ।  
तस्यैव निःशेषतया विनाशे ब्रह्मात्मभावः प्रतिबन्धशून्यः ॥ ३0१ ॥

*yo vā pure so'hamiti pratīto buddhyā prakḷptastamasātimūḍhayā |  
tasyaiva niḥśeṣatayā vināśe brahmātmabhāvaḥ pratibandhaśūnyaḥ || 301 ||*

The Buddhi, deeply deluded by Nescience, has produced something that shows up in this body as 'I am such and such'. When that egoism is wholly destroyed, one gains an unobstructed identity with Brahman.

### 302

ब्रह्मानन्दनिधिर्महाबलवताहंकारघोराहिना संवेष्ट्यात्मनि रक्ष्यते गुणमायाइश्चण्डेस्त्रिभिर्मस्तकैः ।  
विज्ञानाख्यमहासिना श्रुतिमता विच्छिद्य शीर्षत्रयं निर्मूल्याहिमिमं निधिं सुखकरं धीरोऽनुभोक्तुंक्षमः ॥ ३0२ ॥

*brahmānandanidhirmahābalavatāhaṃkāraghorāhinā saṃveṣṭyātmani rakṣyate guṇaMāyāiścaṇḍestribhirmastakaiḥ |  
vijñānākhyamahāsinā śrutimatā vicchidya śīrṣatrayaṃ nirmūlyāhimimaṃ nidhiṃ sukhakaraṃ dhīro'nubhoktuṃkṣamaḥ || 302 ||*

The mighty, dreadful serpent of egoism has coiled itself around the treasure of the Bliss of Brahman and guards it for its own use with three fierce hoods, the three Guṇas. Only the wise person can enjoy this bliss-giving treasure, by destroying the serpent, cutting off its three hoods with the great sword of realization as the Śrutis teach.

### 303

यावद्वा यत्किंचिद्विषदोषस्फूर्तिरस्ति चेद्देहे ।  
कथमारोग्याय भवेत्तद्वदहन्तापि योगिनो मुक्त्यै ॥ ३0३ ॥

*yāvadvā yatkiṃcidviṣadoṣasphūrtirasti ceddehe |  
kathamārogyāya bhavettadvadahantāpi yogino muktyai || 303 ||*

How can anyone hope to recover while even a trace of poison remains in the body? Egoism has the same effect on the Yogi's Liberation.

### 304

अहमोऽत्यन्तनिवृत्त्या तत्कृतनानाविकल्पसंहृत्या ।  
प्रत्यक्तत्त्वविवेकादिदमहमस्मीति विन्दते तत्त्वम् ॥ ३0४ ॥

*ahamo'tyantanivṛttyā tatkṛtanānāvikalpasaṃhṛtyā |  
pratyaktattvavivekādidamahamasmīti vindate tattvam || 304 ||*

When egoism has ceased entirely, when the many mental waves it produces have been stopped, and when the inner Reality has been discriminated, one realizes that Reality as 'I am This'.

### 305

अहंकारे कर्तर्यहमिति मतिं मुञ्च सहसा विकारात्मन्यात्मप्रतिफलजुषि स्वस्थितिमुषि ।  
यदध्यासात्प्राप्ता जनिमृतिजरादुःखबहुला प्रतीचश्चिन्मूर्तेस्तव सुखतनोः संसृतिरियम् ॥ ३0५ ॥

*ahaṃkāre kartaryahamiti matiṃ muñca sahasā vikārātmanyātmapratiphalajuṣi svasthitimuṣi |  
yadadhyāsātprāptā janimṛtijarāduḥkhabahulā pratīcaścinmūrtestava sukhatanoḥ saṃsṛtiriyam || 305 ||*

Drop at once your identification with egoism, the agent. By its nature it is a modification, it carries a reflection of the Self, and it turns one away from being established in the Self. By identifying with it you have fallen into this relative existence, full of the miseries of birth, decay, and death, even though you are the Witness, the Essence of Knowledge and Bliss Absolute.

### 306

सदैकरूपस्य चिदात्मनो विभोर् आनन्दमूर्तेरनवद्यकीर्तेः ।  
नैवान्यथा क्वाप्यविकारिणस्ते विनाहमध्यासममुष्य संसृतिः ॥ ३0६ ॥

*sadaikarūpasya cidātmano vibhor ānandamūrteranavadyakīrteḥ |  
naivānyathā kvāpyavikāriṇaste vināhamadhyāsamamuṣya saṃsṛtiḥ || 306 ||*

If not for your identification with that egoism, you could never undergo transmigration, for you are unchanging and eternally the same, Knowledge Absolute, present everywhere, Bliss Absolute, and of untarnished glory.

### 307

तस्मादहंकारमिमं स्वशत्रुं भोक्तुर्गले कण्टकवत्प्रतीतम् ।  
विच्छिद्य विज्ञानमहासिना स्फुटं भुङ्क्ष्वात्मसाम्राज्यसुखं यथेष्टम् ॥ ३0७ ॥

*tasmādahaṃkāramimaṃ svaśatruṃ bhokturgale kaṇṭakavatpratītam |  
vicchidya vijñānamahāsinā sphuṭaṃ bhuṅkṣvātmasāmrājyasukhaṃ yatheṣṭam || 307 ||*

So destroy this egoism, your enemy, which is like a thorn stuck in the throat of someone eating a meal. Use the great sword of realization, and then enjoy, directly and freely, the bliss of your own empire, the majesty of the Ātman.

### 308

ततोऽहमादेर्विनिवर्त्य वृत्तिं संत्यक्तरागः परमार्थलाभात् ।  
तूष्णीं समास्स्वात्मसुखानुभूत्या पूर्णात्मना ब्रह्मणि निर्विकल्पः ॥ ३0८ ॥

*tato'hamādervinivartya vṛttiṃ saṃtyaktarāgaḥ paramārthalābhāt |  
tūṣṇīṃ samāssvātmasukhānubhūtyā pūrṇātmanā brahmaṇi nirvikalpaḥ || 308 ||*

Restrain the activities of egoism and the like, and let go of all attachment by realizing the Supreme Reality. Through the enjoyment of the Bliss of the Self, be free from all duality, and rest quietly in Brahman, for you have reached your infinite nature.

### 309

समूलकृत्तोऽपि महानहं पुनः व्युल्लेखितः स्याद्यदि चेतसा क्षणम् ।  
संजीव्य विक्षेपशतं करोति नभस्वता प्रावृषि वारिदो यथा ॥ ३0९ ॥

*samūlakṛtto'pi mahānahaṃ punaḥ vyullekhitaḥ syādyadi cetasā kṣaṇam |  
saṃjīvya vikṣepaśataṃ karoti nabhasvatā prāvṛṣi vārido yathā || 309 ||*

Even when it has been pulled out by the roots, this terrible egoism, if turned over in the mind for just a moment, springs back to life and causes hundreds of troubles, like a cloud driven in by the wind in the rainy season.

### 310

<vt>Actions, Thoughts and Vāsanās - Renounce</vt>  
निगृह्य शत्रोरहमोऽवकाशः क्वचिन्न देयो विषयानुचिन्तया ।  
स एव संजीवनहेतुरस्य प्रक्षीणजम्बीरतरोरिवाम्बु ॥ ३१0 ॥

*<vt>Actions, Thoughts and Vāsanās - Renounce</vt>  
nigṛhya śatrorahamo'vakāśaḥ kvacinna deyo viṣayānucintayā |  
sa eva saṃjīvanaheturasya prakṣīṇajambīratarorivāmbu || 310 ||*

Having overpowered this enemy, egoism, give it not a moment's relief by thinking about sense objects. Such thinking is what revives it, as water revives a citron tree that has nearly dried up.

### 311

देहात्मना संस्थित एव कामी विलक्षणः कामयिता कथं स्यात् ।  
अतोऽर्थसन्धानपरत्वमेव भेदप्रसक्त्या भवबन्धहेतुः ॥ ३११ ॥

*dehātmanā saṃsthita eva kāmī vilakṣaṇaḥ kāmayitā kathaṃ syāt |  
ato'rthasandhānaparatvameva bhedaprasaktyā bhavabandhahetuḥ || 311 ||*

Only someone identified with the body is greedy for sense pleasures. How could a person without the body-idea be greedy in that way? So the habit of thinking about sense objects is truly the cause of the bondage of transmigration, since it gives rise to the idea of distinction or duality.

### 312

कार्यप्रवर्धनाद्बीजप्रवृद्धिः परिदृश्यते ।  
कार्यनाशाद्बीजनाशस्तस्मात्कार्यं निरोधयेत् ॥ ३१२ ॥

*kāryapravardhanādbījapravṛddhiḥ paridṛśyate |  
kāryanāśādbījanāśastasmātkāryaṃ nirodhayet || 312 ||*

When the effects develop, the seed is seen to develop as well, and when the effects are destroyed, the seed is seen to be destroyed too. One must therefore subdue the effects.

### 313

वासनावृद्धितः कार्यं कार्यवृद्ध्या च वासना ।  
वर्धते सर्वथा पुंसः संसारो न निवर्तते ॥ ३१३ ॥

*vāsanāvṛddhitaḥ kāryaṃ kāryavṛddhyā ca vāsanā |  
vardhate sarvathā puṃsaḥ saṃsāro na nivartate || 313 ||*

As desires grow, selfish work grows, and as selfish work grows, desire grows too. And so a person's transmigration never ends.

### 314

संसारबन्धविच्छित्त्यै तद्द्वयं प्रदहेद्यतिः ।  
वासनावृद्धिरेताभ्यां चिन्तया क्रियया बहिः ॥ ३१४ ॥

*saṃsārabandhavicchittyai taddvayaṃ pradahedyatiḥ |  
vāsanāvṛddhiretābhyāṃ cintayā kriyayā bahiḥ || 314 ||*

To break the chain of transmigration, the Sannyasin should burn those two to ashes, because thinking about sense objects and performing selfish acts cause desires to increase.

### 315

ताभ्यां प्रवर्धमाना सा सूते संसृतिमात्मनः ।  
त्रयाणां च क्षयोपायः सर्वावस्थासु सर्वदा ॥ ३१५ ॥

*tābhyāṃ pravardhamānā sā sūte saṃsṛtimātmanaḥ |  
trayāṇāṃ ca kṣayopāyaḥ sarvāvasthāsu sarvadā || 315 ||*

Strengthened by these two, desires produce one's transmigration. But the way to destroy these three is to look on everything, in all circumstances, always, everywhere, and in every respect, as Brahman and Brahman alone. As the longing to be one with Brahman grows stronger, those three are wiped out.

### 316

सर्वत्र सर्वतः सर्वब्रह्ममात्रावलोकनैः ।  
सद्भाववासनादार्ढ्यात्तत्त्रयं लयमश्नुते ॥ ३१६ ॥

*sarvatra sarvataḥ sarvabrahmamātrāvalokanaiḥ |  
sadbhāvavāsanādārḍhyāttattrayaṃ layamaśnute || 316 ||*

Strengthened by these two, desires produce one's transmigration. But the way to destroy these three is to look on everything, in all circumstances, always, everywhere, and in every respect, as Brahman and Brahman alone. As the longing to be one with Brahman grows stronger, those three are wiped out.

### 317

क्रियानाशे भवेच्चिन्तानाशोऽस्माद्वासनाक्षयः ।  
वासनाप्रक्षयो मोक्षः सा जीवन्मुक्तिरिष्यते ॥ ३१७ ॥

*kriyānāśe bhaveccintānāśo'smādvāsanākṣayaḥ |  
vāsanāprakṣayo mokṣaḥ sā jīvanmuktiriṣyate || 317 ||*

When selfish action ceases, brooding on sense objects stops, and the destruction of desires follows. The destruction of desires is Liberation, and it is regarded as Liberation-in-life.

### 318

सद्वासनास्फूर्तिविजृम्भणे सति ह्यसौ विलीनाप्यहमादिवासना ।  
अतिप्रकृष्टाप्यरुणप्रभायां विलीयते साधु यथा तमिस्रा ॥ ३१८ ॥

*sadvāsanāsphūrtivijṛmbhaṇe sati hyasau vilīnāpyahamādivāsanā |  
atiprakṛṣṭāpyaruṇaprabhāyāṃ vilīyate sādhu yathā tamisrā || 318 ||*

When the desire to realize Brahman shows itself strongly, egoistic desires quickly disappear, just as the deepest darkness truly disappears before the glow of the rising sun.

### 319

तमस्तमःकार्यमनर्थजालं न दृश्यते सत्युदिते दिनेशे ।  
तथाद्वयानन्दरसानुभूतौ न वास्ति बन्धो न च दुःखगन्धः ॥ ३१९ ॥

*tamastamaḥkāryamanarthajālaṃ na dṛśyate satyudite dineśe |  
tathādvayānandarasānubhūtau na vāsti bandho na ca duḥkhagandhaḥ || 319 ||*

When the sun rises, darkness and the many evils that come with it are no longer seen. In the same way, on realizing the Bliss Absolute, there is neither bondage nor the slightest trace of misery.

### 320

<vt>Total Vigilance - Its Price</vt>  
दृश्यं प्रतीतं प्रविलापयन्सन् सन्मात्रमानन्दघनं विभावयन् ।  
समाहितः सन्बहिरन्तरं वा कालं नयेथाः सति कर्मबन्धे ॥ ३२0 ॥

*<vt>Total Vigilance - Its Price</vt>  
dṛśyaṃ pratītaṃ pravilāpayansan sanmātramānandaghanaṃ vibhāvayan |  
samāhitaḥ sanbahirantaraṃ vā kālaṃ nayethāḥ sati karmabandhe || 320 ||*

Making the outer and inner universe now being perceived disappear, and meditating on the Reality, Bliss Embodied, one should spend one's time watchfully, if any Prārabdha work remains.

### 321

प्रमादो ब्रह्मनिष्ठायां न कर्तव्यः कदाचन ।  
प्रमादो मृत्युरित्याह भगवान्ब्रह्मणः सुतः ॥ ३२१ ॥

*pramādo brahmaniṣṭhāyāṃ na kartavyaḥ kadācana |  
pramādo mṛtyurityāha bhagavānbrahmaṇaḥ sutaḥ || 321 ||*
