One must never be careless in one's steadfastness to Brahman. Bhagavan Sanatkumāra, the son of Brahma, has called inattention death itself.

### 322

न प्रमादादनर्थोऽन्यो ज्ञानिनः स्वस्वरूपतः ।  
ततो मोहस्ततोऽहंधीस्ततो बन्धस्ततो व्यथा ॥ ३२२ ॥

*na pramādādanartho'nyo jñāninaḥ svasvarūpataḥ |  
tato mohastato'haṃdhīstato bandhastato vyathā || 322 ||*

For the Jñānin there is no greater danger than carelessness about his own real nature. From it comes delusion, from delusion egoism, after that bondage, and then misery.

### 323

विषयाभिमुखं दृष्ट्वा विद्वांसमपि विस्मृतिः ।  
विक्षेपयति धीदोषैर्योषा जारमिव प्रियम् ॥ ३२३ ॥

*viṣayābhimukhaṃ dṛṣṭvā vidvāṃsamapi vismṛtiḥ |  
vikṣepayati dhīdoṣairyoṣā jāramiva priyam || 323 ||*

When it finds even a wise man hankering after sense objects, forgetfulness torments him through the evil tendencies of the Buddhi, as a woman torments her doting lover.

### 324

यथापकृष्टं शैवालं क्षणमात्रं न तिष्ठति ।  
आवृणोति तथा माया प्राज्ञं वापि पराङ्मुखम् ॥ ३२४ ॥

*yathāpakṛṣṭaṃ śaivālaṃ kṣaṇamātraṃ na tiṣṭhati |  
āvṛṇoti tathā māyā prājñaṃ vāpi parāṅmukham || 324 ||*

Moss, even when cleared away, does not stay away for a moment but spreads over the water again. In the same way, Māyā or Nescience covers even a wise man if he turns away from meditation on the Self.

### 325

लक्ष्यच्युतं चेद्यदि चित्तमीषद् बहिर्मुखं सन्निपतेत्ततस्ततः ।  
प्रमादतः प्रच्युतकेलिकन्दुकः सोपानपङ्क्तौ पतितो यथा तथा ॥ ३२५ ॥

*lakṣyacyutaṃ cedyadi cittamīṣad bahirmukhaṃ sannipatettatastataḥ |  
pramādataḥ pracyutakelikandukaḥ sopānapaṅktau patito yathā tathā || 325 ||*

If the mind strays even slightly from the Ideal and turns outward, it keeps sinking lower and lower, like a play-ball accidentally dropped on a staircase, bouncing down from one step to the next.

### 326

विषयेष्वाविशच्चेतः संकल्पयति तद्गुणान् ।  
सम्यक्संकल्पनात्कामः कामात्पुंसः प्रवर्तनम् ॥ ३२६ ॥

*viṣayeṣvāviśaccetaḥ saṃkalpayati tadguṇān |  
samyaksaṃkalpanātkāmaḥ kāmātpuṃsaḥ pravartanam || 326 ||*

A mind attached to sense objects dwells on their qualities. From sustained dwelling comes desire, and once he desires a thing, a man sets about getting it.

### 327

अतः प्रमादान्न परोऽस्ति मृत्युः विवेकिनो ब्रह्मविदः समाधौ ।  
समाहितः सिद्धिमुपैति सम्यक् समाहितात्मा भव सावधानः ॥ ३२७ ॥

*ataḥ pramādānna paro'sti mṛtyuḥ vivekino brahmavidaḥ samādhau |  
samāhitaḥ siddhimupaiti samyak samāhitātmā bhava sāvadhānaḥ || 327 ||*

For the discriminating knower of Brahman, then, there is no worse death than inattention in concentration. But the concentrated person gains complete success. (Therefore) carefully concentrate your mind (on Brahman).

### 328

ततः स्वरूपविभ्रंशो विभ्रष्टस्तु पतत्यधः ।  
पतितस्य विना नाशं पुनर्नारोह ईक्ष्यते ॥ ३२८ ॥

*tataḥ svarūpavibhraṃśo vibhraṣṭastu patatyadhaḥ |  
patitasya vinā nāśaṃ punarnāroha īkṣyate || 328 ||*

Through inattention a person strays from his real nature, and one who has strayed falls. The fallen person is ruined and is hardly ever seen to rise again.

### 329

संकल्पं वर्जयेत्तस्मात्सर्वानर्थस्य कारणम् ।  
जीवतो यस्य कैवल्यं विदेहे स च केवलः यत्किंचित्पश्यतो भेदं भयं ब्रूते यजुःश्रुतिः ॥ ३२९ ॥

*saṃkalpaṃ varjayettasmātsarvānarthasya kāraṇam |  
jīvato yasya kaivalyaṃ videhe sa ca kevalaḥ yatkiṃcitpaśyato bhedaṃ bhayaṃ brūte yajuḥśrutiḥ || 329 ||*

One should therefore give up dwelling on sense objects, the root of all trouble. Only someone who is completely detached while living remains detached after the body dissolves. The Yajur-Veda declares that there is fear for anyone who sees even the smallest distinction.

### 330

<vt>In the One, No Plurality</vt>  
यदा कदा वापि विपश्चिदेष ब्रह्मण्यनन्तेऽप्यणुमात्रभेदम् ।  
पश्यत्यथामुष्य भयं तदैव यद्वीक्षितं भिन्नतया प्रमादात् ॥ ३३0 ॥

*<vt>In the One, No Plurality</vt>  
yadā kadā vāpi vipaścideṣa brahmaṇyanante'pyaṇumātrabhedam |  
paśyatyathāmuṣya bhayaṃ tadaiva yadvīkṣitaṃ bhinnatayā pramādāt || 330 ||*

Whenever the wise man sees even the slightest difference in the infinite Brahman, what he has mistakenly seen as different immediately becomes a source of terror for him.

### 331

श्रुतिस्मृतिन्यायशतैर्निषिद्धे दृश्येऽत्र यः स्वात्ममतिं करोति ।  
उपैति दुःखोपरि दुःखजातं निषिद्धकर्ता स मलिम्लुचो यथा ॥ ३३१ ॥

*śrutismṛtinyāyaśatairniṣiddhe dṛśye'tra yaḥ svātmamatiṃ karoti |  
upaiti duḥkhopari duḥkhajātaṃ niṣiddhakartā sa malimluco yathā || 331 ||*

Whoever identifies with the objective universe, which hundreds of Shrutis, Smritis, and reasonings have denied, suffers one misery after another, like a thief, because he is doing what is forbidden.

### 332

सत्याभिसंधानरतो विमुक्तो महत्त्वमात्मीयमुपैति नित्यम् ।  
मिथ्याभिसन्धानरतस्तु नश्येद् दृष्टं तदेतद्यदचौरचौरयोः ॥ ३३२ ॥

*satyābhisaṃdhānarato vimukto mahattvamātmīyamupaiti nityam |  
mithyābhisandhānaratastu naśyed dṛṣṭaṃ tadetadyadacauracaurayoḥ || 332 ||*

The person devoted to meditation on the Reality (Brahman) and free from Nescience reaches the eternal glory of the Ātman. But one who dwells on the unreal (the universe) is destroyed. This is shown by the case of someone who is not a thief and someone who is.

### 333

यतिरसदनुसन्धिं बन्धहेतुं विहाय स्वयमायामहमस्मीत्यात्मदृष्ट्यैव तिष्ठेत् ।  
सुखयति ननु निष्ठा ब्रह्मणि स्वानुभूत्या हरति परमविद्याकार्यदुःखं प्रतीतम् ॥ ३३३ ॥

*yatirasadanusandhiṃ bandhahetuṃ vihāya svayaMāyāmahamasmītyātmadṛṣṭyaiva tiṣṭhet |  
sukhayati nanu niṣṭhā brahmaṇi svānubhūtyā harati paramavidyākāryaduḥkhaṃ pratītam || 333 ||*

The Sannyasin should stop dwelling on the unreal, which causes bondage, and should always keep his thoughts on the Ātman as 'I myself am This'. For steadfastness in Brahman, through realizing one's identity with It, gives rise to bliss and completely removes the misery born of nescience that one experiences (in the ignorant state).

### 334

बाह्यानुसन्धिः परिवर्धयेत्फलं दुर्वासनामेव ततस्ततोऽधिकाम् ।  
ज्ञात्वा विवेकैः परिहृत्य बाह्यं स्वात्मानुसन्धिं विदधीत नित्यम् ॥ ३३४ ॥

*bāhyānusandhiḥ parivardhayetphalaṃ durvāsanāmeva tatastato'dhikām |  
jñātvā vivekaiḥ parihṛtya bāhyaṃ svātmānusandhiṃ vidadhīta nityam || 334 ||*

Dwelling on outer objects will only intensify its fruits, namely the growth of evil tendencies, which get worse and worse. Knowing this through discrimination, one should avoid outer objects and devote oneself constantly to meditation on the Ātman.

### 335

बाह्ये निरुद्धे मनसः प्रसन्नता मनःप्रसादे परमात्मदर्शनम् ।  
तस्मिन्सुदृष्टे भवबन्धनाशो बहिर्निरोधः पदवी विमुक्तेः ॥ ३३५ ॥

*bāhye niruddhe manasaḥ prasannatā manaḥprasāde paramātmadarśanam |  
tasminsudṛṣṭe bhavabandhanāśo bahirnirodhaḥ padavī vimukteḥ || 335 ||*

When the external world is shut out, the mind is cheerful, and a cheerful mind brings the vision of the Paramātman. When It is perfectly realized, the chain of birth and death is broken. Shutting out the external world is therefore the stepping-stone to Liberation.

### 336

कः पण्डितः सन्सदसद्विवेकी श्रुतिप्रमाणः परमार्थदर्शी ।  
जानन्हि कुर्यादसतोऽवलम्बं स्वपातहेतोः शिशुवन्मुमुक्षुः ॥ ३३६ ॥

*kaḥ paṇḍitaḥ sansadasadvivekī śrutipramāṇaḥ paramārthadarśī |  
jānanhi kuryādasato'valambaṃ svapātahetoḥ śiśuvanmumukṣuḥ || 336 ||*

Where is the person who is learned, able to tell the real from the unreal, who accepts the Vedas as authority, whose gaze is fixed on the Ātman, the Supreme Reality, and who seeks Liberation, yet who would knowingly, like a child, turn to the unreal (the universe) that will bring about his fall?

### 337

देहादिसंसक्तिमतो न मुक्तिः मुक्तस्य देहाद्यभिमत्यभावः ।  
सुप्तस्य नो जागरणं न जाग्रतः स्वप्नस्तयोर्भिन्नगुणाश्रयत्वात् ॥ ३३७ ॥

*dehādisaṃsaktimato na muktiḥ muktasya dehādyabhimatyabhāvaḥ |  
suptasya no jāgaraṇaṃ na jāgrataḥ svapnastayorbhinnaguṇāśrayatvāt || 337 ||*

Someone attached to the body and the like has no Liberation, and the liberated person does not identify with the body and the like. The sleeper is not awake and the one awake is not asleep, since these two states contradict each other in nature.

### 338

अन्तर्बहिः स्वं स्थिरजङ्गमेषु ज्ञात्वात्मनाधारतया विलोक्य ।  
त्यक्ताखिलोपाधिरखण्डरूपः पूर्णात्मना यः स्थित एष मुक्तः ॥ ३३८ ॥

*antarbahiḥ svaṃ sthirajaṅgameṣu jñātvātmanādhāratayā vilokya |  
tyaktākhilopādhirakhaṇḍarūpaḥ pūrṇātmanā yaḥ sthita eṣa muktaḥ || 338 ||*

That person is free who, knowing through the mind the Self in things moving and unmoving and seeing It as their substratum, gives up all superimpositions and abides as the Absolute, the infinite Self.

### 339

<vt>Spiritual Growth – the Secret</vt>  
सर्वात्मना बन्धविमुक्तिहेतुः सर्वात्मभावान्न परोऽस्ति कश्चित् ।  
दृश्याग्रहे सत्युपपद्यतेऽसौ सर्वात्मभावोऽस्य सदात्मनिष्ठया ॥ ३३९ ॥

*<vt>Spiritual Growth – the Secret</vt>  
sarvātmanā bandhavimuktihetuḥ sarvātmabhāvānna paro'sti kaścit |  
dṛśyāgrahe satyupapadyate'sau sarvātmabhāvo'sya sadātmaniṣṭhayā || 339 ||*

To be free of bondage, one must realize that the whole universe is the Self. Nothing surpasses identifying the universe with the Self. This state comes from setting aside the objective world and remaining steadfast in the eternal Ātman.

### 340

दृश्यस्याग्रहणं कथं नु घटते देहात्मना तिष्ठतो बाह्यार्थानुभवप्रसक्तमनसस्तत्तत्क्रियां कुर्वतः ।  
संन्यस्ताखिलधर्मकर्मविषयैर्नित्यात्मनिष्ठापरैः तत्त्वज्ञैः करणीयमात्मनि सदानन्देच्छुभिर्यत्नतः ॥ ३४0 ॥

*dṛśyasyāgrahaṇaṃ kathaṃ nu ghaṭate dehātmanā tiṣṭhato bāhyārthānubhavaprasaktamanasastattatkriyāṃ kurvataḥ |  
saṃnyastākhiladharmakarmaviṣayairnityātmaniṣṭhāparaiḥ tattvajñaiḥ karaṇīyamātmani sadānandecchubhiryatnataḥ || 340 ||*

How can the objective world be excluded by someone who lives identified with the body, whose mind clings to perceiving outer objects, and who performs various acts for that purpose? This exclusion should be carefully practiced by sages who have given up all kinds of duties, actions, and objects, who are passionately devoted to the eternal Ātman, and who want to possess undying bliss.

### 341

सर्वात्मसिद्धये भिक्षोः कृतश्रवणकर्मणः ।  
समाधिं विदधात्येषा शान्तो दान्त इति श्रुतिः ॥ ३४१ ॥

*sarvātmasiddhaye bhikṣoḥ kṛtaśravaṇakarmaṇaḥ |  
samādhiṃ vidadhātyeṣā śānto dānta iti śrutiḥ || 341 ||*

For the Sannyasin who has completed the act of hearing, the Shruti passage 'Calm, self-controlled' and so on prescribes Samadhi as the way to realize the identity of the universe with the Self.

### 342

आरूढशक्तेरहमो विनाशः कर्तुन्न शक्य सहसापि पण्डितैः ।  
ये निर्विकल्पाख्यसमाधिनिश्चलाः तानन्तरानन्तभवा हि वासनाः ॥ ३४२ ॥

*ārūḍhaśakterahamo vināśaḥ kartunna śakya sahasāpi paṇḍitaiḥ |  
ye nirvikalpākhyasamādhiniścalāḥ tānantarānantabhavā hi vāsanāḥ || 342 ||*

Once egoism has grown strong, even wise people cannot destroy it suddenly, except those made perfectly calm through Nirvikalpa Samadhi. Desires are truly the result of innumerable births.

### 343

अहंबुद्ध्यैव मोहिन्या योजयित्वावृतेर्बलात् ।  
विक्षेपशक्तिः पुरुषं विक्षेपयति तद्गुणैः ॥ ३४३ ॥

*ahaṃbuddhyaiva mohinyā yojayitvāvṛterbalāt |  
vikṣepaśaktiḥ puruṣaṃ vikṣepayati tadguṇaiḥ || 343 ||*

With the help of the veiling power, the projecting power ties a person to the siren of an egoistic idea and distracts him through its attributes.

### 344

विक्षेपशक्तिविजयो विषमो विधातुं निःशेषमावरणशक्तिनिवृत्त्यभावे ।  
दृग्दृश्ययोः स्फुटपयोजलवद्विभागे नश्येत्तदावरणमात्मनि च स्वभावात् निःसंशयेन भवति प्रतिबन्धशून्यो विक्षेपणं नहिं तदा यदि चेन्मृषार्थे ॥ ३४४ ॥

*vikṣepaśaktivijayo viṣamo vidhātuṃ niḥśeṣamāvaraṇaśaktinivṛttyabhāve |  
dṛgdṛśyayoḥ sphuṭapayojalavadvibhāge naśyettadāvaraṇamātmani ca svabhāvāt niḥsaṃśayena bhavati pratibandhaśūnyo vikṣepaṇaṃ nahiṃ tadā yadi cenmṛṣārthe || 344 ||*

It is extremely difficult to conquer the projecting power unless the veiling power has been perfectly rooted out. That covering over the Ātman naturally disappears when the subject is perfectly distinguished from the objects, as milk is from water. But the victory is undoubtedly (complete and) free from obstacles only when the mind no longer wavers because of unreal sense objects.

### 345

सम्यग्विवेकः स्फुटबोधजन्यो विभज्य दृग्दृश्यपदार्थतत्त्वम् ।  
छिनत्ति मायाकृतमोहबन्धं यस्माद्विमुक्तस्तु पुनर्न संसृतिः ॥ ३४५ ॥

*samyagvivekaḥ sphuṭabodhajanyo vibhajya dṛgdṛśyapadārthatattvam |  
chinatti māyākṛtamohabandhaṃ yasmādvimuktastu punarna saṃsṛtiḥ || 345 ||*

Perfect discrimination arising from direct realization separates the true nature of the subject from that of the object and breaks the bond of delusion created by Māyā. For someone freed from this, there is no more transmigration.

### 346

परावरैकत्वविवेकवन्हिः दहत्यविद्यागहनं ह्यशेषम् ।  
किं स्यात्पुनः संसरणस्य बीजं अद्वैतभावं समुपेयुषोऽस्य ॥ ३४६ ॥

*parāvaraikatvavivekavanhiḥ dahatyavidyāgahanaṃ hyaśeṣam |  
kiṃ syātpunaḥ saṃsaraṇasya bījaṃ advaitabhāvaṃ samupeyuṣo'sya || 346 ||*

Knowledge of the identity of the Jiva and Brahman completely burns up the impenetrable forest of Avidya or Nescience. For one who has realized the state of Oneness, is any seed left for future transmigration?

### 347

आवरणस्य निवृत्तिर्भवति हि सम्यक्पदार्थदर्शनतः ।  
मिथ्याज्ञानविनाशस्तद्विक्षेपजनितदुःखनिवृत्तिः ॥ ३४७ ॥

*āvaraṇasya nivṛttirbhavati hi samyakpadārthadarśanataḥ |  
mithyājñānavināśastadvikṣepajanitaduḥkhanivṛttiḥ || 347 ||*

The veil hiding Truth disappears only when the Reality is fully realized. (Then follow) the destruction of false knowledge and the end of the misery that its distracting influence brought about.

### 348

एतत्त्रितयं दृष्टं सम्यग्रज्जुस्वरूपविज्ञानात् ।  
तस्माद्वस्तुसतत्त्वं ज्ञातव्यं बन्धमुक्तये विदुषा ॥ ३४८ ॥

*etattritayaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ samyagrajjusvarūpavijñānāt |  
tasmādvastusatattvaṃ jñātavyaṃ bandhamuktaye viduṣā || 348 ||*

These three are seen in the case of a rope once its real nature is fully known. The wise person should therefore know the real nature of things in order to break his bonds.

### 349

<vt>Cause-Effect - False</vt>  
अयोऽग्नियोगादिव सत्समन्वयान् मात्रादिरूपेण विजृम्भते धीः ।  
तत्कार्यमेतद्द्वितयं यतो मृषा दृष्टं भ्रमस्वप्नमनोरथेषु ॥ ३४९ ॥

*<vt>Cause-Effect - False</vt>  
ayo'gniyogādiva satsamanvayān mātrādirūpeṇa vijṛmbhate dhīḥ |  
tatkāryametaddvitayaṃ yato mṛṣā dṛṣṭaṃ bhramasvapnamanoratheṣu || 349 ||*

As iron shows itself as sparks through contact with fire, the Buddhi shows itself as knower and known through the inherence of Brahman. These two (knower and known), the effects of the Buddhi, are seen to be unreal in delusion, dream, and fancy. In the same way, the modifications of Prakriti, from egoism down to the body and all sense objects, are also unreal. They are unreal precisely because they change every moment. But the Ātman never changes.

### 350

ततो विकाराः प्रकृतेरहंमुखा देहावसाना विषयाश्च सर्वे ।  
क्षणेऽन्यथाभावितया ह्यमीषाम् असत्त्वमात्मा तु कदापि नान्यथा ॥ ३५0 ॥

*tato vikārāḥ prakṛterahaṃmukhā dehāvasānā viṣayāśca sarve |  
kṣaṇe'nyathābhāvitayā hyamīṣām asattvamātmā tu kadāpi nānyathā || 350 ||*

As iron shows itself as sparks through contact with fire, the Buddhi shows itself as knower and known through the inherence of Brahman. These two (knower and known), the effects of the Buddhi, are seen to be unreal in delusion, dream, and fancy. In the same way, the modifications of Prakriti, from egoism down to the body and all sense objects, are also unreal. They are unreal precisely because they change every moment. But the Ātman never changes.

### 351

नित्याद्वयाखण्डचिदेकरूपो बुद्ध्यादिसाक्षी सदसद्विलक्षणः ।  
अहंपदप्रत्ययलक्षितार्थः प्रत्यक्सदानन्दघनः परात्मा ॥ ३५१ ॥

*nityādvayākhaṇḍacidekarūpo buddhyādisākṣī sadasadvilakṣaṇaḥ |  
ahaṃpadapratyayalakṣitārthaḥ pratyaksadānandaghanaḥ parātmā || 351 ||*

Eternal, indivisible knowledge is always the very nature of the Supreme Self, which is one without a second. It stands as Witness to the Buddhi and the rest, apart from both gross and subtle. It is what the term and idea 'I' really point to, and it is inner, eternal bliss embodied.

### 352

इत्थं विपश्चित्सदसद्विभज्य निश्चित्य तत्त्वं निजबोधदृष्ट्या ।  
ज्ञात्वा स्वमात्मानमखण्डबोधं तेभ्यो विमुक्तः स्वयमेव शाम्यति ॥ ३५२ ॥

*itthaṃ vipaścitsadasadvibhajya niścitya tattvaṃ nijabodhadṛṣṭyā |  
jñātvā svamātmānamakhaṇḍabodhaṃ tebhyo vimuktaḥ svayameva śāmyati || 352 ||*

Discriminating the real from the unreal in this way, determining the Truth through his illumined insight, and realizing his own Self, which is Knowledge Absolute, the wise person is rid of obstructions and directly reaches Peace.

### 353

अज्ञानहृदयग्रन्थेर्निःशेषविलयस्तदा ।  
समाधिनाविकल्पेन यदाद्वैतात्मदर्शनम् ॥ ३५३ ॥

*ajñānahṛdayagrantherniḥśeṣavilayastadā |  
samādhināvikalpena yadādvaitātmadarśanam || 353 ||*

When the Ātman, the One without a second, is realized through Nirvikalpa Samadhi, the heart's knot of ignorance is completely destroyed.

### 354

<vt>Samadhi - Its Nature</vt>  
त्वमहमिदमितीयं कल्पना बुद्धिदोषात् प्रभवति परमात्मन्यद्वये निर्विशेषे ।  
प्रविलसति समाधावस्य सर्वो विकल्पो विलयनमुपगच्छेद्वस्तुतत्त्वावधृत्या ॥ ३५४ ॥

*<vt>Samadhi - Its Nature</vt>  
tvamahamidamitīyaṃ kalpanā buddhidoṣāt prabhavati paramātmanyadvaye nirviśeṣe |  
pravilasati samādhāvasya sarvo vikalpo vilayanamupagacchedvastutattvāvadhṛtyā || 354 ||*

Imaginings such as 'thou', 'I', or 'this' occur because of the defects of the Buddhi. But when the Paramātman, the Absolute, the One without a second, reveals Itself in Samadhi, all such imaginings dissolve for the aspirant through realization of the truth of Brahman.

### 355

शान्तो दान्तः परमुपरतः क्षान्तियुक्तः समाधिं कुर्वन्नित्यं कलयति यतिः स्वस्य सर्वात्मभावम् ।  
तेनाविद्यातिमिरजनितान्साधु दग्ध्वा विकल्पान् ब्रह्माकृत्या निवसति सुखं निष्क्रियो निर्विकल्पः ॥ ३५५ ॥

*śānto dāntaḥ paramuparataḥ kṣāntiyuktaḥ samādhiṃ kurvannityaṃ kalayati yatiḥ svasya sarvātmabhāvam |  
tenāvidyātimirajanitānsādhu dagdhvā vikalpān brahmākṛtyā nivasati sukhaṃ niṣkriyo nirvikalpaḥ || 355 ||*

The Sannyasin, calm, self-controlled, fully withdrawn from the world of the senses, patient, and devoted to the practice of Samadhi, always reflects that his own self is the Self of the whole universe. Having by this means completely destroyed the imaginings that come from the gloom of ignorance, he lives blissfully as Brahman, free from action and the wavering of the mind.

### 356

समाहिता ये प्रविलाप्य बाह्यं श्रोत्रादि चेतः स्वमहं चिदात्मनि ।  
त एव मुक्ता भवपाशबन्धैः नान्ये तु पारोक्ष्यकथाभिधायिनः ॥ ३५६ ॥

*samāhitā ye pravilāpya bāhyaṃ śrotrādi cetaḥ svamahaṃ cidātmani |  
ta eva muktā bhavapāśabandhaiḥ nānye tu pārokṣyakathābhidhāyinaḥ || 356 ||*

Only those who attain Samadhi and merge the objective world, the sense organs, the mind, and even the ego itself in the Ātman, the Knowledge Absolute, are free from the bondage of transmigration. No one else is, who only dabbles in second-hand talk.

### 357

उपाधिभेदात्स्वयमेव भिद्यते चोपाध्यपोहे स्वयमेव केवलः ।  
तस्मादुपाधेर्विलयाय विद्वान् वसेत्सदाकल्पसमाधिनिष्ठया ॥ ३५७ ॥

*upādhibhedātsvayameva bhidyate copādhyapohe svayameva kevalaḥ |  
tasmādupādhervilayāya vidvān vasetsadākalpasamādhiniṣṭhayā || 357 ||*

Because of the variety of the supervening conditions (Upadhis), a person tends to think of himself as full of variety too. But when these are removed he is again his own Self, the unchanging. The wise person should therefore always devote himself to the practice of Nirvikalpa Samadhi for the dissolution of the Upadhis.

### 358

सति सक्तो नरो याति सद्भावं ह्येकनिष्ठया ।  
कीटको भ्रमरं ध्यायन् भ्रमरत्वाय कल्पते ॥ ३५८ ॥

*sati sakto naro yāti sadbhāvaṃ hyekaniṣṭhayā |  
kīṭako bhramaraṃ dhyāyan bhramaratvāya kalpate || 358 ||*

Through one-pointed devotion, the person attached to the Real becomes Real, just as the cockroach that thinks intently of the Bhramara is transformed into a Bhramara.

### 359

क्रियान्तरासक्तिमपास्य कीटको ध्यायन्नलित्वं ह्यलिभावमृच्छति ।  
तथैव योगी परमात्मतत्त्वं ध्यात्वा समायाति तदेकनिष्ठया ॥ ३५९ ॥

*kriyāntarāsaktimapāsya kīṭako dhyāyannalitvaṃ hyalibhāvamṛcchati |  
tathaiva yogī paramātmatattvaṃ dhyātvā samāyāti tadekaniṣṭhayā || 359 ||*

The cockroach gives up its attachment to all other actions, thinks intently of the Bhramara, and is transformed into that worm. In exactly the same way, the Yogi who meditates on the truth of the Paramātman reaches It through one-pointed devotion to It.

### 360

अतीव सूक्ष्मं परमात्मतत्त्वं न स्थूलदृष्ट्या प्रतिपत्तुमर्हति ।  
समाधिनात्यन्तसुसूक्ष्मवृत्या ज्ञातव्यमार्यैरतिशुद्धबुद्धिभिः ॥ ३६0 ॥

*atīva sūkṣmaṃ paramātmatattvaṃ na sthūladṛṣṭyā pratipattumarhati |  
samādhinātyantasusūkṣmavṛtyā jñātavyamāryairatiśuddhabuddhibhiḥ || 360 ||*

The truth of the Paramātman is extremely subtle and cannot be reached by the mind's gross, outgoing tendency. It is accessible only to noble souls with perfectly pure minds, through Samadhi brought about by an extraordinary fineness of the mental state.

### 361

यथा सुवर्णं पुटपाकशोधितं त्यक्त्वा मलं स्वात्मगुणं समृच्छति ।  
तथा मनः सत्त्वरजस्तमोमलं ध्यानेन सन्त्यज्य समेति तत्त्वम् ॥ ३६१ ॥

*yathā suvarṇaṃ puṭapākaśodhitaṃ tyaktvā malaṃ svātmaguṇaṃ samṛcchati |  
tathā manaḥ sattvarajastamomalaṃ dhyānena santyajya sameti tattvam || 361 ||*

When gold is heated thoroughly in the fire, it is refined, sheds its impurities, and regains its own lustre. In the same way, meditation rids the mind of its impurities of Sattva, Rajas, and Tamas, and the mind arrives at the reality of Brahman.

### 362

निरन्तराभ्यासवशात्तदित्थं पक्वं मनो ब्रह्मणि लीयते यदा ।  
तदा समाधिः सविकल्पवर्जितः स्वतोऽद्वयानन्दरसानुभावकः ॥ ३६२ ॥

*nirantarābhyāsavaśāttaditthaṃ pakvaṃ mano brahmaṇi līyate yadā |  
tadā samādhiḥ savikalpavarjitaḥ svato'dvayānandarasānubhāvakaḥ || 362 ||*

Once the mind, cleansed in this way by steady practice, has merged in Brahman, Samadhi moves from the Savikalpa stage to the Nirvikalpa stage and leads straight to the realisation of the Bliss of Brahman, the One without a second.

### 363

समाधिनानेन समस्तवासना ग्रन्थेर्विनाशोऽखिलकर्मनाशः ।  
अन्तर्बहिः सर्वत एव सर्वदा स्वरूपविस्फूर्तिरयत्नतः स्यात् ॥ ३६३ ॥

*samādhinānena samastavāsanā granthervināśo'khilakarmanāśaḥ |  
antarbahiḥ sarvata eva sarvadā svarūpavisphūrtirayatnataḥ syāt || 363 ||*

This Samadhi destroys every desire, each of which is like a knot, and brings all work to an end. Then, within and without, everywhere and at all times, one's real nature shows itself spontaneously.

### 364

श्रुतेः शतगुणं विद्यान्मननं मननादपि ।  
निदिंध्यासं लक्षगुणमनन्तं निर्विकल्पकम् ॥ ३६४ ॥

*śruteḥ śataguṇaṃ vidyānmananaṃ mananādapi |  
nidiṃdhyāsaṃ lakṣaguṇamanantaṃ nirvikalpakam || 364 ||*

Count reflection a hundred times better than hearing, and meditation a hundred thousand times better even than reflection. But the results of Nirvikalpa Samadhi have no limit.

### 365

निर्विकल्पकसमाधिना स्फुटं ब्रह्मतत्त्वमवगम्यते ध्रुवम् ।  
नान्यथा चलतया मनोगतेः प्रत्ययान्तरविमिश्रितं भवेत् ॥ ३६५ ॥

*nirvikalpakasamādhinā sphuṭaṃ brahmatattvamavagamyate dhruvam |  
nānyathā calatayā manogateḥ pratyayāntaravimiśritaṃ bhavet || 365 ||*

Only through Nirvikalpa Samadhi is the truth of Brahman realised clearly and with certainty. There is no other way, because otherwise the mind, unstable by nature, tends to get mixed up with other perceptions.

### 366

अतः समाधत्स्व यतेन्द्रियः सन् निरन्तरं शान्तमनाः प्रतीचि ।  
विध्वंसय ध्वान्तमनाद्यविद्यया कृतं सदेकत्वविलोकनेन ॥ ३६६ ॥

*ataḥ samādhatsva yatendriyaḥ san nirantaraṃ śāntamanāḥ pratīci |  
vidhvaṃsaya dhvāntamanādyavidyayā kṛtaṃ sadekatvavilokanena || 366 ||*

So, with a calm mind and controlled senses, keep submerging the mind in the Supreme Self within. By realising that you are one with that Reality, destroy the darkness produced by beginningless Nescience.

### 367

योगस्य प्रथमद्वारं वाङ्निरोधोऽपरिग्रहः ।  
निराशा च निरीहा च नित्यमेकान्तशीलता ॥ ३६७ ॥

*yogasya prathamadvāraṃ vāṅnirodho'parigrahaḥ |  
nirāśā ca nirīhā ca nityamekāntaśīlatā || 367 ||*

The first steps toward Yoga are these: controlling speech, accepting no gifts, holding no expectations, staying free of activity, and always living in a secluded place.

### 368

एकान्तस्थितिरिन्द्रियोपरमणे हेनुर्दमश्चेतसः संरोधे करणं शमेन विलयं यायादहंवासना ।  
तेनानन्दरसानुभूतिरचला ब्राह्मी सदा योगिनः तस्माच्चित्तनिरोध एव सततं कार्यः प्रयत्नो मुनेः ॥ ३६८ ॥

*ekāntasthitirindriyoparamaṇe henurdamaścetasaḥ saṃrodhe karaṇaṃ śamena vilayaṃ yāyādahaṃvāsanā |  
tenānandarasānubhūtiracalā brāhmī sadā yoginaḥ tasmāccittanirodha eva satataṃ kāryaḥ prayatno muneḥ || 368 ||*

Living in seclusion helps to control the sense-organs. Control of the senses helps to control the mind. Control of the mind destroys egoism, and that gives the Yogi an unbroken realisation of the Bliss of Brahman. For this reason a person of reflection should always strive for one thing alone: control of the mind.

### 369

वाचं नियच्छात्मनि तं नियच्छ बुद्धौ धियं यच्छ च बुद्धिसाक्षिणि ।  
तं चापि पूर्णात्मनि निर्विकल्पे विलाप्य शान्तिं परमां भजस्व ॥ ३६९ ॥

*vācaṃ niyacchātmani taṃ niyaccha buddhau dhiyaṃ yaccha ca buddhisākṣiṇi |  
taṃ cāpi pūrṇātmani nirvikalpe vilāpya śāntiṃ paramāṃ bhajasva || 369 ||*

Hold speech back into the Manas, and hold Manas back into the Buddhi. Then hold that back into the witness of Buddhi. Merge that too in the Infinite Absolute Self, and reach supreme Peace.

### 370

देहप्राणेन्द्रियमनोबुद्ध्यादिभिरुपाधिभिः ।  
यैर्यैर्वृत्तेःसमायोगस्ततद्भावोऽस्य योगिनः ॥ ३७0 ॥

*dehaprāṇendriyamanobuddhyādibhirupādhibhiḥ |  
yairyairvṛtteḥsamāyogastatadbhāvo'sya yoginaḥ || 370 ||*

The body, the Pranas, the organs, the Manas, the Buddhi, and the rest: whichever of these added adjuncts the mind is joined with, the Yogi turns, as it were, into that.

### 371

तन्निवृत्त्या मुनेः सम्यक्सर्वोपरमणं सुखम् ।  
संदृश्यते सदानन्दरसानुभवविप्लवः ॥ ३७१ ॥

*tannivṛttyā muneḥ samyaksarvoparamaṇaṃ sukham |  
saṃdṛśyate sadānandarasānubhavaviplavaḥ || 371 ||*

When this stops, the person of reflection proves to be readily detached from everything and comes to experience everlasting Bliss in abundance.

### 372

अन्तस्त्यागो बहिस्त्यागो विरक्तस्यैव युज्यते ।  
त्यजत्यन्तर्बहिःसङ्गं विरक्तस्तु मुमुक्षया ॥ ३७२ ॥

*antastyāgo bahistyāgo viraktasyaiva yujyate |  
tyajatyantarbahiḥsaṅgaṃ viraktastu mumukṣayā || 372 ||*

Only someone with dispassion (Vairagya) is fit for this renunciation, both inner and outer. Wishing to be free, the dispassionate person lets go of attachment within and without.

### 373

<vt>Fully Detached - Samadhi Easy</vt>  
बहिस्तु विषयैः सङ्गं तथान्तरहमादिभिः ।  
विरक्त एव शक्नोति त्यक्तुं ब्रह्मणि निष्ठितः ॥ ३७३ ॥

*<vt>Fully Detached - Samadhi Easy</vt>  
bahistu viṣayaiḥ saṅgaṃ tathāntarahamādibhiḥ |  
virakta eva śaknoti tyaktuṃ brahmaṇi niṣṭhitaḥ || 373 ||*

Only the dispassionate person, firmly grounded in Brahman, can abandon the outer attachment to sense-objects and the inner attachment to egoism and the like.

### 374

वैराग्यबोधौ पुरुषस्य पक्षिवत् पक्षौ विजानीहि विचक्षण त्वम् ।  
विमुक्तिसौधाग्रलताधिरोहणं ताभ्यां विना नान्यतरेण सिध्यति ॥ ३७४ ॥

*vairāgyabodhau puruṣasya pakṣivat pakṣau vijānīhi vicakṣaṇa tvam |  
vimuktisaudhāgralatādhirohaṇaṃ tābhyāṃ vinā nānyatareṇa sidhyati || 374 ||*

Understand, wise one, that for an aspirant dispassion and discrimination are like the two wings of a bird. Without both, no one can use just one of them to reach the creeper of Liberation, which grows, so to speak, at the top of a building.

### 375

अत्यन्तवैराग्यवतः समाधिः समाहितस्यैव दृढप्रबोधः ।  
प्रबुद्धतत्त्वस्य हि बन्धमुक्तिः मुक्तात्मनो नित्यसुखानुभूतिः ॥ ३७५ ॥

*atyantavairāgyavataḥ samādhiḥ samāhitasyaiva dṛḍhaprabodhaḥ |  
prabuddhatattvasya hi bandhamuktiḥ muktātmano nityasukhānubhūtiḥ || 375 ||*

Only the intensely dispassionate person has Samadhi. Only the person of Samadhi gains steady realisation. Only one who has realised the Truth is free of bondage. And only the free soul experiences eternal Bliss.

### 376

वैराग्यान्न परं सुखस्य जनकं पश्यामि वश्यात्मनः तच्चेच्छुद्धतरात्मबोधसहितं स्वाराज्यसाम्राज्यधुक् ।  
एतद्द्वारमजस्रमुक्तियुवतेर्यस्मात्त्वमस्मात्परं सर्वत्रास्पृहया सदात्मनि सदा प्रज्ञां कुरु श्रेयसे ॥ ३७६ ॥

*vairāgyānna paraṃ sukhasya janakaṃ paśyāmi vaśyātmanaḥ taccecchuddhatarātmabodhasahitaṃ svārājyasāmrājyadhuk |  
etaddvāramajasramuktiyuvateryasmāttvamasmātparaṃ sarvatrāspṛhayā sadātmani sadā prajñāṃ kuru śreyase || 376 ||*

For a person of self-control I find no better means of happiness than dispassion. When it is joined with a very pure realisation of the Self, it leads to the sovereignty of absolute Independence. And because this is the gateway to the maiden of everlasting liberation, for your own good be dispassionate within and without, and keep your mind always fixed on the eternal Self.

### 377

आशां छिन्द्धि विषोपमेषु विषयेष्वेषैव मृत्योः कृतिस् त्यक्त्वा जातिकुलाश्रमेष्वभिमतिं मुञ्चातिदूरात्क्रियाः ।  
देहादावसति त्यजात्मधिषणां प्रज्ञां कुरुष्वात्मनि त्वं द्रष्टास्यमनोऽसि निर्द्वयपरं ब्रह्मासि यद्वस्तुतः ॥ ३७७ ॥

*āśāṃ chinddhi viṣopameṣu viṣayeṣveṣaiva mṛtyoḥ kṛtis tyaktvā jātikulāśrameṣvabhimatiṃ muñcātidūrātkriyāḥ |  
dehādāvasati tyajātmadhiṣaṇāṃ prajñāṃ kuruṣvātmani tvaṃ draṣṭāsyamano'si nirdvayaparaṃ brahmāsi yadvastutaḥ || 377 ||*

Cut off your craving for sense-objects, which are like poison, because that craving is the very image of death. Abandon pride in caste, family, and order of life, and cast actions far away. Stop identifying with unreal things such as the body, and fix your mind on the Atman. For you are really the Witness, Brahman, not bound by the mind, the One without a second, the Supreme.

### 378

लक्ष्ये ब्रह्मणि मानसं दृढतरं संस्थाप्य बाह्येन्द्रियं स्वस्थाने विनिवेश्य निश्चलतनुश्चोपेक्ष्य देहस्थितिम् ।  
ब्रह्मात्मैक्यमुपेत्य तन्मायातया चाखण्डवृत्त्यानिशं ब्रह्मानन्दरसं पिबात्मनि मुदा शून्यैः किमन्यैर्भृशम् ॥ ३७८ ॥

*lakṣye brahmaṇi mānasaṃ dṛḍhataraṃ saṃsthāpya bāhyendriyaṃ svasthāne viniveśya niścalatanuścopekṣya dehasthitim |  
brahmātmaikyamupetya tanMāyātayā cākhaṇḍavṛttyāniśaṃ brahmānandarasaṃ pibātmani mudā śūnyaiḥ kimanyairbhṛśam || 378 ||*

Fix the mind firmly on Brahman, the Ideal. Hold the outer organs back in their own centres. Keep the body steady and give no thought to maintaining it. Reach identity with Brahman and be one with It, and in your own Self drink joyfully and without a break from the Bliss of Brahman. What use are other things, which are completely hollow?

### 379

<vt>Meditation - the Technique</vt>  
अनात्मचिन्तनं त्यक्त्वा कश्मलं दुःखकारणम् ।  
चिन्तयात्मानमानन्दरूपं यन्मुक्तिकारणम् ॥ ३७९ ॥

*<vt>Meditation - the Technique</vt>  
anātmacintanaṃ tyaktvā kaśmalaṃ duḥkhakāraṇam |  
cintayātmānamānandarūpaṃ yanmuktikāraṇam || 379 ||*

Abandon thoughts of the non-Self, which are harmful and produce misery, and think instead of the Self, Bliss Absolute, which leads to Liberation.

### 380

एष स्वयंज्योतिरशेषसाक्षी विज्ञानकोशो विलसत्यजस्रम् ।  
लक्ष्यं विधायैनमसद्विलक्षणम् अखण्डवृत्त्यात्मतयानुभावय ॥ ३८0 ॥

*eṣa svayaṃjyotiraśeṣasākṣī vijñānakośo vilasatyajasram |  
lakṣyaṃ vidhāyainamasadvilakṣaṇam akhaṇḍavṛttyātmatayānubhāvaya || 380 ||*

Here the Atman shines forever, the self-luminous Witness of everything, with the Buddhi as Its seat. Take this Atman, distinct from the unreal, as your goal, and meditate on It as your own Self, shutting out every other thought.

### 381

एतमच्छीन्नया वृत्त्या प्रत्ययान्तरशून्यया ।  
उल्लेखयन्विजानीयात्स्वस्वरूपतया स्फुटम् ॥ ३८१ ॥

*etamacchīnnayā vṛttyā pratyayāntaraśūnyayā |  
ullekhayanvijānīyātsvasvarūpatayā sphuṭam || 381 ||*

Reflecting on this Atman without pause and with no outside thought coming between, one must clearly realise It to be one's real Self.

### 382

अत्रात्मत्वं दृढीकुर्वन्नहमादिषु संत्यजन् ।  
उदासीनतया तेषु तिष्ठेत्स्फुटघटादिवत् ॥ ३८२ ॥

*atrātmatvaṃ dṛḍhīkurvannahamādiṣu saṃtyajan |  
udāsīnatayā teṣu tiṣṭhetsphuṭaghaṭādivat || 382 ||*

Strengthen your identification with This and drop your identification with egoism and the rest. Live with no concern for them, as though they were worthless things such as a cracked jar.

### 383

विशुद्धमन्तःकरणं स्वरूपे निवेश्य साक्षिण्यवबोधमात्रे ।  
शनैः शनैर्निश्चलतामुपानयन् पूर्णं स्वमेवानुविलोकयेत्ततः ॥ ३८३ ॥

*viśuddhamantaḥkaraṇaṃ svarūpe niveśya sākṣiṇyavabodhamātre |  
śanaiḥ śanairniścalatāmupānayan pūrṇaṃ svamevānuvilokayettataḥ || 383 ||*

Settle the purified mind in the Self, the Witness, Knowledge Absolute. Bring it gradually to stillness, and then realise your own infinite Self.

### 384

<vt>Continuous Attention to Self</vt>  
देहेन्द्रियप्राणमनोऽहमादिभिः स्वाज्ञानकॢप्तैरखिलैरुपाधिभिः ।  
विमुक्तमात्मानमखण्डरूपं पूर्णं महाकाशमिवावलोकयेत् ॥ ३८४ ॥

*<vt>Continuous Attention to Self</vt>  
dehendriyaprāṇamano'hamādibhiḥ svājñānakḷptairakhilairupādhibhiḥ |  
vimuktamātmānamakhaṇḍarūpaṃ pūrṇaṃ mahākāśamivāvalokayet || 384 ||*

One should see the Atman, indivisible and infinite like the boundless sky, free of all limiting adjuncts such as the body, organs, Pranas, Manas, and egoism, all of which are products of one's own ignorance.

### 385

घटकलशकुसूलसूचिमुख्यैः गगनमुपाधिशतैर्विमुक्तमेकम् ।  
भवति न विविधं तथैव शुद्धं परमहमादिविमुक्तमेकमेव ॥ ३८५ ॥

*ghaṭakalaśakusūlasūcimukhyaiḥ gaganamupādhiśatairvimuktamekam |  
bhavati na vividhaṃ tathaiva śuddhaṃ paramahamādivimuktamekameva || 385 ||*

Take away the hundreds of limiting adjuncts such as a jar, a pitcher, a grain bin, or a needle, and the sky is one, not many. In exactly the same way, the pure Brahman, once egoism and the rest are taken away, is truly One.

### 386

ब्रह्मादिस्तम्बपर्यन्ता मृषामात्रा उपाधयः ।  
ततः पूर्णं स्वमात्मानं पश्येदेकात्मना स्थितम् ॥ ३८६ ॥

*brahmādistambaparyantā mṛṣāmātrā upādhayaḥ |  
tataḥ pūrṇaṃ svamātmānaṃ paśyedekātmanā sthitam || 386 ||*

All limiting adjuncts, from Brahma down to a clump of grass, are entirely unreal. So one should realise one's own Infinite Self as the sole Principle.

### 387

यत्र भ्रान्त्या कल्पितं तद्विवेके तत्तन्मात्रं नैव तस्माद्विभिन्नम् ।  
भ्रान्तेर्नाशे भाति दृष्टाहितत्त्वं रज्जुस्तद्वद्विश्वमात्मस्वरूपम् ॥ ३८७ ॥

*yatra bhrāntyā kalpitaṃ tadviveke tattanmātraṃ naiva tasmādvibhinnam |  
bhrānternāśe bhāti dṛṣṭāhitattvaṃ rajjustadvadviśvamātmasvarūpam || 387 ||*

Whatever a thing is mistakenly imagined to exist in turns out, on correct discrimination, to be that thing itself and nothing separate. When the mistake is gone, the truth about the falsely seen snake is that it is the rope. Likewise the universe is in reality the Atman.

### 388

स्वयं ब्रह्मा स्वयं विष्णुः स्वयमिन्द्रः स्वयं शिवः ।  
स्वयं विश्वमिदं सर्वं स्वस्मादन्यन्न किंचन ॥ ३८८ ॥

*svayaṃ brahmā svayaṃ viṣṇuḥ svayamindraḥ svayaṃ śivaḥ |  
svayaṃ viśvamidaṃ sarvaṃ svasmādanyanna kiṃcana || 388 ||*

The Self is Brahma, the Self is Vishnu, the Self is Indra, the Self is Shiva. The Self is this whole universe. Apart from the Self, nothing exists.

### 389

अन्तः स्वयं चापि बहिः स्वयं च स्वयं पुरस्तात्स्वयमेव पश्चात् ।  
स्वयं ह्यावाच्यां स्वयमप्युदीच्यां तथोपरिष्टात्स्वयमप्यधस्तात् ॥ ३८९ ॥

*antaḥ svayaṃ cāpi bahiḥ svayaṃ ca svayaṃ purastātsvayameva paścāt |  
svayaṃ hyāvācyāṃ svayamapyudīcyāṃ tathopariṣṭātsvayamapyadhastāt || 389 ||*

The Self is inside and the Self is outside. The Self is in front and the Self is behind. The Self is to the south and the Self is to the north. So too the Self is above and below.

### 390

तरङ्गफेनभ्रमबुद्बुदादि सर्वं स्वरूपेण जलं यथा तथा ।  
चिदेव देहाद्यहमन्तमेतत् सर्वं चिदेवैकरसं विशुद्धम् ॥ ३९0 ॥

*taraṅgaphenabhramabudbudādi sarvaṃ svarūpeṇa jalaṃ yathā tathā |  
cideva dehādyahamantametat sarvaṃ cidevaikarasaṃ viśuddham || 390 ||*

Waves, foam, whirlpools, bubbles, and the like are all water in essence. In the same way, Chit (Knowledge Absolute) is all this, from the body up to egoism. Everything is truly Chit, uniform and pure.

### 391

सदेवेदं सर्वं जगदवगतं वाङ्मनसयोः सतोऽन्यन्नास्त्येव प्रकृतिपरसीम्नि स्थितवतः ।  
पृथक्किं मृत्स्नायाः कलशघटकुम्भाद्यवगतं वदत्येष भ्रान्तस्त्वमहमिति मायामदिरया ॥ ३९१ ॥

*sadevedaṃ sarvaṃ jagadavagataṃ vāṅmanasayoḥ sato'nyannāstyeva prakṛtiparasīmni sthitavataḥ |  
pṛthakkiṃ mṛtsnāyāḥ kalaśaghaṭakumbhādyavagataṃ vadatyeṣa bhrāntastvamahamiti māyāmadirayā || 391 ||*

This entire universe that speech and mind can reach is nothing but Brahman. Beyond the furthest limit of Prakriti there exists Brahman, and besides It there is nothing. Are a pitcher, a jug, and a jar known to be separate from the clay they are made of? It is the deluded person, drunk on the wine of Maya, who speaks of 'you' and 'I'.

### 392

क्रियासमभिहारेण यत्र नान्यदिति श्रुतिः ।  
ब्रवीति द्वैतराहित्यं मिथ्याध्यासनिवृत्तये ॥ ३९२ ॥

*kriyāsamabhihāreṇa yatra nānyaditi śrutiḥ |  
bravīti dvaitarāhityaṃ mithyādhyāsanivṛttaye || 392 ||*

In the passage beginning 'Where one sees nothing else,' the Sruti piles up verbs to declare that there is no duality, so as to remove false superimpositions.

### 393

आकाशवन्निर्मलनिर्विकल्पं निःसीमनिःस्पन्दननिर्विकारम् ।  
अन्तर्बहिःशून्यमनन्यमद्वयं स्वयं परं ब्रह्म किमस्ति बोध्यम् ॥ ३९३ ॥

*ākāśavannirmalanirvikalpaṃ niḥsīmaniḥspandananirvikāram |  
antarbahiḥśūnyamananyamadvayaṃ svayaṃ paraṃ brahma kimasti bodhyam || 393 ||*

The Supreme Brahman is like the sky: pure, absolute, infinite, motionless, and changeless. It has no inside or outside. It is the One Existence without a second, and It is one's own Self. Is there anything else to be known?

### 394

वक्तव्यं किमु विद्यतेऽत्र बहुधा ब्रह्मैव जीवः स्वयं ब्रह्मैतज्जगदाततं नु सकलं ब्रह्माद्वितीयं श्रुतिः ।  
ब्रह्मैवाहमिति प्रबुद्धमतयः संत्यक्तबाह्याः स्फुटं ब्रह्मीभूय वसन्ति सन्ततचिदानन्दात्मनैतद्ध्रुवम् ॥ ३९४ ॥

*vaktavyaṃ kimu vidyate'tra bahudhā brahmaiva jīvaḥ svayaṃ brahmaitajjagadātataṃ nu sakalaṃ brahmādvitīyaṃ śrutiḥ |  
brahmaivāhamiti prabuddhamatayaḥ saṃtyaktabāhyāḥ sphuṭaṃ brahmībhūya vasanti santatacidānandātmanaitaddhruvam || 394 ||*

What is the use of saying more on this subject? The Jiva is nothing other than Brahman. This whole expanse of the universe is Brahman Itself. The Sruti teaches the Brahman without a second. And it is beyond doubt that enlightened people who know their identity with Brahman and have cut their ties with the objective world live visibly as one with Brahman, Eternal Knowledge and Bliss.

### 395

जहि मलमायाकोशेऽहंधियोत्थापिताशां प्रसभमनिलकल्पे लिङ्गदेहेऽपि पश्चात् ।  
निगमगदितकीर्तिं नित्यमानन्दमूर्तिं स्वयमिति परिचीय ब्रह्मरूपेण तिष्ठ ॥ ३९५ ॥

*jahi malaMāyākośe'haṃdhiyotthāpitāśāṃ prasabhamanilakalpe liṅgadehe'pi paścāt |  
nigamagaditakīrtiṃ nityamānandamūrtiṃ svayamiti paricīya brahmarūpeṇa tiṣṭha || 395 ||*

First destroy the hopes that egoism has placed in this filthy gross body. Then do the same, with force, to the air-like subtle body. Realise as your own Self the Brahman who is eternal Bliss embodied, whose glories the Scriptures proclaim, and live as Brahman.

### 396

शवाकारं यावद्भजति मनुजस्तावदशुचिः परेभ्यः स्यात्क्लेशो जननमरणव्याधिनिलयः ।  
यदात्मानं शुद्धं कलयति शिवाकारमचलम् तदा तेभ्यो मुक्तो भवति हि तदाह श्रुतिरपि ॥ ३९६ ॥

*śavākāraṃ yāvadbhajati manujastāvadaśuciḥ parebhyaḥ syātkleśo jananamaraṇavyādhinilayaḥ |  
yadātmānaṃ śuddhaṃ kalayati śivākāramacalam tadā tebhyo mukto bhavati hi tadāha śrutirapi || 396 ||*

As long as a person has any regard for this corpse-like body, he is impure and suffers from his enemies and from birth, death, and disease. But when he thinks of himself as pure, as the essence of good and immovable, he certainly becomes free of them. The Srutis say this too.

### 397

स्वात्मन्यारोपिताशेषाभासर्वस्तुनिरासतः ।  
स्वयमेव परं ब्रह्म पूर्णमद्वयमक्रियम् ॥ ३९७ ॥

*svātmanyāropitāśeṣābhāsarvastunirāsataḥ |  
svayameva paraṃ brahma pūrṇamadvayamakriyam || 397 ||*

When all the apparent existences superimposed on the soul are eliminated, what remains as Itself is the supreme Brahman, Infinite, the One without a second, beyond action.

### 398

<vt>No Diversity in Reality</vt>  
समाहितायां सति चित्तवृत्तौ परात्मनि ब्रह्मणि निर्विकल्पे ।  
न दृश्यते कश्चिदयं विकल्पः प्रजल्पमात्रः परिशिष्यते यतः ॥ ३९८ ॥

*<vt>No Diversity in Reality</vt>  
samāhitāyāṃ sati cittavṛttau parātmani brahmaṇi nirvikalpe |  
na dṛśyate kaścidayaṃ vikalpaḥ prajalpamātraḥ pariśiṣyate yataḥ || 398 ||*

When the functions of the mind merge in the Paramatman, Brahman the Absolute, none of this phenomenal world is seen, and so it is reduced to mere talk.

### 399

असत्कल्पो विकल्पोऽयं विश्वमित्येकवस्तुनि ।  
निर्विकारे निराकारे निर्विशेषे भिदा कुतः ॥ ३९९ ॥

*asatkalpo vikalpo'yaṃ viśvamityekavastuni |  
nirvikāre nirākāre nirviśeṣe bhidā kutaḥ || 399 ||*

In the One Entity (Brahman), the idea of a universe is only a phantom. How could there be any diversity in That which is changeless, formless, and Absolute?

### 400

द्रष्टुदर्शनदृश्यादिभावशून्यैकवस्तुनि ।  
निर्विकारे निराकारे निर्विशेषे भिदा कुतः ॥ ४00 ॥

*draṣṭudarśanadṛśyādibhāvaśūnyaikavastuni |  
nirvikāre nirākāre nirviśeṣe bhidā kutaḥ || 400 ||*

In the One Entity, which has no notions of seer, seeing, and seen, and which is changeless, formless, and Absolute, how could there be any diversity?

### 401

कल्पार्णव इवात्यन्तपरिपूर्णैकवस्तुनि ।  
निर्विकारे निराकारे निर्विशेषे भिदा कुतः ॥ ४0१ ॥

*kalpārṇava ivātyantaparipūrṇaikavastuni |  
nirvikāre nirākāre nirviśeṣe bhidā kutaḥ || 401 ||*

In the One Entity, changeless, formless, and Absolute, wholly all-pervading and motionless like the ocean after the universe has dissolved, how could there be any diversity?

### 402

तेजसीव तमो यत्र प्रलीनं भ्रान्तिकारणम् ।  
अद्वितीये परे तत्त्वे निर्विशेषे भिदा कुतः ॥ ४0२ ॥

*tejasīva tamo yatra pralīnaṃ bhrāntikāraṇam |  
advitīye pare tattve nirviśeṣe bhidā kutaḥ || 402 ||*

In the supreme Reality, the One without a second, the Absolute, where the root of delusion melts away as darkness does in light, how could there be any diversity?

### 403

एकात्मके परे तत्त्वे भेदवार्ता कथं वसेत् ।  
सुषुप्तौ सुखमात्रायां भेदः केनावलोकितः ॥ ४0३ ॥

*ekātmake pare tattve bhedavārtā kathaṃ vaset |  
suṣuptau sukhamātrāyāṃ bhedaḥ kenāvalokitaḥ || 403 ||*

How can talk of diversity apply to the Supreme Reality, which is one and uniform? Who has ever noticed diversity in the unmixed bliss of deep sleep?

### 404

न ह्यस्ति विश्वं परतत्त्वबोधात् सदात्मनि ब्रह्मणि निर्विकल्पे ।  
कालत्रये नाप्यहिरीक्षितो गुणे न ह्यम्बुबिन्दुर्मृगतृष्णिकायाम् ॥ ४0४ ॥

*na hyasti viśvaṃ paratattvabodhāt sadātmani brahmaṇi nirvikalpe |  
kālatraye nāpyahirīkṣito guṇe na hyambubindurmṛgatṛṣṇikāyām || 404 ||*

Even before the highest Truth is realised, the universe does not exist in the Absolute Brahman, the Essence of Existence. At no point in past, present, or future is the snake ever found in the rope or a drop of water in the mirage.

### 405

मायामात्रमिदं द्वैतमद्वैतं परमार्थतः ।  
इति ब्रूते श्रुतिः साक्षात्सुषुप्तावनुभूयते ॥ ४0५ ॥

*māyāmātramidaṃ dvaitamadvaitaṃ paramārthataḥ |  
iti brūte śrutiḥ sākṣātsuṣuptāvanubhūyate || 405 ||*

The Srutis themselves declare that, from the standpoint of Absolute Truth, this dualistic universe is only a delusion. One experiences this also in dreamless sleep.

### 406

अनन्यत्वमधिष्ठानादारोप्यस्य निरीक्षितम् ।  
पण्डितै रज्जुसर्पादौ विकल्पो भ्रान्तिजीवनः ॥ ४0६ ॥

*ananyatvamadhiṣṭhānādāropyasya nirīkṣitam |  
paṇḍitai rajjusarpādau vikalpo bhrāntijīvanaḥ || 406 ||*

The wise see that whatever is superimposed on something else is identical with its substratum, as with the rope that appears as a snake. The apparent difference rests entirely on error.

### 407

<vt>Ātma-vicāra - Contemplation</vt>  
चित्तमूलो विकल्पोऽयं चित्ताभावे न कश्चन ।  
अतश्चित्तं समाधेहि प्रत्यग्रूपे परात्मनि ॥ ४0७ ॥

*<vt>Ātma-vicāra - Contemplation</vt>  
cittamūlo vikalpo'yaṃ cittābhāve na kaścana |  
ataścittaṃ samādhehi pratyagrūpe parātmani || 407 ||*

This apparent universe is rooted in the mind and never continues once the mind is destroyed. So dissolve the mind by concentrating it on the Supreme Self, your inmost Essence.

### 408

किमपि सततबोधं केवलानन्दरूपं निरुपममतिवेलं नित्यमुक्तं निरीहम् ।  
निरवधिगगनाभं निष्कलं निर्विकल्पं हृदि कलयति विद्वान् ब्रह्म पूर्णं समाधौ ॥ ४0८ ॥

*kimapi satatabodhaṃ kevalānandarūpaṃ nirupamamativelaṃ nityamuktaṃ nirīham |  
niravadhigaganābhaṃ niṣkalaṃ nirvikalpaṃ hṛdi kalayati vidvān brahma pūrṇaṃ samādhau || 408 ||*

Through Samadhi, the wise person realises in the heart the Infinite Brahman, whose nature is eternal Knowledge and absolute Bliss, which has no parallel, goes beyond all limits, is always free and without activity, and is, like the boundless sky, indivisible and absolute.

### 409

प्रकृतिविकृतिशून्यं भावनातीतभावं समरसमसमानं मानसं बन्धदूरम् ।  
निगमवचनसिद्धं नित्यमस्मत्प्रसिद्धं हृदि कलयति विद्वान् ब्रह्म पूर्णं समाधौ ॥ ४0९ ॥

*prakṛtivikṛtiśūnyaṃ bhāvanātītabhāvaṃ samarasamasamānaṃ mānasaṃ bandhadūram |  
nigamavacanasiddhaṃ nityamasmatprasiddhaṃ hṛdi kalayati vidvān brahma pūrṇaṃ samādhau || 409 ||*

Through Samadhi, the wise person realises in the heart the Infinite Brahman, which has no notions of cause and effect, is the Reality beyond all imaginings, uniform and matchless, outside the reach of proofs, established by the statements of the Vedas, and always familiar to us as the sense of the ego.

### 410

अजरममरमस्ताभाववस्तुस्वरूपं स्तिमितसलिलराशिप्रख्यमाख्याविहीनम् ।  
शमितगुणविकारं शाश्वतं शान्तमेकं हृदि कलयति विद्वान् ब्रह्म पूर्णं समाधौ ॥ ४१0 ॥

*ajaramamaramastābhāvavastusvarūpaṃ stimitasalilarāśiprakhyamākhyāvihīnam |  
śamitaguṇavikāraṃ śāśvataṃ śāntamekaṃ hṛdi kalayati vidvān brahma pūrṇaṃ samādhau || 410 ||*

Through Samadhi, the wise person realises in the heart the Infinite Brahman, undecaying and immortal, the positive Entity that rules out all negations, like a calm ocean and without a name, in which there is neither merit nor demerit, and which is eternal, at peace, and One.

### 411

समाहितान्तःकरणः स्वरूपे विलोकयात्मानमखण्डवैभवम् ।  
विच्छिन्द्धि बन्धं भवगन्धगन्धितं यत्नेन पुंस्त्वं सफलीकुरुष्व ॥ ४११ ॥

*samāhitāntaḥkaraṇaḥ svarūpe vilokayātmānamakhaṇḍavaibhavam |  
vicchinddhi bandhaṃ bhavagandhagandhitaṃ yatnena puṃstvaṃ saphalīkuruṣva || 411 ||*

With the mind held in Samadhi, see within yourself the Atman of infinite glory. Cut through the bondage made strong by the impressions of past births, and with care bring your birth as a human being to its fulfilment.

### 412

सर्वोपाधिविनिर्मुक्तं सच्चिदानन्दमद्वयम् ।  
भावयात्मानमात्मस्थं न भूयः कल्पसेऽध्वने ॥ ४१२ ॥

*sarvopādhivinirmuktaṃ saccidānandamadvayam |  
bhāvayātmānamātmasthaṃ na bhūyaḥ kalpase'dhvane || 412 ||*

Meditate on the Atman dwelling in you, free of all limiting adjuncts, Existence-Knowledge-Bliss Absolute, the One without a second, and you will never again be caught in the cycle of births and deaths.

### 413

छायेव पुंसः परिदृश्यमान् माभासरूपेण फलानुभूत्या ।  
शरीरमाराच्छववन्निरस्तं पुनर्न संधत्त इदं महात्मा ॥ ४१३ ॥

*chāyeva puṃsaḥ paridṛśyamān mābhāsarūpeṇa phalānubhūtyā |  
śarīramārācchavavannirastaṃ punarna saṃdhatta idaṃ mahātmā || 413 ||*

Once the sage has thrown the body far away like a corpse, he never again attaches himself to it, even though it remains visible as an appearance, like a person's shadow, because the effects of past deeds are still being experienced.

### 414

<vt>Give up Perceptions</vt>  
सततविमलबोधानन्दरूपं समेत्य त्यज जडमलरूपोपाधिमेतं सुदूरे ।  
अथ पुनरपि नैष स्मर्यतां वान्तवस्तु स्मरणविषयभूतं पल्पते कुत्सनाय ॥ ४१४ ॥

*<vt>Give up Perceptions</vt>  
satatavimalabodhānandarūpaṃ sametya tyaja jaḍamalarūpopādhimetaṃ sudūre |  
atha punarapi naiṣa smaryatāṃ vāntavastu smaraṇaviṣayabhūtaṃ palpate kutsanāya || 414 ||*

Realise the Atman, eternal and pure Knowledge and Bliss, and throw far away this limitation of a body, inert and filthy by nature. Then never think of it again, for something vomited brings only disgust when recalled.

### 415

समूलमेतत्परिदाह्य वन्हौ सदात्मनि ब्रह्मणि निर्विकल्पे ।  
ततः स्वयं नित्यविशुद्धबोधा नन्दात्मना तिष्ठति विद्वरिष्ठः ॥ ४१५ ॥

*samūlametatparidāhya vanhau sadātmani brahmaṇi nirvikalpe |  
tataḥ svayaṃ nityaviśuddhabodhā nandātmanā tiṣṭhati vidvariṣṭhaḥ || 415 ||*

Having burned all this down to its very root in the fire of Brahman, the Eternal and Absolute Self, the truly wise person from then on remains alone as the Atman, eternal and pure Knowledge and Bliss.

### 416

प्रारब्धसूत्रग्रथितं शरीरं प्रयातु वा तिष्ठतु गोरिव स्रक् ।  
न तत्पुनः पश्यति तत्त्ववेत्ता (आ)नन्दात्मनि ब्रह्मणि लीनवृत्तिः ॥ ४१६ ॥

*prārabdhasūtragrathitaṃ śarīraṃ prayātu vā tiṣṭhatu goriva srak |  
na tatpunaḥ paśyati tattvavettā (ā)nandātmani brahmaṇi līnavṛttiḥ || 416 ||*

The knower of Truth no longer cares whether this body, spun out by the threads of Prarabdha work, drops away or stays, any more than one cares for the garland on a cow, because the functions of his mind are at rest in Brahman, the Essence of Bliss.

### 417

अखण्डानन्दमात्मानं विज्ञाय स्वस्वरूपतः ।  
किमिच्छन् कस्य वा हेतोर्देहं पुष्णाति तत्त्ववित् ॥ ४१७ ॥

*akhaṇḍānandamātmānaṃ vijñāya svasvarūpataḥ |  
kimicchan kasya vā hetordehaṃ puṣṇāti tattvavit || 417 ||*

Having realised the Atman, Infinite Bliss, as his very Self, for what purpose or for whom should the knower of Truth look after the body?

### 418

संसिद्धस्य फलं त्वेतज्जीवन्मुक्तस्य योगिनः ।  
बहिरन्तः सदानन्दरसास्वादनमात्मनि ॥ ४१८ ॥

*saṃsiddhasya phalaṃ tvetajjīvanmuktasya yoginaḥ |  
bahirantaḥ sadānandarasāsvādanamātmani || 418 ||*

The result gained by the Yogi who has reached perfection and is liberated while living is this: in his mind he enjoys eternal Bliss, within and without.

### 419

<vt>The Science of Reality - Its Benefits</vt>  
वैराग्यस्य फलं बोधो बोधस्योपरतिः फलम् ।  
स्वानन्दानुभवाच्छान्तिरेषैवोपरतेः फलम् ॥ ४१९ ॥

*<vt>The Science of Reality - Its Benefits</vt>  
vairāgyasya phalaṃ bodho bodhasyoparatiḥ phalam |  
svānandānubhavācchāntireṣaivoparateḥ phalam || 419 ||*

Dispassion results in knowledge. Knowledge results in withdrawal from sense-pleasures. That leads to experiencing the Bliss of the Self, and from this comes Peace.

### 420

यद्युत्तरोत्तराभावः पूर्वपूर्वन्तु निष्फलम् ।  
निवृत्तिः परमा तृप्तिरानन्दोऽनुपमः स्वतः ॥ ४२0 ॥

*yadyuttarottarābhāvaḥ pūrvapūrvantu niṣphalam |  
nivṛttiḥ paramā tṛptirānando'nupamaḥ svataḥ || 420 ||*

If the later stages are missing, the earlier ones are of no use. When the series is complete, the ceasing of the objective world, deep satisfaction, and unequalled bliss follow naturally.

### 421

दृष्टदुःखेष्वनुद्वेगो विद्यायाः प्रस्तुतं फलम् ।  
यत्कृतं भ्रान्तिवेलायां नाना कर्म जुगुप्सितम् पश्चान्नरो विवेकेन तत्कथं कर्तुमर्हति ॥ ४२१ ॥

*dṛṣṭaduḥkheṣvanudvego vidyāyāḥ prastutaṃ phalam |  
yatkṛtaṃ bhrāntivelāyāṃ nānā karma jugupsitam paścānnaro vivekena tatkathaṃ kartumarhati || 421 ||*

The result of knowledge being discussed here is that worldly troubles no longer disturb a person. How could someone who did many vile things while deluded go on doing them once he has gained discrimination?

### 422

विद्याफलं स्यादसतो निवृत्तिः प्रवृत्तिरज्ञानफलं तदीक्षितम् ।  
तज्ज्ञाज्ञयोर्यन्मृगतृष्णिकादौ नोचेद्विदां दृष्टफलं किमस्मात् ॥ ४२२ ॥

*vidyāphalaṃ syādasato nivṛttiḥ pravṛttirajñānaphalaṃ tadīkṣitam |  
tajjñājñayoryanmṛgatṛṣṇikādau nocedvidāṃ dṛṣṭaphalaṃ kimasmāt || 422 ||*

Knowledge should result in turning away from what is unreal, while attachment to such things comes from ignorance. We see this in the difference between someone who recognizes a mirage and the like, and someone who does not. Otherwise, what concrete result do the knowers of Brahman gain?

### 423

अज्ञानहृदयग्रन्थेर्विनाशो यद्यशेषतः ।  
अनिच्छोर्विषयः किं नु प्रवृत्तेः कारणं स्वतः ॥ ४२३ ॥

*ajñānahṛdayagranthervināśo yadyaśeṣataḥ |  
anicchorviṣayaḥ kiṃ nu pravṛtteḥ kāraṇaṃ svataḥ || 423 ||*

Once the knot of ignorance in the heart is completely destroyed, what natural cause could prompt such a person to act selfishly, when he has no interest in sense-pleasures?

### 424

वासनानुदयो भोग्ये वैरागस्य तदावधिः ।  
अहंभावोदयाभावो बोधस्य परमावधिः लीनवृत्तैरनुत्पत्तिर्मर्यादोपरतेस्तु सा ॥ ४२४ ॥

*vāsanānudayo bhogye vairāgasya tadāvadhiḥ |  
ahaṃbhāvodayābhāvo bodhasya paramāvadhiḥ līnavṛttairanutpattirmaryādoparatestu sā || 424 ||*

Dispassion reaches its peak when sense-objects no longer stir any desire. Knowledge is at its ultimate perfection when the ego-idea no longer prompts anything. Self-withdrawal reaches its limit when mental functions that have merged do not reappear.

### 425

ब्रह्माकारतया सदा स्थिततया निर्मुक्तबाह्यार्थधीर् अन्यावेदितभोग्यभोगकलनो निद्रालुवद्बालवत् ।  
स्वप्नालोकितलोकवज्जगदिदं पश्यन् क्वचिल्लब्धधीर् आस्ते कश्चिदनन्तपुण्यफलभुग्धन्यः स मान्यो भुवि ॥ ४२५ ॥

*brahmākāratayā sadā sthitatayā nirmuktabāhyārthadhīr anyāveditabhogyabhogakalano nidrāluvadbālavat |  
svapnālokitalokavajjagadidaṃ paśyan kvacillabdhadhīr āste kaścidanantapuṇyaphalabhugdhanyaḥ sa mānyo bhuvi || 425 ||*

Because he remains constantly merged in Brahman, he no longer takes external sense-objects to be real. He only seems to enjoy the sense-objects others offer him, like someone drowsy or like a child. He sees this world as if it were seen in a dream, and notices it only at odd moments. Such a person is rare indeed. He enjoys the fruits of endless merit, and he alone is blessed and honored on earth.

### 426

<vt>Signs of a Realised Seer</vt>  
स्थितप्रज्ञो यतिरयं यः सदानन्दमश्नुते ।  
ब्रह्मण्येव विलीनात्मा निर्विकारो विनिष्क्रियः ॥ ४२६ ॥

*<vt>Signs of a Realised Seer</vt>  
sthitaprajño yatirayaṃ yaḥ sadānandamaśnute |  
brahmaṇyeva vilīnātmā nirvikāro viniṣkriyaḥ || 426 ||*

A Sannyasin has steady illumination when his soul is wholly merged in Brahman and he enjoys eternal bliss, unchanging and free from activity.

### 427

ब्रह्मात्मनोः शोधितयोरेकभावावगाहिनी ।  
निर्विकल्पा च चिन्मात्रा वृत्तिः प्रज्ञेति कथ्यते सुस्थितासौ भवेद्यस्य स्थितप्रज्ञः स उच्यते ॥ ४२७ ॥

*brahmātmanoḥ śodhitayorekabhāvāvagāhinī |  
nirvikalpā ca cinmātrā vṛttiḥ prajñeti kathyate susthitāsau bhavedyasya sthitaprajñaḥ sa ucyate || 427 ||*

Illumination is the kind of mental function that recognizes only the identity of the Self and Brahman, cleansed of all adjuncts, free from duality and concerned solely with Pure Intelligence. Someone in whom this is perfectly steady is called a person of steady illumination.

### 428

यस्य स्थिता भवेत्प्रज्ञा यस्यानन्दो निरन्तरः ।  
प्रपञ्चो विस्मृतप्रायः स जीवन्मुक्त इष्यते ॥ ४२८ ॥

*yasya sthitā bhavetprajñā yasyānando nirantaraḥ |  
prapañco vismṛtaprāyaḥ sa jīvanmukta iṣyate || 428 ||*

One whose illumination is steady, whose bliss is constant, and who has all but forgotten the phenomenal universe is accepted as liberated in this very life.

### 429

लीनधीरपि जागर्ति जाग्रद्धर्मविवर्जितः ।  
बोधो निर्वासनो यस्य स जीवन्मुक्त इष्यते ॥ ४२९ ॥

*līnadhīrapi jāgarti jāgraddharmavivarjitaḥ |  
bodho nirvāsano yasya sa jīvanmukta iṣyate || 429 ||*

One whose mind is merged in Brahman and who is yet fully alert, though without the characteristics of the waking state, and whose realization is free from desires, is accepted as liberated in life.

### 430

शान्तसंसारकलनः कलावानपि निष्कलः ।  
यस्य चित्तं विनिश्चिन्तं स जीवन्मुक्त इष्यते ॥ ४३0 ॥

*śāntasaṃsārakalanaḥ kalāvānapi niṣkalaḥ |  
yasya cittaṃ viniścintaṃ sa jīvanmukta iṣyate || 430 ||*

One whose concerns about the phenomenal state have been stilled, who has no parts even though his body consists of parts, and whose mind is free from anxiety, is accepted as liberated in life.

### 431

वर्तमानेऽपि देहेऽस्मिञ्छायावदनुवर्तिनि ।  
अहन्ताममताभावो जीवन्मुक्तस्य लक्षणम् ॥ ४३१ ॥

*vartamāne'pi dehe'smiñchāyāvadanuvartini |  
ahantāmamatābhāvo jīvanmuktasya lakṣaṇam || 431 ||*

A mark of someone liberated in life is having no sense of "I" and "mine," even in this present body that follows along like a shadow.

### 432

अतीताननुसन्धानं भविष्यदविचारणम् ।  
अउदासीन्यमपि प्राप्तं जीवन्मुक्तस्य लक्षणम् ॥ ४३२ ॥

*atītānanusandhānaṃ bhaviṣyadavicāraṇam |  
audāsīnyamapi prāptaṃ jīvanmuktasya lakṣaṇam || 432 ||*

Marks of someone liberated in life are not lingering over past enjoyments, giving no thought to the future, and regarding the present with indifference.

### 433

गुणदोषविशिष्टेऽस्मिन्स्वभावेन विलक्षणे ।  
सर्वत्र समदर्शित्वं जीवन्मुक्तस्य लक्षणम् ॥ ४३३ ॥

*guṇadoṣaviśiṣṭe'sminsvabhāvena vilakṣaṇe |  
sarvatra samadarśitvaṃ jīvanmuktasya lakṣaṇam || 433 ||*

A mark of someone liberated in life is looking at everything with an equal eye in this world, which is full of elements that have merits and defects and that differ from one another by nature.

### 434

इष्टानिष्टार्थसम्प्राप्तौ समदर्शितयात्मनि ।  
उभयत्राविकारित्वं जीवन्मुक्तस्य लक्षणम् ॥ ४३४ ॥

*iṣṭāniṣṭārthasamprāptau samadarśitayātmani |  
ubhayatrāvikāritvaṃ jīvanmuktasya lakṣaṇam || 434 ||*

A mark of someone liberated in life is staying undisturbed when pleasant or painful things arise, with the same attitude toward both.

### 435

ब्रह्मानन्दरसास्वादासक्तचित्ततया यतेः ।  
अन्तर्बहिरविज्ञानं जीवन्मुक्तस्य लक्षणम् ॥ ४३५ ॥

*brahmānandarasāsvādāsaktacittatayā yateḥ |  
antarbahiravijñānaṃ jīvanmuktasya lakṣaṇam || 435 ||*

A mark of someone liberated in life is that a Sannyasin has no notion at all of inner or outer, because his mind is absorbed in savoring the bliss of Brahman.

### 436

देहेन्द्रियादौ कर्तव्ये ममाहंभाववर्जितः ।  
अउदासीन्येन यस्तिष्ठेत्स जीवन्मुक्तलक्षणः ॥ ४३६ ॥

*dehendriyādau kartavye mamāhaṃbhāvavarjitaḥ |  
audāsīnyena yastiṣṭhetsa jīvanmuktalakṣaṇaḥ || 436 ||*

One who lives unconcerned, with no idea of "I" or "mine" in relation to the body, the organs and so on, or in relation to his duties, is known as liberated in life.

### 437

विज्ञात आत्मनो यस्य ब्रह्मभावः श्रुतेर्बलात् ।  
भवबन्धविनिर्मुक्तः स जीवन्मुक्तलक्षणः ॥ ४३७ ॥

*vijñāta ātmano yasya brahmabhāvaḥ śruterbalāt |  
bhavabandhavinirmuktaḥ sa jīvanmuktalakṣaṇaḥ || 437 ||*

One who has realized that he is Brahman, with the aid of the Scriptures, and who is free from the bondage of transmigration, is known as liberated in life.

### 438

देहेन्द्रियेष्वहंभाव इदंभावस्तदन्यके ।  
यस्य नो भवतः क्वापि स जीवन्मुक्त इष्यते ॥ ४३८ ॥

*dehendriyeṣvahaṃbhāva idaṃbhāvastadanyake |  
yasya no bhavataḥ kvāpi sa jīvanmukta iṣyate || 438 ||*

One who never thinks "I" about the body, organs and so on, and never thinks "it" about what is other than these, is accepted as liberated in life.

### 439

न प्रत्यग्ब्रह्मणोर्भेदं कदापि ब्रह्मसर्गयोः ।  
प्रज्ञया यो विजानिति स जीवन्मुक्तलक्षणः ॥ ४३९ ॥

*na pratyagbrahmaṇorbhedaṃ kadāpi brahmasargayoḥ |  
prajñayā yo vijāniti sa jīvanmuktalakṣaṇaḥ || 439 ||*

One whose illumination means he never draws a distinction between the Jiva and Brahman, or between the universe and Brahman, is known as liberated in life.

### 440

साधुभिः पूज्यमानेऽस्मिन् पीड्यमानेऽपि दुर्जनैः ।  
समभावो भवेद्यस्य स जीवन्मुक्तलक्षणः ॥ ४४0 ॥

*sādhubhiḥ pūjyamāne'smin pīḍyamāne'pi durjanaiḥ |  
samabhāvo bhavedyasya sa jīvanmuktalakṣaṇaḥ || 440 ||*

One who feels exactly the same whether the good worship his body or the wicked torment it is known as liberated in life.

### 441

यत्र प्रविष्टा विषयाः परेरिता नदीप्रवाहा इव वारिराशौ ।  
लिनन्ति सन्मात्रतया न विक्रियां उत्पादयन्त्येष यतिर्विमुक्तः ॥ ४४१ ॥

*yatra praviṣṭā viṣayāḥ pareritā nadīpravāhā iva vārirāśau |  
linanti sanmātratayā na vikriyāṃ utpādayantyeṣa yatirvimuktaḥ || 441 ||*

A Sannyasin is truly liberated when the sense-objects others direct toward him are swallowed up in him as rivers are in the sea and cause no change, because he is one with Existence Absolute.

### 442

विज्ञातब्रह्मतत्त्वस्य यथापूर्वं न संसृतिः ।  
अस्ति चेन्न स विज्ञातब्रह्मभावो बहिर्मुखः ॥ ४४२ ॥

*vijñātabrahmatattvasya yathāpūrvaṃ na saṃsṛtiḥ |  
asti cenna sa vijñātabrahmabhāvo bahirmukhaḥ || 442 ||*

Someone who has realized the Truth of Brahman is no longer attached to sense-objects as he once was. If he is, then he has not realized his identity with Brahman, and his senses are still turned outward.

### 443

प्राचीनवासनावेगादसौ संसरतीति चेत् ।  
न सदेकत्वविज्ञानान्मन्दी भवति वासना ॥ ४४३ ॥

*prācīnavāsanāvegādasau saṃsaratīti cet |  
na sadekatvavijñānānmandī bhavati vāsanā || 443 ||*

If someone argues that he remains attached to sense-objects through the momentum of old desires, the answer is no, because desires grow weak through realizing one's identity with Brahman.

### 444

अत्यन्तकामुकस्यापि वृत्तिः कुण्ठति मातरि ।  
तथैव ब्रह्मणि ज्ञाते पूर्णानन्दे मनीषिणः ॥ ४४४ ॥

*atyantakāmukasyāpi vṛttiḥ kuṇṭhati mātari |  
tathaiva brahmaṇi jñāte pūrṇānande manīṣiṇaḥ || 444 ||*

Even a hardened libertine's urges are held in check in his mother's presence. In the same way, once Brahman, the Bliss Absolute, has been realized, the person of realization no longer has any worldly tendency.

### 445

निदिध्यासनशीलस्य बाह्यप्रत्यय ईक्ष्यते ।  
ब्रवीति श्रुतिरेतस्य प्रारब्धं फलदर्शनात् ॥ ४४५ ॥

*nididhyāsanaśīlasya bāhyapratyaya īkṣyate |  
bravīti śrutiretasya prārabdhaṃ phaladarśanāt || 445 ||*

Someone who practices meditation constantly is observed to have external perceptions. The Śrutis speak of Prārabdha work in such a person's case, and we can infer this from the results we actually see.

### 446

<vt>Prārabdha for a Saint</vt>  
सुखाद्यनुभवो यावत्तावत्प्रारब्धमिष्यते ।  
फलोदयः क्रियापूर्वो निष्क्रियो न हि कुत्रचित् ॥ ४४६ ॥

*<vt>Prārabdha for a Saint</vt>  
sukhādyanubhavo yāvattāvatprārabdhamiṣyate |  
phalodayaḥ kriyāpūrvo niṣkriyo na hi kutracit || 446 ||*

Prārabdha work is acknowledged to continue as long as happiness and the like are perceived. Every result comes after an action, and no result is ever seen to arise apart from action.

### 447

अहं ब्रह्मेति विज्ञानात्कल्पकोटिशतार्जितम् ।  
सञ्चितं विलयं याति प्रबोधात्स्वप्नकर्मवत् ॥ ४४७ ॥

*ahaṃ brahmeti vijñānātkalpakoṭiśatārjitam |  
sañcitaṃ vilayaṃ yāti prabodhātsvapnakarmavat || 447 ||*

Through realizing one's identity with Brahman, all the actions accumulated over a hundred crore of cycles come to nothing, like the actions of a dream on waking.

### 448

यत्कृतं स्वप्नवेलायां पुण्यं वा पापमुल्बणम् ।  
सुप्तोत्थितस्य किं तत्स्यात्स्वर्गाय नरकाय वा ॥ ४४८ ॥

*yatkṛtaṃ svapnavelāyāṃ puṇyaṃ vā pāpamulbaṇam |  
suptotthitasya kiṃ tatsyātsvargāya narakāya vā || 448 ||*

Can the good deeds or terrible sins a person imagines committing in a dream send him to heaven or hell once he has woken from sleep?

### 449

स्वमसङ्गमुदासीनं परिज्ञाय नभो यथा ।  
न श्लिष्यति च यत्किंचित्कदाचिद्भाविकर्मभिः ॥ ४४९ ॥

*svamasaṅgamudāsīnaṃ parijñāya nabho yathā |  
na śliṣyati ca yatkiṃcitkadācidbhāvikarmabhiḥ || 449 ||*

Having realized the Ātman, which is unattached and indifferent like the sky, the aspirant is never touched in the slightest by actions still to be done.

### 450

न नभो घटयोगेन सुरागन्धेन लिप्यते ।  
तथात्मोपाधियोगेन तद्धर्मैर्नैव लिप्यते ॥ ४५0 ॥

*na nabho ghaṭayogena surāgandhena lipyate |  
tathātmopādhiyogena taddharmairnaiva lipyate || 450 ||*

Merely by being connected with a jar, the sky is not affected by the smell of the liquor in it. Likewise the Ātman, through Its connection with the limitations, is not affected by their properties.

### 451

ज्ञानोदयात्पुरारब्धं कर्मज्ञानान्न नश्यति ।  
अदत्वा स्वफलं लक्ष्यमुद्दिश्योत्सृष्टबाणवत् ॥ ४५१ ॥

*jñānodayātpurārabdhaṃ karmajñānānna naśyati |  
adatvā svaphalaṃ lakṣyamuddiśyotsṛṣṭabāṇavat || 451 ||*

The work that shaped this body before knowledge dawned is not destroyed by that knowledge until it has yielded its fruits, like an arrow already shot at a target.

### 452

व्याघ्रबुद्ध्या विनिर्मुक्तो बाणः पश्चात्तु गोमतौ ।  
न तिष्ठति छिनत्येव लक्ष्यं वेगेन निर्भरम् ॥ ४५२ ॥

*vyāghrabuddhyā vinirmukto bāṇaḥ paścāttu gomatau |  
na tiṣṭhati chinatyeva lakṣyaṃ vegena nirbharam || 452 ||*

An arrow shot at something taken to be a tiger does not stop itself when the target turns out to be a cow; it pierces the target with full force.

### 453

प्राब्धं बलवत्तरं खलु विदां भोगेन तस्य क्षयः सम्यग्ज्ञानहुताशनेन विलयः प्राक्षंचितागामिनाम् ।  
ब्रह्मात्मैक्यमवेक्ष्य तन्मायातया ये सर्वदा संस्थिताः तेषां तत्त्रितयं नहि क्वचिदपि ब्रह्मैव ते निर्गुणम् ॥ ४५३ ॥

*prābdhaṃ balavattaraṃ khalu vidāṃ bhogena tasya kṣayaḥ samyagjñānahutāśanena vilayaḥ prākṣaṃcitāgāminām |  
brahmātmaikyamavekṣya tanMāyātayā ye sarvadā saṃsthitāḥ teṣāṃ tattritayaṃ nahi kvacidapi brahmaiva te nirguṇam || 453 ||*

For the person of realization, Prārabdha work is certainly very strong and is used up only by actually experiencing its fruit, while previously accumulated actions and those yet to come are destroyed by the fire of perfect knowledge. Yet none of the three affects in any way those who have realized their identity with Brahman and live always absorbed in that idea. They are truly the transcendent Brahman.

### 454

उपाधितादात्म्यविहीनकेवल ब्रह्मात्मनैवात्मनि तिष्ठतो मुनेः ।  
प्रारब्धसद्भावकथा न युक्ता स्वप्नार्थसंबन्धकथेव जाग्रतः ॥ ४५४ ॥

*upādhitādātmyavihīnakevala brahmātmanaivātmani tiṣṭhato muneḥ |  
prārabdhasadbhāvakathā na yuktā svapnārthasaṃbandhakatheva jāgrataḥ || 454 ||*

For the sage who lives in his own Self as Brahman, the One without a second, and who does not identify with the limiting adjuncts, asking whether Prārabdha work exists makes no sense. It would be like asking whether someone awake from sleep has any connection with the objects seen in a dream.

### 455

न हि प्रबुद्धः प्रतिभासदेहे देहोपयोगिन्यपि च प्रपञ्चे ।  
करोत्यहन्तां ममतानिदन्तां किन्तु स्वयं तिष्ठति जागरेण ॥ ४५५ ॥

*na hi prabuddhaḥ pratibhāsadehe dehopayoginyapi ca prapañce |  
karotyahantāṃ mamatānidantāṃ kintu svayaṃ tiṣṭhati jāgareṇa || 455 ||*

Someone awake from sleep never thinks "I" or "mine" about his dream-body and the dream-objects that served that body. He lives fully awake, as his own Self.

### 456

न तस्य मिथ्यार्थसमर्थनेच्छा न संग्रहस्तज्जगतोऽपि दृष्टः ।  
तत्रानुवृत्तिर्यदि चेन्मृषार्थे न निद्रया मुक्त इतीष्यते ध्रुवम् ॥ ४५६ ॥

*na tasya mithyārthasamarthanecchā na saṃgrahastajjagato'pi dṛṣṭaḥ |  
tatrānuvṛttiryadi cenmṛṣārthe na nidrayā mukta itīṣyate dhruvam || 456 ||*

He has no wish to make the unreal objects real, and he is not seen keeping up that dream-world. If he still clings to those unreal objects, it is stated firmly that he has not yet left sleep behind.

### 457

तद्वत्परे ब्रह्मणि वर्तमानः सदात्मना तिष्ठति नान्यदीक्षते ।  
स्मृतिर्यथा स्वप्नविलोकितार्थे तथा विदः प्राशनमोचनादौ ॥ ४५७ ॥

*tadvatpare brahmaṇi vartamānaḥ sadātmanā tiṣṭhati nānyadīkṣate |  
smṛtiryathā svapnavilokitārthe tathā vidaḥ prāśanamocanādau || 457 ||*

In the same way, one absorbed in Brahman lives identified with that eternal Reality and sees nothing else. Just as one remembers things seen in a dream, the person of realization has a memory of everyday actions such as eating.

### 458

कर्मणा निर्मितो देहः प्रारब्धं तस्य कल्प्यताम् ।  
नानादेरात्मनो युक्तं नैवात्मा कर्मनिर्मितः ॥ ४५८ ॥

*karmaṇā nirmito dehaḥ prārabdhaṃ tasya kalpyatām |  
nānāderātmano yuktaṃ naivātmā karmanirmitaḥ || 458 ||*

Karma has shaped the body, so one may imagine Prarabdha work in relation to it. But attributing the same to the Ātman is unreasonable, since the Ātman is never the product of work.

### 459

अजो नित्यः शाश्वत इति ब्रूते श्रुतिरमोघवाक् ।  
तदात्मना तिष्ठतोऽस्य कुतः प्रारब्धकल्पना ॥ ४५९ ॥

*ajo nityaḥ śāśvata iti brūte śrutiramoghavāk |  
tadātmanā tiṣṭhato'sya kutaḥ prārabdhakalpanā || 459 ||*

The Śrutis, whose words cannot err, declare the Ātman to be "birthless, eternal and undecaying." So how can Prārabdha work be attributed to the person who lives identified with That?

### 460

प्रारब्धं सिध्यति तदा यदा देहात्मना स्थितिः ।  
देहात्मभावो नैवेष्टः प्रारब्धं त्यज्यतामतः ॥ ४६0 ॥

*prārabdhaṃ sidhyati tadā yadā dehātmanā sthitiḥ |  
dehātmabhāvo naiveṣṭaḥ prārabdhaṃ tyajyatāmataḥ || 460 ||*

Prārabdha work can be upheld only so long as a person lives identified with the body. But no one claims that the person of realization ever identifies with the body. So Prārabdha work should be rejected in his case.

### 461

शरीरस्यापि प्रारब्धकल्पना भ्रान्तिरेव हि ।  
अध्यस्तस्य कुतः सत्त्वमसत्यस्य कुतो जनिः अजातस्य कुतो नाशः प्रारब्धमसतः कुतः ॥ ४६१ ॥

*śarīrasyāpi prārabdhakalpanā bhrāntireva hi |  
adhyastasya kutaḥ sattvamasatyasya kuto janiḥ ajātasya kuto nāśaḥ prārabdhamasataḥ kutaḥ || 461 ||*

Attributing Prārabdha work even to the body is certainly a mistake. How can something superimposed on another have any existence, and how can what is unreal be born? And how can what was never born die? So how can Prārabdha work exist for something unreal?

### 462

ज्ञानेनाज्ञानकार्यस्य समूलस्य लयो यदि ।  
तिष्ठत्ययं कथं देह इति शङ्कावतो जडान् ॥ ४६२ ॥

*jñānenājñānakāryasya samūlasya layo yadi |  
tiṣṭhatyayaṃ kathaṃ deha iti śaṅkāvato jaḍān || 462 ||*

Some fools raise this doubt: "If knowledge destroys the effects of ignorance along with their root, how does the body go on living?" It is to convince them that the Śrutis, from a relative standpoint, posit Prārabdha work, and not to prove that the body and so on of the person of realization are real.

### 463

समाधातुं बाह्यदृष्ट्या प्रारब्धं वदति श्रुतिः ।  
न तु देहादिसत्यत्वबोधनाय विपश्चिताम् ॥ ४६३ ॥

*samādhātuṃ bāhyadṛṣṭyā prārabdhaṃ vadati śrutiḥ |  
na tu dehādisatyatvabodhanāya vipaścitām || 463 ||*

Some fools raise this doubt: "If knowledge destroys the effects of ignorance along with their root, how does the body go on living?" It is to convince them that the Śrutis, from a relative standpoint, posit Prārabdha work, and not to prove that the body and so on of the person of realization are real.

### 464

परिपूर्णमनाद्यन्तमप्रमेयमविक्रियम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४६४ ॥

*paripūrṇamanādyantamaprameyamavikriyam |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 464 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: infinite, without beginning or end, transcendent and changeless. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 465

<vt>There is No Plurality</vt>  
सद्गनं चिद्घनं नित्यमानन्दघनमक्रियम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४६५ ॥

*<vt>There is No Plurality</vt>  
sadganaṃ cidghanaṃ nityamānandaghanamakriyam |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 465 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: the Essence of Existence, Knowledge and Eternal Bliss, and free of activity. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 466

प्रत्यगेकरसं पूर्णमनन्तं सर्वतोमुखम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४६६ ॥

*pratyagekarasaṃ pūrṇamanantaṃ sarvatomukham |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 466 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: present within everything, homogeneous, infinite, endless and all-pervading. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 467

अहेयमनुपादेयमनादेयमनाश्रयम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४६७ ॥

*aheyamanupādeyamanādeyamanāśrayam |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 467 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: It cannot be shunned, taken up or accepted, and It rests on no support. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 468

निर्गुणं निष्कलं सूक्ष्मं निर्विकल्पं निरञ्जनम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४६८ ॥

*nirguṇaṃ niṣkalaṃ sūkṣmaṃ nirvikalpaṃ nirañjanam |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 468 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: beyond attributes, without parts, subtle, absolute and stainless. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 469

अनिरूप्य स्वरूपं यन्मनोवाचामगोचरम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४६९ ॥

*anirūpya svarūpaṃ yanmanovācāmagocaram |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 469 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: Its real nature cannot be comprehended, and It lies beyond the reach of mind and speech. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 470

सत्समृद्धं स्वतःसिद्धं शुद्धं बुद्धमनीदृशम् ।  
एकमेवाद्वयं ब्रह्म नेह नानास्ति किंचन ॥ ४७0 ॥

*satsamṛddhaṃ svataḥsiddhaṃ śuddhaṃ buddhamanīdṛśam |  
ekamevādvayaṃ brahma neha nānāsti kiṃcana || 470 ||*

Only Brahman exists, the One without a second: the Reality, radiant, self-existent, pure, intelligent and unlike anything finite. In It there is no duality of any kind.

### 471

निरस्तरागा विनिरस्तभोगाः शान्ताः सुदान्ता यतयो महान्तः ।  
विज्ञाय तत्त्वं परमेतदन्ते प्राप्ताः परां निर्वृतिमात्मयोगात् ॥ ४७१ ॥

*nirastarāgā vinirastabhogāḥ śāntāḥ sudāntā yatayo mahāntaḥ |  
vijñāya tattvaṃ parametadante prāptāḥ parāṃ nirvṛtimātmayogāt || 471 ||*

Great-souled Sannyasins who have shed all attachment and cast aside all sense-enjoyments, and who are serene and perfectly restrained, realize this Supreme Truth, and in the end reach the Supreme Bliss through their Self-realization.

### 472

<vt>Experience of Selfhood</vt>  
भवानपीदं परतत्त्वमात्मनः स्वरूपमानन्दघनं विचार्य ।  
विधूय मोहं स्वमनःप्रकल्पितं मुक्तः कृतार्थो भवतु प्रबुद्धः ॥ ४७२ ॥

*<vt>Experience of Selfhood</vt>  
bhavānapīdaṃ paratattvamātmanaḥ svarūpamānandaghanaṃ vicārya |  
vidhūya mohaṃ svamanaḥprakalpitaṃ muktaḥ kṛtārtho bhavatu prabuddhaḥ || 472 ||*

You too must discern this Supreme Truth, the real nature of the Self, which is undiluted Bliss. Shake off the delusion your own mind has created, be free and illumined, and reach the fulfillment of your life.

### 473

समाधिना साधुविनिश्चलात्मना पश्यात्मतत्त्वं स्फुटबोधचक्षुषा ।  
निःसंशयं सम्यगवेक्षितश्चेच् छ्रुतः पदार्थो न पुनर्विकल्प्यते ॥ ४७३ ॥

*samādhinā sādhuviniścalātmanā paśyātmatattvaṃ sphuṭabodhacakṣuṣā |  
niḥsaṃśayaṃ samyagavekṣitaścec chrutaḥ padārtho na punarvikalpyate || 473 ||*

Through the Samadhi in which the mind has been perfectly stilled, behold the Truth of the Self with the eye of clear realization. If the meaning of the Scriptural words heard from the Guru is discerned perfectly and without question, no further doubt can arise from it.

### 474

स्वस्याविद्याबन्धसंबन्धमोक्षात् सत्यज्ञानानन्दरूपात्मलब्धौ ।  
शास्त्रं युक्तिर्देशिकोक्तिः प्रमाणं चान्तःसिद्धा स्वानुभूतिः प्रमाणम् ॥ ४७४ ॥

*svasyāvidyābandhasaṃbandhamokṣāt satyajñānānandarūpātmalabdhau |  
śāstraṃ yuktirdeśikoktiḥ pramāṇaṃ cāntaḥsiddhā svānubhūtiḥ pramāṇam || 474 ||*

In realizing the Ātman, the Existence-Knowledge-Bliss Absolute, by breaking one's connection with the bondage of Avidya (ignorance), the Scriptures, reasoning and the Guru's words serve as proofs, and one's own experience, gained by concentrating the mind, is a further proof.

### 475

बन्धो मोक्षश्च तृप्तिश्च चिन्तारोग्यक्षुदादयः ।  
स्वेनैव वेद्या यज्ज्ञानं परेषामानुमानिकम् ॥ ४७५ ॥

*bandho mokṣaśca tṛptiśca cintārogyakṣudādayaḥ |  
svenaiva vedyā yajjñānaṃ pareṣāmānumānikam || 475 ||*

Bondage, liberation, contentment, anxiety, recovery from illness, hunger and similar things are known only to the person concerned. What others know of them is mere inference.

### 476

तटस्थिता बोधयन्ति गुरवः श्रुतयो यथा ।  
प्रज्ञयैव तरेद्विद्वानीश्वरानुगृहीतया ॥ ४७६ ॥

*taṭasthitā bodhayanti guravaḥ śrutayo yathā |  
prajñayaiva taredvidvānīśvarānugṛhītayā || 476 ||*

Gurus and the Śrutis alike instruct the disciple while standing apart. The person of realization crosses over Avidya through Illumination alone, supported by the grace of God.

### 477

स्वानुभूत्या स्वयं ज्ञात्वा स्वमात्मानमखण्डितम् ।  
संसिद्धः संमुखं तिष्ठेन्निर्विकल्पात्मनात्मनि ॥ ४७७ ॥

*svānubhūtyā svayaṃ jñātvā svamātmānamakhaṇḍitam |  
saṃsiddhaḥ saṃmukhaṃ tiṣṭhennirvikalpātmanātmani || 477 ||*

Knowing his indivisible Self through his own realization and so becoming perfect, a person should stand face to face with the Ātman, his mind free from dualistic ideas.

### 478

वेदान्तसिद्धान्तनिरुक्तिरेषा ब्रह्मैव जीवः सकलं जगच्च ।  
अखण्डरूपस्थितिरेव मोक्षो ब्रह्माद्वितीये श्रुतयः प्रमाणम् ॥ ४७८ ॥

*vedāntasiddhāntaniruktireṣā brahmaiva jīvaḥ sakalaṃ jagacca |  
akhaṇḍarūpasthitireva mokṣo brahmādvitīye śrutayaḥ pramāṇam || 478 ||*

The conclusion of all Vedanta discussions is that the Jīva and the entire universe are nothing but Brahman, and that liberation means abiding in Brahman, the indivisible Entity. The Śrutis themselves are the authority for the statement that Brahman is One without a second.

### 479

इति गुरुवचनाच्छ्रुतिप्रमाणात् परमवगम्य सतत्त्वमात्मयुक्त्या ।  
प्रशमितकरणः समाहितात्मा क्वचिदचलाकृतिरात्मनिष्ठतोऽभूत् ॥ ४७९ ॥

*iti guruvacanācchrutipramāṇāt paramavagamya satattvamātmayuktyā |  
praśamitakaraṇaḥ samāhitātmā kvacidacalākṛtirātmaniṣṭhato'bhūt || 479 ||*

At a blessed moment, through the Guru's teachings above, the authority of the Scriptures and his own reasoning, the disciple realized the Supreme Truth. With his senses quieted and his mind concentrated, he became motionless in form and perfectly established in the Ātman.

### 480

<vt>Practice of Knowledge - Disciple</vt>  
किंचित्कालं समाधाय परे ब्रह्मणि मानसम् ।  
उत्थाय परमानन्दादिदं वचनमब्रवीत् ॥ ४८0 ॥

*<vt>Practice of Knowledge - Disciple</vt>  
kiṃcitkālaṃ samādhāya pare brahmaṇi mānasam |  
utthāya paramānandādidaṃ vacanamabravīt || 480 ||*

After concentrating his mind for a while on the Supreme Brahman, he rose and, out of supreme bliss, spoke these words.

### 481

बुद्धिर्विनष्टा गलिता प्रवृत्तिः ब्रह्मात्मनोरेकतयाधिगत्या ।  
इदं न जानेऽप्यनिदं न जाने किं वा कियद्वा सुखमस्त्यपारम् ॥ ४८१ ॥

*buddhirvinaṣṭā galitā pravṛttiḥ brahmātmanorekatayādhigatyā |  
idaṃ na jāne'pyanidaṃ na jāne kiṃ vā kiyadvā sukhamastyapāram || 481 ||*

Through realising the identity of the Self and Brahman, my mind has vanished and all its activities have melted away. I do not know this or not-this, nor what the boundless Bliss (of Samadhi) is or how great it is!

### 482

वाचा वक्तुमशक्यमेव मनसा मन्तुं न वा शक्यते स्वानन्दामृतपूरपूरितपरब्रह्माम्बुधेर्वैभवम् ।  
अम्भोराशिविशीर्णवार्षिकशिलाभावं भजन्मे मनो यस्यांशांशलवे विलीनमधुनानन्दात्मना निर्वृतम् ॥ ४८२ ॥

*vācā vaktumaśakyameva manasā mantuṃ na vā śakyate svānandāmṛtapūrapūritaparabrahmāmbudhervaibhavam |  
ambhorāśiviśīrṇavārṣikaśilābhāvaṃ bhajanme mano yasyāṃśāṃśalave vilīnamadhunānandātmanā nirvṛtam || 482 ||*

The grandeur of the ocean of Supreme Brahman, brimming with the swell of the Self's nectar-like Bliss, truly cannot be put into words or grasped by the mind. In the tiniest fraction of it my mind melted, like a hailstone dissolving in the sea, and it now rests content in that Essence of Bliss.

### 483

क्व गतं केन वा नीतं कुत्र लीनमिदं जगत् ।  
अधुनैव मया दृष्टं नास्ति किं महदद्भुतम् ॥ ४८३ ॥

*kva gataṃ kena vā nītaṃ kutra līnamidaṃ jagat |  
adhunaiva mayā dṛṣṭaṃ nāsti kiṃ mahadadbhutam || 483 ||*

Where has the universe gone? Who took it away? Into what has it merged? I saw it only a moment ago, and has it stopped existing? This is very strange!

### 484

किं हेयं किमुपादेयं किमन्यत्किं विलक्षणम् ।  
अखण्डानन्दपीयूषपूर्णे ब्रह्ममहार्णवे ॥ ४८४ ॥

*kiṃ heyaṃ kimupādeyaṃ kimanyatkiṃ vilakṣaṇam |  
akhaṇḍānandapīyūṣapūrṇe brahmamahārṇave || 484 ||*

In the ocean of Brahman, full of the nectar of Absolute Bliss, what is there to reject or to accept? What is other (than oneself), and what is different?

### 485

न किंचिदत्र पश्यामि न शृणोमि न वेद्म्यहम् ।  
स्वात्मनैव सदानन्दरूपेणास्मि विलक्षणः ॥ ४८५ ॥

*na kiṃcidatra paśyāmi na śṛṇomi na vedmyaham |  
svātmanaiva sadānandarūpeṇāsmi vilakṣaṇaḥ || 485 ||*

In this state I see nothing, hear nothing, and know nothing. I simply exist as the Self, eternal Bliss, distinct from everything else.

### 486

नमो नमस्ते गुरवे महात्मने विमुक्तसङ्गाय सदुत्तमाय ।  
नित्याद्वयानन्दरसस्वरूपिणे भूम्ने सदापारदयाम्बुधाम्ने ॥ ४८६ ॥

*namo namaste gurave mahātmane vimuktasaṅgāya saduttamāya |  
nityādvayānandarasasvarūpiṇe bhūmne sadāpāradayāmbudhāmne || 486 ||*

Salutations again and again to you, O noble Teacher, free from attachment, best of good souls, embodiment of the essence of Eternal Bliss, the One without a second, infinite and forever a boundless ocean of mercy:

### 487

यत्कटाक्षशशिसान्द्रचन्द्रिका पातधूतभवतापजश्रमः ।  
प्राप्तवानहमखण्डवैभवा नन्दमात्मपदमक्षयं क्षणात् ॥ ४८७ ॥

*yatkaṭākṣaśaśisāndracandrikā pātadhūtabhavatāpajaśramaḥ |  
prāptavānahamakhaṇḍavaibhavā nandamātmapadamakṣayaṃ kṣaṇāt || 487 ||*

Your glance, like a shower of concentrated moonbeams, has taken away the exhaustion brought on by the world's afflictions, and in a moment has admitted me to the undecaying state of the Ātman, the Bliss of infinite majesty!

### 488

धन्योऽहं कृतकृत्योऽहं विमुक्तोऽहं भवग्रहात् ।
