नित्यानन्दस्वरूपोऽहं पूर्णोऽहं त्वदनुग्रहात् ॥ ४८८ ॥

*dhanyo'haṃ kṛtakṛtyo'haṃ vimukto'haṃ bhavagrahāt |  
nityānandasvarūpo'haṃ pūrṇo'haṃ tvadanugrahāt || 488 ||*

I am blessed. I have reached the fulfilment of my life and am free from the grip of transmigration. I am the Essence of Eternal Bliss, I am infinite, and all of it through your mercy!

### 489

असङ्गोऽहमनङ्गोऽहमलिङ्गोऽहमभङ्गुरः ।  
प्रशान्तोऽहमनन्तोऽहममलोऽहं चिरन्तनः ॥ ४८९ ॥

*asaṅgo'hamanaṅgo'hamaliṅgo'hamabhaṅguraḥ |  
praśānto'hamananto'hamamalo'haṃ cirantanaḥ || 489 ||*

I am unattached. I have no body. I am free from the subtle body and undecaying. I am calm, I am infinite, I am stainless and eternal.

### 490

अकर्ताहमभोक्ताहमविकारोऽहमक्रियः ।  
शुद्धबोधस्वरूपोऽहं केवलोऽहं सदाशिवः ॥ ४९0 ॥

*akartāhamabhoktāhamavikāro'hamakriyaḥ |  
śuddhabodhasvarūpo'haṃ kevalo'haṃ sadāśivaḥ || 490 ||*

I am not the doer and not the experiencer. I am changeless and beyond activity. I am the essence of Pure Knowledge, Absolute, and one with Eternal Good.

### 491

द्रष्टुः श्रोतुर्वक्तुः कर्तुर्भोक्तुर्विभिन्न एवाहम् ।  
नित्यनिरन्तरनिष्क्रियनिःसीमासङ्गपूर्णबोधात्मा ॥ ४९१ ॥

*draṣṭuḥ śroturvaktuḥ karturbhokturvibhinna evāham |  
nityanirantaraniṣkriyaniḥsīmāsaṅgapūrṇabodhātmā || 491 ||*

I am truly other than the one who sees, hears, speaks, does, and experiences. I am the essence of Knowledge: eternal, unbroken, beyond activity, limitless, unattached, and infinite.

### 492

नाहमिदं नाहमदोऽप्युभयोरवभासकं परं शुद्धम् ।  
बाह्याभ्यन्तरशून्यं पूर्णं ब्रह्माद्वितीयमेवाहम् ॥ ४९२ ॥

*nāhamidaṃ nāhamado'pyubhayoravabhāsakaṃ paraṃ śuddham |  
bāhyābhyantaraśūnyaṃ pūrṇaṃ brahmādvitīyamevāham || 492 ||*

I am neither this nor that, but the Supreme that lights up both. I am truly Brahman, the One without a second, pure, with no inside or outside, and infinite.

### 493

निरुपममनादितत्त्वं त्वमहमिदमद इति कल्पनादूरम् ।  
नित्यानन्दैकरसं सत्यं ब्रह्माद्वितीयमेवाहम् ॥ ४९३ ॥

*nirupamamanāditattvaṃ tvamahamidamada iti kalpanādūram |  
nityānandaikarasaṃ satyaṃ brahmādvitīyamevāham || 493 ||*

I am truly Brahman, the One without a second, without equal, the beginningless Reality, beyond such imaginings as you and I, or this and that, the Essence of Eternal Bliss, the Truth.

### 494

नारायणोऽहं नरकान्तकोऽहं पुरान्तकोऽहं पुरुषोऽहमीशः ।  
अखण्डबोधोऽहमशेषसाक्षी निरीश्वरोऽहं निरहं च निर्ममः ॥ ४९४ ॥

*nārāyaṇo'haṃ narakāntako'haṃ purāntako'haṃ puruṣo'hamīśaḥ |  
akhaṇḍabodho'hamaśeṣasākṣī nirīśvaro'haṃ nirahaṃ ca nirmamaḥ || 494 ||*

I am Nārāyaṇa, the slayer of Nāraka. I am the destroyer of Tripura, the Supreme Being, the Ruler. I am Knowledge Absolute, the Witness of everything. I have no Ruler other than myself, and I am without the ideas of "I" and "mine."

### 495

सर्वेषु भूतेष्वहमेव संस्थितो ज्ञानात्मनान्तर्बहिराश्रयः सन् ।  
भोक्ता च भोग्यं स्वयमेव सर्वं यद्यत्पृथग्दृष्टमिदन्तया पुरा ॥ ४९५ ॥

*sarveṣu bhūteṣvahameva saṃsthito jñānātmanāntarbahirāśrayaḥ san |  
bhoktā ca bhogyaṃ svayameva sarvaṃ yadyatpṛthagdṛṣṭamidantayā purā || 495 ||*

I alone dwell as knowledge in all beings, their inner and outer support. I myself am the experiencer and everything experienced, all that I earlier regarded as "this," the not-Self.

### 496

मय्यखण्डसुखाम्भोधौ बहुधा विश्ववीचयः ।  
उत्पद्यन्ते विलीयन्ते मायामारुतविभ्रमात् ॥ ४९६ ॥

*mayyakhaṇḍasukhāmbhodhau bahudhā viśvavīcayaḥ |  
utpadyante vilīyante māyāmārutavibhramāt || 496 ||*

In me, the ocean of Infinite Bliss, the waves of the universe rise and fall through the play of the wind of Māyā.

### 497

स्थुलादिभावा मयि कल्पिता भ्रमाद् आरोपितानुस्फुरणेन लोकैः ।  
काले यथा कल्पकवत्सराय णर्त्वादयो निष्कलनिर्विकल्पे ॥ ४९७ ॥

*sthulādibhāvā mayi kalpitā bhramād āropitānusphuraṇena lokaiḥ |  
kāle yathā kalpakavatsarāya ṇartvādayo niṣkalanirvikalpe || 497 ||*

People mistakenly imagine in me such ideas as gross (or subtle) through the appearance of superimposed things, just as cycles, years, half-years, seasons, and so on are imagined in indivisible, absolute time.

### 498

आरोपितं नाश्रयदूषकं भवेत् कदापि मूढैरतिदोषदूषितैः ।  
नार्द्रिकरोत्यूषरभूमिभागं मरीचिकावारि महाप्रवाहः ॥ ४९८ ॥

*āropitaṃ nāśrayadūṣakaṃ bhavet kadāpi mūḍhairatidoṣadūṣitaiḥ |  
nārdrikarotyūṣarabhūmibhāgaṃ marīcikāvāri mahāpravāhaḥ || 498 ||*

What grossly ignorant fools superimpose can never stain the substratum. The great flood of water seen in a mirage never dampens the desert.

### 499

आकाशवल्लेपविदूरगोऽहं आदित्यवद्भास्यविलक्षणोऽहम् ।  
अहार्यवन्नित्यविनिश्चलोऽहं अम्भोधिवत्पारविवर्जितोऽहम् ॥ ४९९ ॥

*ākāśavallepavidūrago'haṃ ādityavadbhāsyavilakṣaṇo'ham |  
ahāryavannityaviniścalo'haṃ ambhodhivatpāravivarjito'ham || 499 ||*

Like the sky, I am beyond contamination. Like the sun, I am separate from the things lit up. Like the mountain, I am forever motionless. Like the ocean, I am without limit.

### 500

न मे देहेन संबन्धो मेघेनेव विहायसः ।  
अतः कुतो मे तद्धर्मा जाक्रत्स्वप्नसुषुप्तयः ॥ ५00 ॥

*na me dehena saṃbandho megheneva vihāyasaḥ |  
ataḥ kuto me taddharmā jākratsvapnasuṣuptayaḥ || 500 ||*

I have no connection with the body, just as the sky has none with the clouds. So how can waking, dream, and deep sleep, which are attributes of the body, affect me?

### 501

उपाधिरायाति स एव गच्छति स एव कर्माणि करोति भुङ्क्ते ।  
स एव जीर्यन्म्रियते सदाहं कुलाद्रिवन्निश्चल एव संस्थितः ॥ ५0१ ॥

*upādhirāyāti sa eva gacchati sa eva karmāṇi karoti bhuṅkte |  
sa eva jīryanmriyate sadāhaṃ kulādrivanniścala eva saṃsthitaḥ || 501 ||*

It is the Upādhi (superimposed attribute) that comes, and that alone goes. It is that which acts and experiences (the results), and that alone which decays and dies. I remain forever firm, like the Kula mountain.

### 502

न मे प्रवृत्तिर्न च मे निवृत्तिः सदैकरूपस्य निरंशकस्य ।  
एकात्मको यो निविडो निरन्तरो व्योमेव पूर्णः स कथं नु चेष्टते ॥ ५0२ ॥

*na me pravṛttirna ca me nivṛttiḥ sadaikarūpasya niraṃśakasya |  
ekātmako yo niviḍo nirantaro vyomeva pūrṇaḥ sa kathaṃ nu ceṣṭate || 502 ||*

For me, who am always the same and have no parts, there is neither taking up work nor ceasing from it. How could what is One, concentrated, unbroken, and infinite like the sky ever exert itself?

### 503

पुण्यानि पापानि निरिन्द्रियस्य निश्चेतसो निर्विकृतेर्निराकृतेः ।  
कुतो ममाखण्डसुखानुभूतेः ब्रूते ह्यनन्वागतमित्यपि श्रुतिः ॥ ५0३ ॥

*puṇyāni pāpāni nirindriyasya niścetaso nirvikṛternirākṛteḥ |  
kuto mamākhaṇḍasukhānubhūteḥ brūte hyananvāgatamityapi śrutiḥ || 503 ||*

How can there be merit or demerit for me, who have no organs, no mind, no change, and no form, and who am the realisation of Bliss Absolute? The Shruti says this too, in the passage "Not touched," and so on.

### 504

छायया स्पृष्टमुष्णं वा शीतं वा सुष्ठु दुःष्ठु वा ।  
न स्पृशत्येव यत्किंचित्पुरुषं तद्विलक्षणम् ॥ ५0४ ॥

*chāyayā spṛṣṭamuṣṇaṃ vā śītaṃ vā suṣṭhu duḥṣṭhu vā |  
na spṛśatyeva yatkiṃcitpuruṣaṃ tadvilakṣaṇam || 504 ||*

If heat or cold, good or evil, happens to touch the shadow of a person's body, it does not affect in the least the person himself, who is separate from the shadow.

### 505

न साक्षिणं साक्ष्यधर्माः संस्पृशन्ति विलक्षणम् ।  
अविकारमुदासीनं गृहधर्माः प्रदीपवत् ॥ ५0५ ॥

*na sākṣiṇaṃ sākṣyadharmāḥ saṃspṛśanti vilakṣaṇam |  
avikāramudāsīnaṃ gṛhadharmāḥ pradīpavat || 505 ||*

The properties of things observed do not affect the Witness, which is separate from them, changeless, and indifferent, just as the properties of a room (do not affect) the lamp (that lights it).

### 506

रवेर्यथा कर्मणि साक्षिभावो वन्हेर्यथा दाहनियामकत्वम् ।  
रज्जोर्यथारोपितवस्तुसङ्गः तथैव कूटस्थचिदात्मनो मे ॥ ५0६ ॥

*raveryathā karmaṇi sākṣibhāvo vanheryathā dāhaniyāmakatvam |  
rajjoryathāropitavastusaṅgaḥ tathaiva kūṭasthacidātmano me || 506 ||*

Just as the sun is only a witness of people's actions, as fire burns everything without distinction, and as the rope is related to what is superimposed on it, so am I, the unchanging Self, Intelligence Absolute.

### 507

कर्तापि वा कारयितापि नाहं भोक्तापि वा भोजयितापि नाहम् ।  
द्रष्टापि वा दर्शयितापि नाहं सोऽहं स्वयंज्योतिरनीदृगात्मा ॥ ५0७ ॥

*kartāpi vā kārayitāpi nāhaṃ bhoktāpi vā bhojayitāpi nāham |  
draṣṭāpi vā darśayitāpi nāhaṃ so'haṃ svayaṃjyotiranīdṛgātmā || 507 ||*

I do not act or cause others to act. I do not enjoy or cause others to enjoy. I do not see or cause others to see. I am that Self-effulgent, Transcendent Ātman.

### 508

चलत्युपाधौ प्रतिबिम्बलौल्यम् अउपाधिकं मूढधियो नयन्ति ।  
स्वबिम्बभूतं रविवद्विनिष्क्रियं कर्तास्मि भोक्तास्मि हतोऽस्मि हेति ॥ ५0८ ॥

*calatyupādhau pratibimbalaulyam aupādhikaṃ mūḍhadhiyo nayanti |  
svabimbabhūtaṃ ravivadviniṣkriyaṃ kartāsmi bhoktāsmi hato'smi heti || 508 ||*

When the added adjunct (Upādhi) moves, fools attribute the resulting movement of the reflection to the thing reflected, such as the sun, which has no activity. (So they think) "I am the doer," "I am the experiencer," "I am killed, oh, alas!"

### 509

जले वापि स्थले वापि लुठत्वेष जडात्मकः ।  
नाहं विलिप्ये तद्धर्मैर्घटधर्मैर्नभो यथा ॥ ५0९ ॥

*jale vāpi sthale vāpi luṭhatveṣa jaḍātmakaḥ |  
nāhaṃ vilipye taddharmairghaṭadharmairnabho yathā || 509 ||*

Let this inert body fall in water or on land. Its properties do not touch me, just as the properties of a jar do not touch the sky.

### 510

कर्तृत्वभोक्तृत्वखलत्वमत्तता जडत्वबद्धत्वविमुक्ततादयः ।  
बुद्धेर्विकल्पा न तु सन्ति वस्तुतः स्वस्मिन् परे ब्रह्मणि केवलेऽद्वये ॥ ५१0 ॥

*kartṛtvabhoktṛtvakhalatvamattatā jaḍatvabaddhatvavimuktatādayaḥ |  
buddhervikalpā na tu santi vastutaḥ svasmin pare brahmaṇi kevale'dvaye || 510 ||*

Agency, experience, cunning, drunkenness, dullness, bondage, freedom: these are passing states of the Buddhi. They never truly belong to the Self, the Supreme Brahman, the Absolute, the One without a second.

### 511

सन्तु विकाराः प्रकृतेर्दशधा शतधा सहस्रधा वापि ।  
किं मेऽसङ्गचितस्तैर्न घनः क्वचिदम्बरं स्पृशति ॥ ५११ ॥

*santu vikārāḥ prakṛterdaśadhā śatadhā sahasradhā vāpi |  
kiṃ me'saṅgacitastairna ghanaḥ kvacidambaraṃ spṛśati || 511 ||*

Let the Prakriti change in ten, a hundred, or a thousand ways. What have those changes to do with me, the unattached Knowledge Absolute? The clouds never touch the sky!

### 512

अव्यक्तादिस्थूलपर्यन्तमेतत् विश्व यत्राभासमात्रं प्रतीतम् ।  
व्योमप्रख्यं सूक्ष्ममाद्यन्तहीनं ब्रह्माद्वैतं यत्तदेवाहमस्मि ॥ ५१२ ॥

*avyaktādisthūlaparyantametat viśva yatrābhāsamātraṃ pratītam |  
vyomaprakhyaṃ sūkṣmamādyantahīnaṃ brahmādvaitaṃ yattadevāhamasmi || 512 ||*

I am truly that Brahman, the One without a second, subtle like the sky, without beginning or end, in which the whole universe, from the Undifferentiated down to the gross body, appears as nothing more than a shadow.

### 513

सर्वाधारं सर्ववस्तुप्रकाशं सर्वाकारं सर्वगं सर्वशून्यम् ।  
नित्यं शुद्धं निश्चलं निर्विकल्पं ब्रह्माद्वैतं यत्तदेवाहमस्मि ॥ ५१३ ॥

*sarvādhāraṃ sarvavastuprakāśaṃ sarvākāraṃ sarvagaṃ sarvaśūnyam |  
nityaṃ śuddhaṃ niścalaṃ nirvikalpaṃ brahmādvaitaṃ yattadevāhamasmi || 513 ||*

I am truly that Brahman, the One without a second, the support of all, which lights up all things, has infinite forms, is present everywhere, is free of multiplicity, and is eternal, pure, unmoved, and absolute.

### 514

यत्प्रत्यस्ताशेषमायाविशेषं प्रत्यग्रूपं प्रत्ययागम्यमानम् ।  
सत्यज्ञानानन्तमानन्दरूपं ब्रह्माद्वैतं यत्तदेवाहमस्मि ॥ ५१४ ॥

*yatpratyastāśeṣamāyāviśeṣaṃ pratyagrūpaṃ pratyayāgamyamānam |  
satyajñānānantamānandarūpaṃ brahmādvaitaṃ yattadevāhamasmi || 514 ||*

I am truly that Brahman, the One without a second, which lies beyond the endless differentiations of Māyā, is the innermost essence of all, is out of the reach of consciousness, and is Truth, Knowledge, Infinity, and Bliss Absolute.

### 515

निष्क्रियोऽस्म्यविकारोऽस्मि निष्कलोऽस्मि निराकृतिः ।  
निर्विकल्पोऽस्मि नित्योऽस्मि निरालम्बोऽस्मि निर्द्वयः ॥ ५१५ ॥

*niṣkriyo'smyavikāro'smi niṣkalo'smi nirākṛtiḥ |  
nirvikalpo'smi nityo'smi nirālambo'smi nirdvayaḥ || 515 ||*

I am without activity, without change, without parts, without form. I am absolute, eternal, in need of no other support, the One without a second.

### 516

सर्वात्मकोऽहं सर्वोऽहं सर्वातीतोऽहमद्वयः ।  
केवलाखण्डबोधोऽहमानन्दोऽहं निरन्तरः ॥ ५१६ ॥

*sarvātmako'haṃ sarvo'haṃ sarvātīto'hamadvayaḥ |  
kevalākhaṇḍabodho'hamānando'haṃ nirantaraḥ || 516 ||*

The Universal and the All am I, transcendent and One without a second. Absolute and Infinite Knowledge is what I am, and indivisible Bliss.

### 517

स्वाराज्यसाम्राज्यविभूतिरेषा भवत्कृपाश्रीमहिमप्रसादात् ।  
प्राप्ता मया श्रीगुरवे महात्मने नमो नमस्तेऽस्तु पुनर्नमोऽस्तु ॥ ५१७ ॥

*svārājyasāmrājyavibhūtireṣā bhavatkṛpāśrīmahimaprasādāt |  
prāptā mayā śrīgurave mahātmane namo namaste'stu punarnamo'stu || 517 ||*

Through the supreme majesty of your grace I have received this splendour, the sovereignty of Self-effulgence. Salutations to you, O glorious, noble-minded Teacher, salutations again and again!

### 518

महास्वप्ने मायाकृतजनिजरामृत्युगहने भ्रमन्तं क्लिश्यन्तं बहुलतरतापैरनुदिनम् ।  
अहंकारव्याघ्रव्यथितमिममत्यन्तकृपया प्रबोध्य प्रस्वापात्परमवितवान्मामसि गुरो ॥ ५१८ ॥

*mahāsvapne māyākṛtajanijarāmṛtyugahane bhramantaṃ kliśyantaṃ bahulataratāpairanudinam |  
ahaṃkāravyāghravyathitamimamatyantakṛpayā prabodhya prasvāpātparamavitavānmāmasi guro || 518 ||*

O Master, out of pure grace you have woken me from sleep and rescued me completely. I was wandering in an endless dream, in a forest of birth, decay, and death created by illusion, tormented day after day by countless afflictions and badly harried by the tiger of egoism.

### 519

नमस्तस्मै सदैकस्मै कस्मैचिन्महसे नमः ।  
यदेतद्विश्वरूपेण राजते गुरुराज ते ॥ ५१९ ॥

*namastasmai sadaikasmai kasmaicinmahase namaḥ |  
yadetadviśvarūpeṇa rājate gururāja te || 519 ||*

Salutations to you, O Prince of Teachers, Greatness beyond naming, who are always the same and who show yourself as this universe. To you I bow.

### 520

इति नतमवलोक्य शिष्यवर्यं समधिगतात्मसुखं प्रबुद्धतत्त्वम् ।  
प्रमुदितहृदयं स देशिकेन्द्रः पुनरिदमाह वचः परं महात्मा ॥ ५२0 ॥

*iti natamavalokya śiṣyavaryaṃ samadhigatātmasukhaṃ prabuddhatattvam |  
pramuditahṛdayaṃ sa deśikendraḥ punaridamāha vacaḥ paraṃ mahātmā || 520 ||*

Seeing the worthy disciple prostrating himself in this way, having reached the Bliss of the Self, realised the Truth, and grown glad at heart, that noble, ideal Teacher spoke again in these excellent words:

### 521

<vt>Final Words of Advice</vt>  
ब्रह्मप्रत्ययसन्ततिर्जगदतो ब्रह्मैव तत्सर्वतः पश्याध्यात्मदृशा प्रशान्तमनसा सर्वास्ववस्थास्वपि ।  
रूपादन्यदवेक्षितं किमभितश्चक्षुष्मतां दृश्यते तद्वद्ब्रह्मविदः सतः किमपरं बुद्धेर्विहारास्पदम् ॥ ५२१ ॥

*<vt>Final Words of Advice</vt>  
brahmapratyayasantatirjagadato brahmaiva tatsarvataḥ paśyādhyātmadṛśā praśāntamanasā sarvāsvavasthāsvapi |  
rūpādanyadavekṣitaṃ kimabhitaścakṣuṣmatāṃ dṛśyate tadvadbrahmavidaḥ sataḥ kimaparaṃ buddhervihārāspadam || 521 ||*

The universe is an unbroken series of perceptions of Brahman, and so it is in every respect nothing but Brahman. See this with the eye of illumination and a calm mind, in all circumstances. Does a person with eyes ever see anything around him except forms? In the same way, what is there apart from Brahman to occupy the intellect of a realised person?

### 522

कस्तां परानन्दरसानुभूति मृत्सृज्य शून्येषु रमेत विद्वान् ।  
चन्द्रे महाल्हादिनि दीप्यमाने चित्रेन्दुमालोकयितुं क इच्छेत् ॥ ५२२ ॥

*kastāṃ parānandarasānubhūti mṛtsṛjya śūnyeṣu rameta vidvān |  
candre mahālhādini dīpyamāne citrendumālokayituṃ ka icchet || 522 ||*

What wise person would throw away that enjoyment of Supreme Bliss and take delight in insubstantial things? When the utterly lovely moon is shining, who would want to look at a painted moon?

### 523

असत्पदार्थानुभवेन किंचिन् न ह्यस्ति तृप्तिर्न च दुःखहानिः ।  
तदद्वयानन्दरसानुभूत्या तृप्तः सुखं तिष्ठ सदात्मनिष्ठया ॥ ५२३ ॥

*asatpadārthānubhavena kiṃcin na hyasti tṛptirna ca duḥkhahāniḥ |  
tadadvayānandarasānubhūtyā tṛptaḥ sukhaṃ tiṣṭha sadātmaniṣṭhayā || 523 ||*

Perceiving unreal things brings neither satisfaction nor an end to misery. So, content with realising the Bliss Absolute, the One without a second, live happily in identity with that Reality.

### 524

स्वमेव सर्वथा पश्यन्मन्यमानः स्वमद्वयम् ।  
स्वानन्दमनुभुञ्जानः कालं नय महामते ॥ ५२४ ॥

*svameva sarvathā paśyanmanyamānaḥ svamadvayam |  
svānandamanubhuñjānaḥ kālaṃ naya mahāmate || 524 ||*

O noble soul, spend your time seeing only the Self in every circumstance, thinking of the Self, the One without a second, and enjoying the Bliss of the Self!

### 525

अखण्डबोधात्मनि निर्विकल्पे विकल्पनं व्योम्नि पुरप्रकल्पनम् ।  
तदद्वयानन्दमयात्मना सदा शान्तिं परामेत्य भजस्व मौनम् ॥ ५२५ ॥

*akhaṇḍabodhātmani nirvikalpe vikalpanaṃ vyomni puraprakalpanam |  
tadadvayānandamayātmanā sadā śāntiṃ parāmetya bhajasva maunam || 525 ||*

Ideas of duality in the Ātman, the Infinite Knowledge, the Absolute, are like castles imagined in the air. So, always identifying yourself with the Bliss Absolute, the One without a second, and reaching Supreme Peace in that way, remain quiet.

### 526

तूष्णीमवस्था परमोपशान्तिः बुद्धेरसत्कल्पविकल्पहेतोः ।  
ब्रह्मात्मन ब्रह्मविदो महात्मनो यत्राद्वयानन्दसुखं निरन्तरम् ॥ ५२६ ॥

*tūṣṇīmavasthā paramopaśāntiḥ buddherasatkalpavikalpahetoḥ |  
brahmātmana brahmavido mahātmano yatrādvayānandasukhaṃ nirantaram || 526 ||*

For the sage who has realised Brahman, the mind, the source of unreal fancies, becomes fully tranquil. This truly is his state of quietude, in which, one with Brahman, he constantly enjoys the Bliss Absolute, the One without a second.

### 527

नास्ति निर्वासनान्मौनात्परं सुखकृदुत्तमम् ।  
विज्ञातात्मस्वरूपस्य स्वानन्दरसपायिनः ॥ ५२७ ॥

*nāsti nirvāsanānmaunātparaṃ sukhakṛduttamam |  
vijñātātmasvarūpasya svānandarasapāyinaḥ || 527 ||*

For the person who has realised his own nature and drinks the undiluted Bliss of the Self, nothing is more exhilarating than the quietude that comes from being free of desire.

### 528

गच्छंस्तिष्ठन्नुपविशञ्छयानो वान्यथापि वा ।  
यथेच्छया वेसेद्विद्वानात्नारामः सदा मुनिः ॥ ५२८ ॥

*gacchaṃstiṣṭhannupaviśañchayāno vānyathāpi vā |  
yathecchayā vesedvidvānātnārāmaḥ sadā muniḥ || 528 ||*

The illumined sage, whose only pleasure is in the Self, always lives at ease, whether walking or standing still, sitting or lying down, or in any other condition.

### 529

न देशकालासनदिग्यमादि लक्ष्याद्यपेक्षाप्रतिबद्धवृत्तेः ।  
संसिद्धतत्त्वस्य महात्मनोऽस्ति स्ववेदने का नियमाद्यवस्था ॥ ५२९ ॥

*na deśakālāsanadigyamādi lakṣyādyapekṣāpratibaddhavṛtteḥ |  
saṃsiddhatattvasya mahātmano'sti svavedane kā niyamādyavasthā || 529 ||*

The noble soul who has fully realised the Truth, and whose mind-functions meet no obstruction, no longer depends on conditions of place, time, posture, direction, moral disciplines, objects of meditation, and so on. What rules can apply to knowing one's own Self?

### 530

घटोऽयमिति विज्ञातुं नियमः कोऽन्ववेक्षते ।  
विना प्रमाणसुष्ठुत्वं यस्मिन् सति पदार्थधीः ॥ ५३0 ॥

*ghaṭo'yamiti vijñātuṃ niyamaḥ ko'nvavekṣate |  
vinā pramāṇasuṣṭhutvaṃ yasmin sati padārthadhīḥ || 530 ||*

To know that this is a jar, what condition is needed other than that the means of knowledge be free of defect, which alone ensures knowledge of the object?

### 531

अयमात्मा नित्यसिद्धः प्रमाणे सति भासते ।  
न देशं नापि कालं न शुद्धिं वाप्यपेक्षते ॥ ५३१ ॥

*ayamātmā nityasiddhaḥ pramāṇe sati bhāsate |  
na deśaṃ nāpi kālaṃ na śuddhiṃ vāpyapekṣate || 531 ||*

In the same way this Ātman, an eternal truth, reveals Itself as soon as the right means of knowledge is present, and does not depend on place, time, or (inner) purity.

### 532

देवदत्तोऽहमित्येतद्विज्ञानं निरपेक्षकम् ।  
तद्वद्ब्रह्मविदोऽप्यस्य ब्रह्माहमिति वेदनम् ॥ ५३२ ॥

*devadatto'hamityetadvijñānaṃ nirapekṣakam |  
tadvadbrahmavido'pyasya brahmāhamiti vedanam || 532 ||*

The awareness "I am Devadatta" does not depend on circumstances. The same holds for the knower of Brahman's realisation that he is Brahman.

### 533

भानुनेव जगत्सर्वं भासते यस्य तेजसा ।  
अनात्मकमसत्तुच्छं किं नु तस्यावभासकम् ॥ ५३३ ॥

*bhānuneva jagatsarvaṃ bhāsate yasya tejasā |  
anātmakamasattucchaṃ kiṃ nu tasyāvabhāsakam || 533 ||*

What could possibly reveal That whose radiance, like the sun's, makes the whole universe, insubstantial, unreal, and insignificant, appear at all?

### 534

वेदशास्त्रपुराणानि भूतानि सकलान्यपि ।  
येनार्थवन्ति तं किन्नु विज्ञातारं प्रकाशयेत् ॥ ५३४ ॥

*vedaśāstrapurāṇāni bhūtāni sakalānyapi |  
yenārthavanti taṃ kinnu vijñātāraṃ prakāśayet || 534 ||*

What could possibly light up that Eternal Subject through which the Vedas, the Puranas, the other Scriptures, and all beings are given meaning?

### 535

एष स्वयंज्योतिरनन्तशक्तिः आत्माप्रमेयः सकलानुभूतिः ।  
यमेव विज्ञाय विमुक्तबन्धो जयत्ययं ब्रह्मविदुत्तमोत्तमः ॥ ५३५ ॥

*eṣa svayaṃjyotiranantaśaktiḥ ātmāprameyaḥ sakalānubhūtiḥ |  
yameva vijñāya vimuktabandho jayatyayaṃ brahmaviduttamottamaḥ || 535 ||*

Here is the Self-effulgent Ātman, of infinite power, beyond the reach of conditioned knowledge and yet the common experience of all. By realising this alone, this incomparable knower of Brahman lives his glorious life, free from bondage.

### 536

न खिद्यते नो विषयैः प्रमोदते न सज्जते नापि विरज्यते च ।  
स्वस्मिन्सदा क्रीडति नन्दति स्वयं निरन्तरानन्दरसेन तृप्तः ॥ ५३६ ॥

*na khidyate no viṣayaiḥ pramodate na sajjate nāpi virajyate ca |  
svasminsadā krīḍati nandati svayaṃ nirantarānandarasena tṛptaḥ || 536 ||*

Content with undiluted, unending Bliss, he is neither saddened nor elated by sense-objects, neither attached to them nor averse to them, but always sports with the Self and takes pleasure in it.

### 537

क्षुधां देहव्यथां त्यक्त्वा बालः क्रीडति वस्तुनिः ।  
तथैव विद्वान् रमते निर्ममो निरहं सुखी ॥ ५३७ ॥

*kṣudhāṃ dehavyathāṃ tyaktvā bālaḥ krīḍati vastuniḥ |  
tathaiva vidvān ramate nirmamo nirahaṃ sukhī || 537 ||*

A child plays with its toys, forgetting hunger and bodily pain. In exactly that way, the realised person takes pleasure in the Reality, without ideas of "I" or "mine," and is happy.

### 538

चिन्ताशून्यमदैन्यभैक्षमशनं पानं सरिद्वारिषु स्वातन्त्र्येण निरङ्कुशा स्थितिरभीर्निद्रा श्मशाने वने ।  
वस्त्रं क्षालनशोषणादिरहितं दिग्वास्तु शय्या मही संचारो निगमान्तवीथिषु विदां क्रीडा परे ब्रह्मणि ॥ ५३८ ॥

*cintāśūnyamadainyabhaikṣamaśanaṃ pānaṃ saridvāriṣu svātantryeṇa niraṅkuśā sthitirabhīrnidrā śmaśāne vane |  
vastraṃ kṣālanaśoṣaṇādirahitaṃ digvāstu śayyā mahī saṃcāro nigamāntavīthiṣu vidāṃ krīḍā pare brahmaṇi || 538 ||*

Realised people get their food by begging, without anxiety or humiliation, and drink from rivers. They live freely and independently, and sleep without fear in cremation grounds or forests. Their clothing may be the directions themselves, which need no washing or drying, or some bark or the like. The earth is their bed. They roam along the avenue of Vedanta, and their pastime is in the Supreme Brahman.

### 539

विमानमालम्ब्य शरीरमेतद् भुनक्त्यशेषान्विषयानुपस्थितान् ।  
परेच्छया बालवदात्मवेत्ता योऽव्यक्तलिङ्गोऽननुषक्तबाह्यः ॥ ५३९ ॥

*vimānamālambya śarīrametad bhunaktyaśeṣānviṣayānupasthitān |  
parecchayā bālavadātmavettā yo'vyaktaliṅgo'nanuṣaktabāhyaḥ || 539 ||*

The knower of the Ātman, who wears no outward sign and is unattached to external things, rests on this body without identifying with it, and, like a child, experiences all kinds of sense-objects as they come through the wishes of others.

### 540

दिगम्बरो वापि च साम्बरो वा त्वगम्बरो वापि चिदम्बरस्थः ।  
उन्मत्तवद्वापि च बालवद्वा पिशाचवद्वापि चरत्यवन्याम् ॥ ५४0 ॥

*digambaro vāpi ca sāmbaro vā tvagambaro vāpi cidambarasthaḥ |  
unmattavadvāpi ca bālavadvā piśācavadvāpi caratyavanyām || 540 ||*

Established in the ethereal plane of Absolute Knowledge, he wanders through the world, sometimes like a madman, sometimes like a child, and at other times like a ghoul, wearing nothing but the directions, or sometimes clothes, or at other times perhaps skins.

### 541

कामान्निष्कामरूपी संश्चरत्येकचारो मुनिः ।  
स्वात्मनैव सदा तुष्टः स्वयं सर्वात्मना स्थितः ॥ ५४१ ॥

*kāmānniṣkāmarūpī saṃścaratyekacāro muniḥ |  
svātmanaiva sadā tuṣṭaḥ svayaṃ sarvātmanā sthitaḥ || 541 ||*

Living alone, the sage partakes of sense-objects as the very embodiment of desirelessness. He is always satisfied with his own Self, and he himself is present in the All.

### 542

क्वचिन्मूढो विद्वान् क्वचिदपि महाराजविभवः क्वचिद्भ्रान्तः सौम्यः क्वचिदजगराचारकलितः ।  
क्वचित्पात्रीभूतः क्वचिदवमतः क्वाप्यविदितः चरत्येवं प्राज्ञः सततपरमानन्दसुखितः ॥ ५४२ ॥

*kvacinmūḍho vidvān kvacidapi mahārājavibhavaḥ kvacidbhrāntaḥ saumyaḥ kvacidajagarācārakalitaḥ |  
kvacitpātrībhūtaḥ kvacidavamataḥ kvāpyaviditaḥ caratyevaṃ prājñaḥ satataparamānandasukhitaḥ || 542 ||*

At times he seems a fool, at times a sage, at times he has the splendour of a king. At times he wanders, at times he acts like a motionless python, at times his face is kindly. Sometimes honoured, sometimes insulted, sometimes unknown: so lives the man of realization, always happy in Supreme Bliss.

### 543

निर्धनोऽपि सदा तुष्टोऽप्यसहायो महाबलः ।  
नित्यतृप्तोऽप्यभुञ्जानोऽप्यसमः समदर्शनः ॥ ५४३ ॥

*nirdhano'pi sadā tuṣṭo'pyasahāyo mahābalaḥ |  
nityatṛpto'pyabhuñjāno'pyasamaḥ samadarśanaḥ || 543 ||*

He has no wealth, yet he is always content. He has no one to help him, yet he has great power. He does not enjoy sense-objects, yet he is eternally satisfied. He has no exemplar, yet he looks on everyone with an equal eye.

### 544

अपि कुर्वन्नकुर्वाणश्चाभोक्ता फलभोग्यपि ।  
शरीर्यप्यशरीर्येष परिच्छिन्नोऽपि सर्वगः ॥ ५४४ ॥

*api kurvannakurvāṇaścābhoktā phalabhogyapi |  
śarīryapyaśarīryeṣa paricchinno'pi sarvagaḥ || 544 ||*

He acts, yet he is inactive. He experiences the fruits of past actions, yet they do not touch him. He has a body, yet does not identify with it. He seems limited, yet he is omnipresent.

### 545

अशरीरं सदा सन्तमिमं ब्रह्मविदं क्वचित् ।  
प्रियाप्रिये न स्पृशतस्तथैव च शुभाशुभे ॥ ५४५ ॥

*aśarīraṃ sadā santamimaṃ brahmavidaṃ kvacit |  
priyāpriye na spṛśatastathaiva ca śubhāśubhe || 545 ||*

Neither pleasure nor pain, neither good nor evil, ever touches this knower of Brahman, who lives constantly without the idea of being a body.

### 546

स्थूलादिसंबन्धवतोऽभिमानिनः सुखं च दुःखं च शुभाशुभे च ।  
विध्वस्तबन्धस्य सदात्मनो मुनेः कुतः शुभं वाप्यशुभं फलं वा ॥ ५४६ ॥

*sthūlādisaṃbandhavato'bhimāninaḥ sukhaṃ ca duḥkhaṃ ca śubhāśubhe ca |  
vidhvastabandhasya sadātmano muneḥ kutaḥ śubhaṃ vāpyaśubhaṃ phalaṃ vā || 546 ||*

Pleasure and pain, good and evil, affect only the one who is connected with the gross body and the like and who identifies with them. How can good or evil, or what they bring, touch the sage who has identified with the Reality and so has shattered his bondage?

### 547

तमसा ग्रस्तवद्भानादग्रस्तोऽपि रविर्जनैः ।  
ग्रस्त इत्युच्यते भ्रान्त्यां ह्यज्ञात्वा वस्तुलक्षणम् ॥ ५४७ ॥

*tamasā grastavadbhānādagrasto'pi ravirjanaiḥ |  
grasta ityucyate bhrāntyāṃ hyajñātvā vastulakṣaṇam || 547 ||*

Rāhu appears to swallow the sun but does not actually do so. People who do not know the sun's real nature say, out of delusion, that it has been swallowed.

### 548

तद्वद्देहादिबन्धेभ्यो विमुक्तं ब्रह्मवित्तमम् ।  
पश्यन्ति देहिवन्मूढाः शरीराभासदर्शनात् ॥ ५४८ ॥

*tadvaddehādibandhebhyo vimuktaṃ brahmavittamam |  
paśyanti dehivanmūḍhāḥ śarīrābhāsadarśanāt || 548 ||*

In the same way, ignorant people regard the perfect knower of Brahman, who is wholly rid of the bonds of the body and the like, as having a body, because they see only an appearance of one.

### 549

अहिर्निर्ल्वयनीं वायं मुक्त्वा देहं तु तिष्ठति ।  
इतस्ततश्चाल्यमानो यत्किंचित्प्राणवायुना ॥ ५४९ ॥

*ahirnirlvayanīṃ vāyaṃ muktvā dehaṃ tu tiṣṭhati |  
itastataścālyamāno yatkiṃcitprāṇavāyunā || 549 ||*

In truth he rests, having cast off the body as a snake casts off its slough, and the force of the Prāṇa moves the body about wherever it will.

### 550

स्त्रोतसा नीयते दारु यथा निम्नोन्नतस्थलम् ।  
दैवेन नीयते देहो यथाकालोपभुक्तिषु ॥ ५५0 ॥

*strotasā nīyate dāru yathā nimnonnatasthalam |  
daivena nīyate deho yathākālopabhuktiṣu || 550 ||*

As a current carries a piece of wood now to high ground and now to low, so the momentum of past actions carries his body along to the varied experience of their fruits, as these arrive in due course.

### 551

प्रारब्धकर्मपरिकल्पितवासनाभिः संसारिवच्चरति भुक्तिषु मुक्तदेहः ।  
सिद्धः स्वयं वसति साक्षिवदत्र तूष्णीं चक्रस्य मूलमिव कल्पविकल्पशून्यः ॥ ५५१ ॥

*prārabdhakarmaparikalpitavāsanābhiḥ saṃsārivaccarati bhuktiṣu muktadehaḥ |  
siddhaḥ svayaṃ vasati sākṣivadatra tūṣṇīṃ cakrasya mūlamiva kalpavikalpaśūnyaḥ || 551 ||*

The man of realization, having lost the idea of being a body, moves among sense-enjoyments like someone subject to transmigration, through desires produced by Prārabdha work. But he himself stays unmoved in the body, like a witness, free of mental wavering, like the pivot of a potter's wheel.

### 552

नैवेन्द्रियाणि विषयेषु नियुङ्क्त एष नैवापयुङ्क्त उप्दर्शनलक्षणस्थः ।  
नैव क्रियाफलमपीषदवेक्षते स स्वानन्दसान्द्ररसपानसुमत्तचित्तः ॥ ५५२ ॥

*naivendriyāṇi viṣayeṣu niyuṅkta eṣa naivāpayuṅkta updarśanalakṣaṇasthaḥ |  
naiva kriyāphalamapīṣadavekṣate sa svānandasāndrarasapānasumattacittaḥ || 552 ||*

He neither sends his sense-organs toward their objects nor pulls them back, but remains like an unconcerned spectator. He has no regard whatever for the fruits of actions, for his mind is thoroughly intoxicated from drinking the pure elixir of the Bliss of Ātman.

### 553

लक्ष्यालक्ष्यगतिं त्यक्त्वा यस्तिष्ठेत्केवलात्मना ।  
शिव एव स्वयं साक्षादयं ब्रह्मविदुत्तमः ॥ ५५३ ॥

*lakṣyālakṣyagatiṃ tyaktvā yastiṣṭhetkevalātmanā |  
śiva eva svayaṃ sākṣādayaṃ brahmaviduttamaḥ || 553 ||*

One who gives up all such thoughts as "this is fit for meditation and this is not" and lives as the absolute Ātman is truly Śiva himself, and he is the finest of the Knowers of Brahman.

### 554

जीवन्नेव सदा मुक्तः कृतार्थो ब्रह्मवित्तमः ।  
उपाधिनाशाद्ब्रह्मैव सन् ब्रह्माप्येति निर्द्वयम् ॥ ५५४ ॥

*jīvanneva sadā muktaḥ kṛtārtho brahmavittamaḥ |  
upādhināśādbrahmaiva san brahmāpyeti nirdvayam || 554 ||*

When the adjuncts that had come upon him are destroyed, the perfect knower of Brahman merges into the One Brahman without a second, which he has been all along. He becomes wholly free while still living and reaches the fulfilment of his life.

### 555

शैलूषो वेषसद्भावा भावयोश्च यथा पुमान् ।  
तथैव ब्रह्मविच्छ्रेष्ठः सदा ब्रह्मैव नापरः ॥ ५५५ ॥

*śailūṣo veṣasadbhāvā bhāvayośca yathā pumān |  
tathaiva brahmavicchreṣṭhaḥ sadā brahmaiva nāparaḥ || 555 ||*

An actor remains a man whether or not he is wearing his costume. In the same way, the perfect knower of Brahman is always Brahman and nothing else.

### 556

यत्र क्वापि विशीर्णं सत्पर्णमिव तरोर्वपुः पततात् ।  
ब्रह्मीभूतस्य यतेः प्रागेव तच्चिदग्निना दग्धम् ॥ ५५६ ॥

*yatra kvāpi viśīrṇaṃ satparṇamiva tarorvapuḥ patatāt |  
brahmībhūtasya yateḥ prāgeva taccidagninā dagdham || 556 ||*

Let the body of the Sannyasin who has realized his identity with Brahman wither and drop anywhere, like a leaf from a tree. It matters little to him, for the fire of knowledge has already burnt it.

### 557

सदात्मनि ब्रह्मणि तिष्ठतो मुनेः पूर्णाद्वयानन्दमयात्मना सदा ।  
न देशकालाद्युचितप्रतीक्षा त्वङ्मांसविट्पिण्डविसर्जनाय ॥ ५५७ ॥

*sadātmani brahmaṇi tiṣṭhato muneḥ pūrṇādvayānandamayātmanā sadā |  
na deśakālādyucitapratīkṣā tvaṅmāṃsaviṭpiṇḍavisarjanāya || 557 ||*

The sage who lives always in the Reality, Brahman, as Infinite Bliss, the One without a second, does not rely on the usual considerations of place, time, and so on when giving up this heap of skin, flesh, and filth.

### 558

देहस्य मोक्षो नो मोक्षो न दण्डस्य कमण्डलोः ।  
अविद्याहृदयग्रन्थिमोक्षो मोक्षो यतस्ततः ॥ ५५८ ॥

*dehasya mokṣo no mokṣo na daṇḍasya kamaṇḍaloḥ |  
avidyāhṛdayagranthimokṣo mokṣo yatastataḥ || 558 ||*

Liberation is not giving up the body, nor giving up the staff and water-bowl. Liberation is the destruction of the heart's knot, which is Nescience.

### 559

कुल्यायामथ नद्यां वा शिवक्षेत्रेऽपि चत्वरे ।  
पर्णं पतति चेत्तेन तरोः किं नु शुभाशुभम् ॥ ५५९ ॥

*kulyāyāmatha nadyāṃ vā śivakṣetre'pi catvare |  
parṇaṃ patati cettena taroḥ kiṃ nu śubhāśubham || 559 ||*

Whether a leaf falls into a small stream, a river, a place consecrated by Shiva, or a crossroads, what good or harm does that do the tree?

### 560

पत्रस्य पुष्पस्य फलस्य नाशवद् देहेन्द्रियप्राणधियां विनाशः ।  
नैवात्मनः स्वस्य सदात्मकस्या नन्दाकृतेर्वृक्षवदस्ति चैषः ॥ ५६0 ॥

*patrasya puṣpasya phalasya nāśavad dehendriyaprāṇadhiyāṃ vināśaḥ |  
naivātmanaḥ svasya sadātmakasyā nandākṛtervṛkṣavadasti caiṣaḥ || 560 ||*

The destruction of the body, organs, Prāṇas, and Buddhi is like the loss of a leaf, flower, or fruit to a tree. It does not affect the Ātman, the Reality, the Embodiment of Bliss, which is one's true nature. That lives on, as the tree does.

### 561

प्रज्ञानघन इत्यात्मलक्षणं सत्यसूचकम् ।  
अनूद्यौपाधिकस्यैव कथयन्ति विनाशनम् ॥ ५६१ ॥

*prajñānaghana ityātmalakṣaṇaṃ satyasūcakam |  
anūdyaupādhikasyaiva kathayanti vināśanam || 561 ||*

The Śrutis set out the real nature of the Ātman in words such as "The Embodiment of Knowledge," which point to Its Reality, and they speak of the destruction of the apparent limitations only.

### 562

अविनाशी वा अरेऽयमात्मेति श्रुतिरात्मनः ।  
प्रब्रवीत्यविनाशित्वं विनश्यत्सु विकारिषु ॥ ५६२ ॥

*avināśī vā are'yamātmeti śrutirātmanaḥ |  
prabravītyavināśitvaṃ vinaśyatsu vikāriṣu || 562 ||*

The Śruti passage "Truly this Ātman is immortal, my dear" states the immortality of the Ātman among things that perish and undergo change.

### 563

पाषाणवृक्षतृणधान्यकडङ्कराद्या दग्धा भवन्ति हि मृदेव यथा तथैव ।  
देहेन्द्रियासुमन आदि समस्तदृश्यं ज्ञानाग्निदग्धमुपयाति परात्मभावम् ॥ ५६३ ॥

*pāṣāṇavṛkṣatṛṇadhānyakaḍaṅkarādyā dagdhā bhavanti hi mṛdeva yathā tathaiva |  
dehendriyāsumana ādi samastadṛśyaṃ jñānāgnidagdhamupayāti parātmabhāvam || 563 ||*

When stone, tree, grass, paddy, husk, and so on are burnt, they become nothing but earth (ashes). In the same way, the entire objective universe, including the body, organs, Prāṇas, Manas, and the rest, is reduced to the Supreme Self when burnt by the fire of realization.

### 564

विलक्षणं यथा ध्वान्तं लीयते भानुतेजसि ।  
तथैव सकलं दृश्यं ब्रह्मणि प्रविलीयते ॥ ५६४ ॥

*vilakṣaṇaṃ yathā dhvāntaṃ līyate bhānutejasi |  
tathaiva sakalaṃ dṛśyaṃ brahmaṇi pravilīyate || 564 ||*

Just as darkness, which is distinct from sunshine, vanishes in the sun's radiance, so the entire objective universe dissolves in Brahman.

### 565

घटे नष्टे यथा व्योम व्योमैव भवति स्फुटम् ।  
तथैवोपाधिविलये ब्रह्मैव ब्रह्मवित्स्वयम् ॥ ५६५ ॥

*ghaṭe naṣṭe yathā vyoma vyomaiva bhavati sphuṭam |  
tathaivopādhivilaye brahmaiva brahmavitsvayam || 565 ||*

When a jar breaks, the space it enclosed is plainly the limitless space. In the same way, when the apparent limitations are destroyed, the knower of Brahman truly becomes Brahman Itself.

### 566

क्षीरं क्षीरे यथा क्षिप्तं तैलं तैले जलं जले ।  
संयुक्तमेकतां याति तथात्मन्यात्मविन्मुनिः ॥ ५६६ ॥

*kṣīraṃ kṣīre yathā kṣiptaṃ tailaṃ taile jalaṃ jale |  
saṃyuktamekatāṃ yāti tathātmanyātmavinmuniḥ || 566 ||*

As milk poured into milk, oil into oil, or water into water blends and becomes one with it, so the sage who has realized the Ātman becomes one in the Ātman.

### 567

एवं विदेहकैवल्यं सन्मात्रत्वमखण्डितम् ।  
ब्रह्मभावं प्रपद्यैष यतिर्नावर्तते पुनः ॥ ५६७ ॥

*evaṃ videhakaivalyaṃ sanmātratvamakhaṇḍitam |  
brahmabhāvaṃ prapadyaiṣa yatirnāvartate punaḥ || 567 ||*

Having thus realized the utter isolation that comes from being without a body, and having become forever identified with the Absolute Reality, Brahman, the sage suffers transmigration no more.

### 568

सदात्मैकत्वविज्ञानदग्धाविद्यादिवर्ष्मणः ।  
अमुष्य ब्रह्मभूतत्वाद्ब्रह्मणः कुत उद्भवः ॥ ५६८ ॥

*sadātmaikatvavijñānadagdhāvidyādivarṣmaṇaḥ |  
amuṣya brahmabhūtatvādbrahmaṇaḥ kuta udbhavaḥ || 568 ||*

His bodies, made of Nescience and the rest, have been burnt by the realization that the Jīva and Brahman are one, so he becomes Brahman Itself. And how could Brahman ever be reborn?

### 569

मायाकॢप्तौ बन्धमोक्षौ न स्तः स्वात्मनि वस्तुतः ।  
यथा रज्जौ निष्क्रियायां सर्पाभासविनिर्गमौ ॥ ५६९ ॥

*māyākḷptau bandhamokṣau na staḥ svātmani vastutaḥ |  
yathā rajjau niṣkriyāyāṃ sarpābhāsavinirgamau || 569 ||*

Bondage and Liberation are conjured up by Māyā and do not truly exist in the Ātman, one's Reality. The snake's appearing and departing likewise do not belong to the rope, which never changes.

### 570

आवृतेः सदसत्त्वाभ्यां वक्तव्ये बन्धमोक्षणे ।  
नावृतिर्ब्रह्मणः काचिदन्याभावादनावृतम् यद्यस्त्यद्वैतहानिः स्याद्द्वैतं नो सहते श्रुतिः ॥ ५७0 ॥

*āvṛteḥ sadasattvābhyāṃ vaktavye bandhamokṣaṇe |  
nāvṛtirbrahmaṇaḥ kācidanyābhāvādanāvṛtam yadyastyadvaitahāniḥ syāddvaitaṃ no sahate śrutiḥ || 570 ||*

One might speak of bondage and Liberation where a covering veil is present or absent. But Brahman can have no covering veil, since It is always uncovered, there being no second thing besides Itself. If there were a veil, the non-duality of Brahman would be contradicted, and the Śrutis can never tolerate duality.

### 571

बन्धञ्च मोक्षञ्च मृषैव मूढा बुद्धेर्गुणं वस्तुनि कल्पयन्ति ।  
दृगावृतिं मेघकृतां यथा रवौ यतोऽद्वयासङ्गचिदेतदक्षरम् ॥ ५७१ ॥

*bandhañca mokṣañca mṛṣaiva mūḍhā buddherguṇaṃ vastuni kalpayanti |  
dṛgāvṛtiṃ meghakṛtāṃ yathā ravau yato'dvayāsaṅgacidetadakṣaram || 571 ||*

Bondage and Liberation are attributes of the Buddhi, which ignorant people falsely superimpose on the Reality, just as a cloud's covering of the eyes is attributed to the sun. For this Immutable Brahman is Knowledge Absolute, the One without a second, and unattached.

### 572

अस्तीति प्रत्ययो यश्च यश्च नास्तीति वस्तुनि ।  
बुद्धेरेव गुणावेतौ न तु नित्यस्य वस्तुनः ॥ ५७२ ॥

*astīti pratyayo yaśca yaśca nāstīti vastuni |  
buddhereva guṇāvetau na tu nityasya vastunaḥ || 572 ||*

The idea that bondage exists and the idea that it does not are, with regard to the Reality, both merely attributes of the Buddhi. They never belong to the Eternal Reality, Brahman.

### 573

अतस्तौ मायया कॢप्तौ बन्धमोक्षौ न चात्मनि ।  
निष्कले निष्क्रिये शान्ते निरवद्ये निरञ्जने अद्वितीये परे तत्त्वे व्योमवत्कल्पना कुतः ॥ ५७३ ॥

*atastau māyayā kḷptau bandhamokṣau na cātmani |  
niṣkale niṣkriye śānte niravadye nirañjane advitīye pare tattve vyomavatkalpanā kutaḥ || 573 ||*

So this bondage and Liberation are created by Māyā and are not in the Ātman. How could any idea of limitation apply to the Supreme Truth, which has no parts and no activity, and is calm, beyond reproach, stainless, and One without a second? It could no more apply there than to the infinite sky.

### 574

न निरोधो न चोत्पत्तिर्न बद्धो न च साधकः ।  
न मुमुक्षुर्न वै मुक्त इत्येषा परमार्थता ॥ ५७४ ॥

*na nirodho na cotpattirna baddho na ca sādhakaḥ |  
na mumukṣurna vai mukta ityeṣā paramārthatā || 574 ||*

There is no death and no birth, no bound soul and no struggling soul, no seeker of Liberation and no liberated one. This is the ultimate truth.

### 575

सकलनिगमचूडास्वान्तसिद्धान्तरूपं परमिदमतिगुह्यं दर्शितं ते मयाद्य ।  
अपगतकलिदोषं कामनिर्मुक्तबुद्धिं स्वसुतवदसकृत्त्वां भावयित्वा मुमुक्षुम् ॥ ५७५ ॥

*sakalanigamacūḍāsvāntasiddhāntarūpaṃ paramidamatiguhyaṃ darśitaṃ te mayādya |  
apagatakalidoṣaṃ kāmanirmuktabuddhiṃ svasutavadasakṛttvāṃ bhāvayitvā mumukṣum || 575 ||*

Today I have repeatedly revealed to you, as to my own son, this excellent and deep secret, the innermost meaning of all Vedanta, the crest of the Vedas. I did so regarding you as an aspirant to Liberation, cleansed of the taints of this Dark Age, with a mind free of desires.

### 576

<vt>Blessed Disciple Liberated</vt>  
इति श्रुत्वा गुरोर्वाक्यं प्रश्रयेण कृतानतिः ।  
स तेन समनुज्ञातो ययौ निर्मुक्तबन्धनः ॥ ५७६ ॥

*<vt>Blessed Disciple Liberated</vt>  
iti śrutvā gurorvākyaṃ praśrayeṇa kṛtānatiḥ |  
sa tena samanujñāto yayau nirmuktabandhanaḥ || 576 ||*

On hearing these words of the Guru, the disciple bowed down before him in reverence and, with his leave, went on his way, freed from bondage.

### 577

गुरुरेव सदानन्दसिन्धौ निर्मग्नमानसः ।  
पावयन्वसुधां सर्वाण्विचचार निरन्तरः ॥ ५७७ ॥

*gurureva sadānandasindhau nirmagnamānasaḥ |  
pāvayanvasudhāṃ sarvāṇvicacāra nirantaraḥ || 577 ||*

And the Guru, his mind immersed in the ocean of Existence and Bliss Absolute, travelled on, truly purifying the whole world, with every idea of difference banished from his mind.

### 578

इत्याचार्यस्य शिष्यस्य संवादेनात्मलक्षणम् ।  
निरूपितं मुमुक्षूणां सुखबोधोपपत्तये ॥ ५७८ ॥

*ityācāryasya śiṣyasya saṃvādenātmalakṣaṇam |  
nirūpitaṃ mumukṣūṇāṃ sukhabodhopapattaye || 578 ||*

In this way, through a dialogue between Teacher and disciple, the nature of the Ātman has been established, so that seekers of Liberation may grasp it easily.

### 579

<vt>The Glory of the Textbook</vt>  
हितमिदमुपदेशमाद्रियन्तां विहितनिरस्तसमस्तचित्तदोषाः ।  
भवसुखविरताः प्रशान्तचित्ताः श्रुतिरसिका यतयो मुमुक्षवो ये ॥ ५७९ ॥

*<vt>The Glory of the Textbook</vt>  
hitamidamupadeśamādriyantāṃ vihitanirastasamastacittadoṣāḥ |  
bhavasukhaviratāḥ praśāntacittāḥ śrutirasikā yatayo mumukṣavo ye || 579 ||*

May those Sannyasins who seek Liberation, who have cleansed their minds of every taint by following the prescribed methods, who turn away from worldly pleasures, who are serene of mind and delight in the Śruti, value this beneficial teaching!

### 580

संसाराध्वनि तापभानुकिरणप्रोद्भूतदाहव्यथा खिन्नानां जलकाङ्क्षया मरुभुवि भ्रान्त्या परिभ्राम्यताम् ।  
अत्यासन्नसुधाम्बुधिं सुखकरं ब्रह्माद्वयं दर्शयत्य् एषा शंकरभारती विजयते निर्वाणसंदायिनी ॥ ५८0 ॥

*saṃsārādhvani tāpabhānukiraṇaprodbhūtadāhavyathā khinnānāṃ jalakāṅkṣayā marubhuvi bhrāntyā paribhrāmyatām |  
atyāsannasudhāmbudhiṃ sukhakaraṃ brahmādvayaṃ darśayaty eṣā śaṃkarabhāratī vijayate nirvāṇasaṃdāyinī || 580 ||*

There are those who, in the course of worldly life, are afflicted by the burning pain of the scorching sunshine of threefold misery, and who wander deluded through a desert in search of water. For them here is Shankara's triumphant message, showing that the soothing ocean of nectar, Brahman, the One without a second, is close at hand, to lead them on to Liberation.
